# Project-centric Fugaro: implementation plan

**Goal:** make the **project**, not the repository, the unit of configuration, policy, credentials and setup — the reconciled design [docs/design/project-centric.md](../design/project-centric.md) (§11 phases 0 to 6), implemented so that **each phase merges alone, leaves `main` green and the system working**, and adds a repository to a project with no Terraform and no confirmations. No code in this plan has been written or run; this is a design-to-code plan, the same status `docs/plans/2026-10-14-*` had before its Task 1 started. **This PR (the one that adds this plan) is docs-only: no Go, Terraform or product-behavior file under this plan's "Files" lists is touched by it.** Unlike the price-catalog and project-secrets plans it edits **nothing else at all** — not even `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`: the check numbers this plan reserves (48 to 56) are *named* here and their rows and sections are written by each phase's own check task when that phase ships, because a reservation without a written check is exactly the "Reserved number" row `main` still carries for Check 42 and the design says nothing about those numbers. Read §"Check numbers this plan takes" for why nothing below 48 is free. Tasks 0.1 onward are future work, gated on the owner approving the design.

**Revision 2026-10-15 — the backing location may be a folder inside an existing repository.** Docs only, like the plan itself, and it changes three things and nothing else. **(1)** Phase 6 (and the design's §8, §11 phase 6 and §12) now support a backing location of **three settings** — `repo`, `path` (default `fugaro-project/`) and `branch` (default `fugaro-project`, never the monorepo's `main` unless the owner writes it) — so a **monorepo can hold the project's record in a folder** instead of needing a dedicated repository; the export writes and stages **only** that folder, pushes by **fetching and committing on the fetched tip and then pushing plainly (never a force)**, and commits **quietly** (a `[skip ci]` marker, the fixed `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>` identity, a sorted tree): the owner's requirement of 2026-10-15, quoted in the design's §8, implemented by **Task 6.2** and pinned by **P11**. **(2)** A new owner decision, **D14**, is numbered and carries the recommendation (a dedicated branch, pushed directly; a PR mode is the documented opt-in for a monorepo that reviews every branch) — it lives in the design's §12, since the D-series is the design's. **(3)** A new owner-run check, **Check 61** (**Task 6.5**): a folder export into a **scratch repository that also carries unrelated files**, asserting they are untouched and that a moved branch is rebased onto or refused, never forced. **Its reservation is written into `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`'s register by this revision** — the one deliberate exception to this plan's "each check's row is written with its phase, and this plan edits no other file" rule, and it is a *reservation only*: **no row and no section**, which Task 6.5 writes when phase 6 ships. **No code, Terraform, workflow or product-behavior file is touched by this revision**, and its claim about the code (`internal/gitops`' identity, `Push`, `checkRunBranch`, `internal/projectpolicy`'s keys) was read on `origin/next` at `8355645`.

**Revision 2026-10-15 (fix round) — the five things the first revision left implicit, after the review of PR #405.** Docs only again, and it changes no file outside the three the first revision touched. **(1) A wrong claim is corrected:** **PR #401 (the desktop console, Check 59) merged to `main` as `411fc18` on 2026-10-15**, so the plan and the checklist's register no longer call it open (re-read on `origin/main`; `main`'s checklist still does not carry the number, because that plan's Task 4.3 writes it). **(2) The push credential is one thing in both documents:** the export authenticates with **the operator's own local git credentials** — never the runner's git secret, which no person can read and which a manual export therefore cannot use (design §8's concurrency paragraph and Task 6.2 step 2 said different things; both now say this). **(3) D14's PR mode has one wording everywhere** — *a dedicated branch by default; the PR mode is a documented opt-in, not built in the first slice* — and `path` has **one grammar** (design §8's table, with `TestBackingPathGrammar` carrying one row per case, and its filesystem half as three more tests) instead of a rule that contradicted its own exception. **(4) The conflict rule gets a detector**, which is the correction that matters: the export records the **tree hash it last wrote** in a marker inside the folder and **refuses** when the folder no longer matches it, with `--adopt` and `--force-regenerate` as the only two overrides — without it, the rule and its test had nothing to observe. The same step specifies the **branch bootstrap** (from the default branch's tip, never orphan), the **clone cost** (shallow, blobless, sparse) and the three **filesystem-escape** tests. **(5) Check 61 is redesigned so it can actually be run** — its scratch repository is the owner's own (a local `file://` bare repository, or one under the owner's personal account), **never the `edgeappinc` workspace the sandbox lives in**, each step asserts **one** outcome rather than two, and Task 6.5 now **runs `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** as well as its own test, repairing only its own section.

**Revision 2026-10-15 (fix round 2) — the belt-and-braces round: the branch is docs only again, it carries `next`, and the folder check can be run.** **(1) The code an earlier revision of this branch added was reverted, so this PR is docs only as it was asked to be.** Commit `e12b19c7` had changed `internal/cli/project_cmd.go` and `internal/projectpolicy` (the `routing` key a publish dropped, the `repos rm` superset sentence and their tests); the reviewer asked for it to be reverted rather than carried under a docs PR, and it is (`38c4ddf`, an ordinary revert commit — no history rewritten, nothing force-pushed, and the reverted head is preserved on the origin branch `backup-405-code` for the change to be reopened from): **a code change would ship an unreviewed behaviour change under a docs PR's description, and those two fixes belong against the code they fix.** **(2) The branch is merged with `origin/next` (PR #386, its Task 3.0 and the D5 ruling), and the conflict in this file is resolved by keeping both sides**: next's **nine** design amendments (the eighth is D3 and the `belong` note; the ninth is D5, "Bitbucket is not first-class in phase 3") and this branch's **P11** and its paragraph saying the backing-location change is written into the design by this revision rather than deferred to Task 0.5. **(3) The detector's hash recipe was wrong and is fixed everywhere it is stated** — the marker excludes itself, while `git rev-parse <tip>:<path>` names the committed folder *with* the marker in it, so the two could never be equal and **every export would have refused, forever, printing "somebody edited the folder" about its own last write.** Design §8 now states the recipe once, and Task 6.2's step 4, its test list and Check 61's step 6 use it: the folder's entries with the marker's line dropped, built into a tree object — `git ls-tree <tip>:<path> | sed -e '/[[:space:]]\.fugaro-export\.json$/d' | git mktree` — with the export's own side the same object built the other way (`git rev-parse "$(git write-tree):<path>"` taken **before** the marker is in the index). `TestExportAcceptsAFolderItWroteItself` is the test that fails if the recipe regresses: with the old one, a second export into an unchanged folder refuses. **(4) Check 61's four step-level corrections are made, so the steps can be run**: the push target has its own guard line (`${R:?}`, `${path:?}`, `${branch:?}` and a hard stop that refuses a target in the `edgeappinc` workspace, because Check 43's hard stops pin the checkout and not the target it writes to); the identity assertion reads `<default-branch>..fugaro-project` rather than the whole branch, since the branch is bootstrapped from the default branch's tip and that tip carries the owner's own seed commit; the "no force" assertion reads a **recorded** push command (`GIT_TRACE=1`, with a recording `git` shim as the fallback) instead of the owner's memory; and step 1 **records the policy**, which step 10 restores — the earlier text restored a recording that nothing had made. **(5) D14 is answered, and nowhere is it "open":** the owner ruled on 2026-10-15 for a **dedicated branch by default — `fugaro-project`, writing only under the `fugaro-project/` folder — never the monorepo's `main` by default; the PR mode is a documented opt-in, not built in the first slice.** The design's §8 paragraph, its §12 intro and D14's row, this plan's spec line, P11, the phase-6 revision paragraph and Task 6.2 step 7 all now say ruled-and-answered, and D14's row records the alternatives the owner did not take. **(6) The release-placement lines are untouched**: this revision changes nothing about which release a phase ships in — **PR #415 amends those lines to the owner's split (0.8.0 after phases 1 and 2, phases 3 to 6 in 0.9.0)** — and this revision does not touch them at all, so it cannot collide with that one's edit there (the R4 line at §"Releases", the `policySince` paragraph in phase 1, and the design-amendment 8 tail are all left exactly as they were).

**Spec:** [docs/design/project-centric.md](../design/project-centric.md) (approved 2026-10-14; the owner's decisions D1a to D12 are in its §12, with **D13** ruled 2026-10-15 (applied by Task 0.5) and **D14** **answered by the owner on 2026-10-15** — a **dedicated branch by default** (the branch `fugaro-project`, writing only under the `fugaro-project/` folder), **never the monorepo's `main` by default**, with the **PR mode a documented opt-in, not built in the first slice** — which phases 6 and this plan's P11 implement as ruled rather than as recommended; its §12a lists the fourteen review items this plan must carry, and its §13 lists what was UNVERIFIED when it was written) and the owner's verbatim draft [project-centric-spec-source.md](../design/project-centric-spec-source.md). The design's decisions are settled; this plan's own decisions continue as **P1 onward**, each independently vetoable before its phase starts.

**Architecture — what the seven phases build, in one paragraph.** Phase 0 is three small fixes with no shared surface: the budget refusals stop naming flags that do not exist, `anthropic-api-key` stops being mandatory, and the global per-run cap becomes the fallback the RTDB rules already deny for. Phase 1 moves the ceiling out of each job's environment into **one RTDB policy object** (`config/policy`), read by the runner at start, unreadable-by-wrong-slug by rule, and fail-closed: "project-centric mode" is *the rules-version marker `config/limits/policyRules` being at least `RulesVersion` **and** `config/policy/shared` existing* (R3; P3), so an installation that has not published one behaves exactly as today. Phase 1 also ships the enrollment allow-list (`allow_repos`) that a shared job needs from its first day, and the CLI that publishes and shows the policy. Phase 1b decouples the model from the key route with the operator-written `routing.claude` switch and the canonical-to-route ID map, and ships only after #359's phase 2 and the price catalog are merged. Phase 2 makes project scope the default for `fugaro secrets set` (D2, which reverses #343's default-safe rule). Phase 3 replaces the per-(repository, workflow, account) job with **one job and one job service account per (profile, provider, account)**, and with it the runner binds its slug to the repository it clones, admits only repositories its profile allows, and takes its remote from `task.json` and the policy instead of `FUGARO_REMOTE`. Phase 4 turns enrollment into data (`project sync`, `project repos ls\|add\|rm`), retires a repository's whole Terraform root, retires `init --repo` for clone repositories, and drops baked workflows (D3). Phase 5 makes Claude OAuth tokens person-owned and shared by reference, with the CLI (not the skill) doing the failover. Phase 6 writes the project's policy, config, inventory and spend into a backing Git repository, secrets excluded. Nothing in phase *n* is required by phase *n-1*'s merge: each phase is a working system, and the last section of this plan lists every cross-task dependency so a reviewer can check that claim.

**Releases (R4, as amended by the owner's release ruling of 2026-10-15 — R6).** The next release on `main` is **0.7.5** (it carries the config-transitions work, R2, and the rename of `cloneSince` from `0.7.1` to `0.7.5`, #370 — already merged on `main`). **The whole redesign still lands on the `next` branch**, and **phase 0 still lands on `main`** (its three fixes are independent and D6's rules deploy is the owner's own step there); what the 2026-10-15 ruling changes is **where the release is cut — 0.8.0 is cut after phases 1 and 2**. So **0.8.0 carries phase 1** (policy as data), **phase 1b** (the `routing.claude` switch) and **phase 2** (project-scope keys by default), plus the three 0.8.0 items that are not this plan's: the **senior-reviewer opt-in** (`docs/plans/2026-10-14-openrouter-senior-reviewer.md` — its operator-config slice and its project-layer slice are merged on `main`, and nothing in that plan names a release), **artifact runs** (its design and its plan are on `main` and already say 0.8.0) and **headroom-compression** (`docs/plans/2026-10-12-headroom-compression.md`, whose design's §5 and whose `compressionSince = "0.8.0"` still target 0.8.0 — the ruling does not move it, and it is not a project-centric phase). **Phases 3, 4, 5 and 6 move to 0.9.0**, and the **Docker-capable compute backend moves with them**: the ruling places `docs/design/compute-backend.md`'s Cloud Batch work (generic-tool Task 28) in 0.9.0 too, and this commit retitles that design's §6.2 from "the 0.8.0 phases" to "the 0.9.0 phases" — reading the rest of that document for release statements found no other one in it beyond its own "Docker stays out" message, so nothing else there changes. **The desktop console is not tied to this split**: the owner ruled it an artifact of its own, published from the CLI's own `vX.Y.Z` tag (that design's D11 — `docs/design/desktop-console.md`, on `main`), so neither half of the split promises or blocks it. Every task below that names a release — the `cloneSince` value, the `policySince` launch gate (finding 16), the `--retire` spelling (finding 18) — is written against that: 0.7.5 is the last release on `main` before the model, **0.8.0 is the release of the model's first half** (phases 1, 1b and 2 — and with phase 1 the policy model itself, which is why `policySince` stays `"0.8.0"`), and **0.9.0 is the release of the rest** (phases 3 to 6 and the compute backend). **The tasks whose release the ruling moves, named so nobody has to re-derive them: Task 3.6** (`cloneShipped`'s flip after Check 38), **Task 3b.1** (the budget token's move out of `runs/`), **Tasks 3.0 and 3.7** with **Checks 52 and 53**, **Task 4.5** with **Check 54**, **Task 5.5** with **Check 55** and **Task 6.4** with **Check 56**. **No Check number changes**: 48 to 56 stay this plan's, and 57 stays the compute backend's. (Checked, 2026-10-15, against `origin/next`: no document on this branch besides this plan and `docs/design/compute-backend.md` places a phase-3-to-6 item in 0.8.0. `docs/release.md`'s `policySince` bullet names 0.8.0 correctly, because the constant is phase 1's; `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`'s Check 52 row and section name no release at all; and the desktop console's own plan, the artifact-runs plan and the senior-reviewer plan are on `main`, not here.)

**TODO(0.8.0 Highlights).** There is no `docs/releases/v0.8.0.md` draft on this branch (the newest release note here is `docs/releases/v0.7.5.md`,
for the config-transitions work that lands on `main`), so the two operator-facing facts this plan's **0.8.0 half** ships are recorded here until one exists, and the file must not be written without them: **(a)** a budget-off installation that has a database URL now depends on Firebase and Cloud Storage at launch and at run start, and fails closed — the line Task 1.4 added to `docs/gcp-setup.md` beside the rollback (L4 of the security review of PR #379), which belongs under `### For operators`; **(b)** `init --repo` re-applied from a local config with no `budget.rtdb_url` removes the backend from jobs that have it, and the apply says so (L3), which belongs beside it as the rollout order: `fugaro init --firebase` first, `init --repo` after. **Both are phase 1's** (Tasks 1.3 to 1.5), so both stay the 0.8.0 notes under the ruling (R6) and **nothing from phases 3 to 6 belongs in them any more**; Task 3.6 and Task 4.6/6.3's release sweeps read the 0.9.0 line below instead. A Highlights file written without these two is missing 0.8.0's only operator-visible breaking change.

**TODO(0.9.0 Highlights).** Phases 3 to 6 move to 0.9.0 (R6), and there is no `docs/releases/v0.9.0.md` draft either, so their operator-facing facts are recorded here in the same way. A 0.9.0 Highlights written without them is missing that release's breaking changes; when one is written it must carry at least: **(a)** the budget token's exposure under phase 3's broadened `runs/` grant — within a profile a run can read and take another *queued* run's budget token, so **repositories that must not share a budget identity use separate profiles** (D13's ruling of 2026-10-15, in the words Task 3.2 writes into SECURITY.md); **(b)** `init --repo` stops deploying a Terraform root for a clone repository and baked workflows are refused for new onboarding, with the owner's migration order and the `belong` carve-out (Tasks 4.3, 4.4 — never `belong`); **(c)** Claude OAuth accounts become person-owned and shared by reference, with failover moved out of the skill and into `fugaro run` (phase 5); and **(d)** `fugaro project export` writes the project's policy, config, inventory and spend into a backing Git repository, secrets excluded and people omitted by default (phase 6). The **compute backend** lands in the same release and its own design's task outline (`docs/design/compute-backend.md` §8, task 10) owns its release bullet, so this line names it rather than duplicating it.

**The budget token's seal, and which release it ships in (an owner decision, not a ruling).** Phase 3b's design recommends the seal (D19, `docs/design/budget-token-channel.md` §7.1) and the owner answered "the seal ships first, on its own" on 2026-10-15, but **no release was named**: `budget-token-channel.md` said nothing about one, and neither did this plan. Stated here so the two agree, and **it stays an explicit decision the owner makes rather than a ruling of the split**: **the seal ships with phase 3, in 0.9.0** — **unless the owner pulls Slice A earlier.** *Slice A* is **the runner's side** — `takeBudgetToken` unsealing before `checkNames` and `BackendFromEnv`'s strict parse of the marker's value — and it is **a runner-first change that is safe to release on its own**, because the envelope's version byte keeps a plaintext object from a launcher that does not seal yet readable and no grant changes with it. The **launcher-seals slice** (the mint writing a sealed object and the marker carrying the key) and the **`tokens/` move with its prefix condition** are **not** earlier than 0.9.0: the move lands together with the grant that reads it, Task 3.2's, exactly as D18 and D19 order it. So the three slices are: Slice A (the runner's, earlier only if the owner says so), the launcher-seals slice, and the move — the last two with phase 3 in 0.9.0.

**Follow-ups (found by review, not this PR's to fix).** **`routing`'s decode drops a misspelt key.** `internal/projectpolicy/policy.go`'s `routing` branch (`:321`) is a plain `json.Unmarshal` into `Routing`, and `validRouting` (`:541`) checks only `routing.claude.via`: a key spelt wrong *inside* `routing` — `routimg`, `claud`, `viaa` — is silently dropped, so the node means what "direct" means (or leaves a field unset) where the operator wrote a route. That is the fail-open shape finding 16 describes for the marker, and it differs from the node's **top-level** keys, which Decode lists in `UnknownKeys`, and from `providers`' inner keys, which are refused. It is **pre-existing on `next` and outside this PR**, which changes documentation only, and it is phase 1b's own surface — so it belongs to that phase's review and task, not to this one.

**The Release column is the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15 (R6), and it is the whole of the split: 0.8.0 is cut after phases 1 and 2** — phases 1, 1b and 2 ship together as 0.8.0, phases 3 to 6 ship together as 0.9.0 with the compute backend, and phase 0 is not either release's: it lands on `main` in 0.7.5's line. Phases 3 to 6 still run **on `next` and in this order**, and each still merges alone; only the tag they are released under moves.

**Nothing in this table depends on a later row.** Phase 1b may be deferred indefinitely (its two prerequisites are other PRs); phases 2, 3, 4 and 5 do not read the routing switch; phase 6 reads only the policy and the bucket. The last section lists the per-task dependencies.

## Check numbers this plan takes (48 to 56, and 61)

`docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` on `main` (as merged by #353 and #354) says *"Check 45 is the next free number"* (line 242). The **open PRs change that**, so this plan takes nothing below 48 and says whose each number is. **The 2026-10-15 revision adds 61** — phase 6's own second check, the folder export's fence (Task 6.5) — because the folder case is an assertion Check 56 does not make, and the numbers between 56 and it are held by others; the rows for 57 to 61 were read on 2026-10-15 from the reservations themselves (the checklist's register on `origin/main` at `fd06635`, and the PRs that carry the open claims), not inferred from the gaps:

**Checked, not assumed (R5):** `main`'s checklist ends at 47; #359's patch claims 45 and 46; #375 claims 57; and **#360, #361, #362, #365, #369, #371, #372 and #373 add no number at all** — so **48 to 56 are free and stay this plan's**. **Re-checked on 2026-10-15 for this revision, which adds 61**: 58 is #393's (**merged**, `fd06635`), 59 is #401's (**merged to `main` as `411fc18`**, re-read for the fix round) and 60 is #399's (open, based on `next`), so **61 is the first number past every reservation that is free for this plan to take** — the register on `origin/main` still ends at 57 and therefore does not name 58 to 60, and reading the gaps alone would have been wrong in both directions (a gap is not a free number, and a reserved one is not free because its row is unwritten). **Three in-flight collisions the plan states so an implementer meets them here and not at the rebase** (R2-7, finding 25): **#365 also edits `internal/infra/spec.go`** (and `internal/gcpfake`), so Tasks 0.2, 1.4 and 2.2 overlap with it — **#365 lands first** (it ships on `main` in 0.7.5, R2), and the three tasks rebase onto its `spec.go` rather than the other way round; **#359's phase 1 documents the placeholder `anthropic-api-key`** that Task 0.2 removes, so whichever merges second updates that paragraph (`docs/multi-model.md` and #359's own text), and the rule is that **Task 0.2's behaviour wins** — a placeholder key is not needed — while #359's wording is corrected; and **#362 is a checklist PR that claims no number** (finding 25's correction of the earlier "43 by #362"). **#374** (the compute-instance backend's own plan, generic-tool Task 28) is separate for the same reason 57 is: one line here so a reader does not confuse its "project job" with phase 3's.

**Each check's row and section is written by the phase's own task, not by this PR** — that is the one place this plan differs from its predecessors' habit of reserving a row up front. The reason is #359: its numbers live only in an unmerged PR, and a reservation this plan wrote into the checklist would have to be amended by whichever of the two merges second. Taking 48 as the next free number and writing each row with its phase keeps one writer per row. **The 2026-10-15 revision's 61 is the one exception, and a deliberate one:** its *reservation* is written into the register now (one sentence in `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`, **no row and no section**), because it is a number this revision takes and the register is the single record both sides read; the row and the section stay Task 6.5's, and its own docs test is `TestChecklistHasCheck61`. **All ten are owner-run, on installation `fugaro` and the sandbox repository only, never the `belong` project** (which holds `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb`: no agent touches it, ever; migrating it is the owner's own work after the model is proven on `fugaro`, D2 and D3). Every check is written with FACT lines, a restore step **and Check 43's full guard, spelled once in Task 0.4 step 1 and required verbatim in every check 49 to 56 and 61** (`docs/gcp-live-checklist.md:1158-1192` on `main`: the three hard stops, `--project fugaro` on every `fugaro` command and `--project "${P:?}"` on every `gcloud` command, re-run from a fresh shell before each step — R2-6).

## Citation drift: where the design's `file:line` are now

The design's citations are `file:line` on `main` at `b744aeca`, and it warns they drift. **Re-verified one by one for this plan, on `fd86542`** — with **two rows that postdate it and say so in their Note**: `cloneSince`'s rename to `0.7.5` (#370) and Check 47's heading (#363) are verified on `main` at `2b6f55b`, the tip when this plan was finished, because on `fd86542` the constant is still `0.7.1` and Check 47 does not exist (the file ends at 1683 lines there). Every task below cites the *current* location; this table is what a later reader uses to read the design's prose against the code. Two systematic differences first:

- **The design quotes bare filenames** (`spec.go`, `names.go`, `evaluate.go`, `run-gen.go`, `task.go`, `readiness.go`). Their packages are not always the ones a reader guesses: the job spec builder is **`internal/infra/spec.go`**, not `internal/cli/spec.go`; the four wrong flag names are in **`internal/budget/{evaluate,session}.go`**, not `internal/cli/`; `task.json`'s type is **`internal/task/task.go`**; the readiness gate is **`internal/infra/readiness.go`**; the per-repository secret ID is **`internal/backend/gcp/names.go`**. Terraform paths resolve under **`deploy/terraform/gcp/`** (`modules/repo/...`, `modules/workflow/...`, `modules/installation/...`, `modules/firebase/...`).
- **`internal/cli/run-gen.go` does not exist** on this branch and neither does a `run.jobs.runWithOverrides` merge function by that name; the override is the request body `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:106-115` builds, and the API's merge behaviour is the design's citation of `run-gen.go:769-778` (generated client code, not in this repository). **This is the one citation the plan could not re-verify from the repository** and is why the D10 live check (Task 3.0 step 3) is a *probe*: what the API does with a `ContainerOverride` that repeats a job's own env var is read from the API's behaviour, not from a file here.
