**If the merged code differs when a task starts, that task's implementer adapts its edits to the then-current file, not the other way round** (the rule `docs/plans/2026-10-08-upgrade.md` and `docs/plans/2026-10-13-project-secrets.md` use). A task that finds a cited symbol gone stops and reports rather than inventing a replacement.

## Decisions (veto any before its phase starts)

- **P1. The D6 rules change ships alone, in phase 0, and the rules, the code, the tests and the m9 document change in one commit.** Making the global per-run cap the fallback is a **fail-open-direction change to security rules** (design §2.5): shipped as one commit it is reviewable as one thing, and shipped as two it leaves a window in which the code enforces a fallback the rules deny (every write refused) or the rules allow one the code does not compute. *Veto alternative:* fold it into phase 1's policy work — rejected: it would make phase 1's rules deploy carry two unrelated changes, and the design's own phase 0 names it.
- **P2. The policy object is one RTDB subtree, `config/policy`, with two readable children — `shared` and `repos/$slug` — and one node outside it, `config/admin`, which no run token may read.** The types, the paths and the version live in one new package, `internal/projectpolicy`; `internal/budget` keeps its own `Path*` helpers (`internal/budget/model.go:44-80`) and neither package imports the other. `internal/projectpolicy` imports `internal/rtdb` and `internal/task` only (Task 1.1's own header says the same — finding 21: the two sentences disagreed), and never `internal/budget`, `internal/runner` or `internal/cli`. The rule is per child because **RTDB read grants cascade to children** (design §5): a `TOKEN` read on `config/policy` itself would make every child readable by every run token, which is the narrowing the design exists to keep. **`config/admin`, not `config/policy/admin`**: the design's §5 wording is "an admin-only `config/admin`", and 12a item 7 adds the reason not to put anything secret-adjacent there — launchers hold the RTDB *viewer* role and IAM reads bypass the rules, so launchers read it. *Veto alternative:* one flat `config/policy` node readable by run tokens — rejected outright: a prompt-injected agent can take the run token through `/proc` and would then read the project's whole policy, including who owns which account.
- **P3. "Project-centric mode" is the rules-version marker `config/limits/policyRules` being at least `RulesVersion` **and** `config/policy/shared` existing (owner ruling R3, 2026-10-15), and an unreadable-or-unparseable existing node halts the run.** The marker is the first half for a reason the round-2 review found (R2-4): a policy published without the rules deployed cannot exist — `policy publish` refuses without the marker (Task 1.6) — and the marker is readable under both the pre-phase-1 and the phase-1 rules, so "marker absent" is provably "this installation's rules predate the model", never "a policy is hiding behind a denied read". Not a flag in the job env (a launcher can clear it, D10), not a version probe on the parent (denied under per-child rules). **The pointer to the node is not protected either** (finding 27): a launcher that clears `FUGARO_RTDB_URL`, or points it at another database, sends the run down the env-ceiling path — D10-accepted, and SECURITY.md says so in Task 1.5 step 5, so no reader of P3 should conclude the mode cannot be turned off by a launcher. Absent ⇒ today's behaviour exactly (the env ceiling, the per-repository path): an installation that has not published a policy cannot be changed by this phase, which is what makes phase 1 merge alone. **The halt applies to a run that has the budget credential** (round-2 review): without `FUGARO_RTDB_URL`/`FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` there is no RTDB client and the marker cannot be read at all. **On a budget-off job** that case is the mode-off path and the launcher's readiness gate is the refusal (Task 1.3 step 1, Task 1.4 step 2). **On a budget-on job the same absence is a refusal, not the mode-off path** (B1): the run stops on the shipped messages at `internal/runner/budget_session.go:174-175` (no API key) and `:186-187` (`token.ErrNoToken`), and the launcher's gate is what keeps such a job from being deployed or launched in the first place. *Veto alternative:* a `FUGARO_POLICY_URL`/`FUGARO_POLICY=1` env flag — rejected: it is exactly the override surface D10 documents, and it would let one launcher override turn the project policy off for one run.
- **P4. `allow_repos` is seeded once, from the local config's `repos:` map, and a later publish does not silently re-add a name an operator removed (12a item 4).** `project policy publish` seeds **only** when the node does not exist yet, or when the operator passes `--seed-repos`; the seeded source is **the operator's own `repos:` map and the shared config's repository list** — never the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes, because **a slug cannot be turned back into a name** (finding 19): it is a readable prefix plus a truncated hash of `(provider, canonical repo)` (`internal/task/task.go:183-196`), and `task.json` carries no provider (`internal/task/task.go:45-79`). Recovering a canonical name from a prefix means reading that slug's `task.json` repository and testing `task.Slug(kind, repo)` against the slug for each known provider kind — which is exactly what Task 4.1's *seen* list does, under a different question ("what has run", not "what to enroll"). Seeding from it would enroll a repository the operator never named, which is the thing this decision exists to prevent. A name removed from the allow-list stays removed until an operator adds it, and `project show` prints the difference between the allow-list and the seen repositories. *Veto alternative:* seed on every publish — rejected: it makes `fugaro project repos rm` impossible to keep, which is a security-relevant action (it is how a repository is un-enrolled).
- **P5. `allow_repos` patterns are canonical `provider/owner/name` globs (`github/acme/*`, `bitbucket/edgeappinc/service-*`, `*/acme/*`), matched against `projectpolicy.Canonical(provider, repo)` — the provider kind plus `task.CanonicalRepo`'s lower-cased `owner/name` (`internal/task/task.go:127-138`), never `task.Slug` (12a item 5).** **The distinction matters and is easy to get wrong**: `task.Slug(provider, repo)` is the *storage* identity, a readable prefix plus a truncated SHA-256 of exactly that (provider, canonical name) pair (`internal/task/task.go:179-196`) — matching globs against it would compare patterns to `acme-app-3f9c…`, which matches nothing and carries no provider segment at all. The two agree because they hash the same pair: the allow-list is checked against the canonical string, and the slug binding of Task 1.5 asserts `slug == task.Slug(provider, spec.Repo)`, so a policy that enrolls `github/acme/app` admits exactly the runs whose slug is that repository's. The pattern's provider segment is required (a bare `acme/*` is refused with a message naming the canonical form) so a GitHub and a Bitbucket repository of the same name cannot be enrolled by one glob. Matching lives in one function, `projectpolicy.MatchRepo(pattern, canonical string) bool`, used by the runner's admission check and by the CLI's advisory check, so the two cannot disagree; `Canonical` itself is the single place the two segments are joined, so no caller can build a third spelling. *Veto alternative:* match the bare `owner/name` — rejected: `dimipaun/fugarosandbox` and `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` already exist in the owner's installations (design §4.4), so a bare-name allow-list would enroll the wrong one.
- **P6. Phase 1 enforces `allow_repos` only; profile admission ships in phase 3, where profiles arrive (12a item 8).** The runner's phase-1 check is therefore "the slug matches the allow-list and equals `task.Slug(provider, spec.Repo)`", with the provider **derived from the slug and `task.json`'s repository** (`providerForSlug`, Task 1.5 step 2 — the round-1 text said "the job's own provider", which phase 1 does not have: a phase-1 job carries none, R2-12) — the binding's *first half*, which is enforceable before shared jobs exist and is what a shared job will need on its first day. Nothing in phase 1 creates a profile.
- **P7. Phase 1b is separately shippable and separately deferrable; nothing in phases 2 to 6 reads the routing switch.** Its gate is real: #359's phase 2 (the `anthropic/<id>` claim, `IsAlias(model, claimed)`) and the price catalog (#361) must be merged, because the switch is delivered *through* the claim and the canonical-to-route ID map is carried *by* the catalog rows. If both are merged when phase 1 ends, 1b runs next; if not, the plan proceeds to phase 2 and 1b runs whenever they land. This is the only prerequisite in the plan that is another team's PR, and it is stated here so a reviewer does not read the phase order as a hidden dependency.
- **P8. Version skew is handled by one integer, `version`, in `config/policy/shared`, and by three messages, not by a capability table.** The runner refuses a `version` it does not know, naming both numbers and the fix (`fugaro upgrade`, then refresh the job images); `project policy publish` refuses to write a version above the release the binary is, so a beta cannot publish a shape `main` cannot read; `fugaro doctor` reports a published policy newer than the binary and a policy published while the installation's jobs still carry the old image (12a item 6 — an **old baked runner image silently ignores the published policy until refreshed**, which is the trap this message exists for). *Veto alternative:* no version field, tolerate unknown keys — rejected: the design's §11 names version skew as a cross-cutting item, and this is the cheapest mechanism that answers it.
- **P9. A profile is `{provider, cpu, memory, timeout class, secrets, allow_repos}` in the policy — **the provider is required** — and the installation root creates **one job and one job service account per (profile, provider, account)**, which is one job per (profile, account) *within* the single provider the profile pins.** **One provider per profile, settled (R2-11):** a profile names exactly one provider ('github' or 'bitbucket', validated with `task.ValidProvider`, which #372 exports at `internal/task/task.go:169`), so the triple is really (profile, account) with the provider determined by the profile — but the plan writes the **triple everywhere**, because it is the triple that names a job, and "per (profile, account) alone" is what let the two cardinalities drift apart in the earlier draft. A project with repositories on both providers defines two profiles (`standard` for GitHub, `standard-bitbucket` for Bitbucket); the default `standard` names one provider and a GitHub-only project never notices. `workflows.<name>.job_profile` selects it, defaulting to `standard` (the key that ships is `job_profile`, not the `profile` this plan and the design write: `profile` already names the project layer's workflow template in `fugaro.yaml`, so one key cannot be both selections — a design amendment Task 3.0 applies). **The provider is a field of the profile, not of the job env** (finding 4, a design amendment): one job cannot serve a GitHub and a Bitbucket repository at once, and the provider cannot come from `$FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER` because a launcher can override it (12a item 2). It is encoded in the job's own name, which Cloud Run sets — the one identifier a launcher cannot write. A shared account (phase 5) is a new job *per profile* and is created by Terraform through the installation root — **the plan picks Terraform, not the API**, for the phase-3 job set, because the installation root already owns every project-level resource and the `image refresh` precedent (`internal/cli/image_refresh_steps.go:241-304`) is a *patch*, not a create. Task 3.2 says what that costs (one apply per profile added) and Task 5.2 revisits it for OAuth accounts once the phase-3 pattern exists.
- **P10. The export omits people by default and never writes a secret value; the choice is a flag, and the flag's help text says what it publishes.** `fugaro project export` writes no `byPerson` spend and no kill-switch author, and `--with-people` includes them as the operator's own choice ("they are committed to a git repository", design §8). **The only other switch is `--with-admin`, and it is not an exception to "nothing else is optional"** (finding 28): the design's §5/§7 put the owner's identity in `config/admin` **and in the export**, so `--with-admin` is the *content* switch for admin **metadata** (which is always written in some form), while `--with-people` and its default are the *privacy* switch — Task 6.1's layout says which file each lands in and how the two interact. A test asserts that a byte-for-byte scan of the export for every value Secret Manager holds finds none (Task 6.1). *Veto alternative:* hash the emails and always write them — rejected by the design's own reasoning (a hash is reversible against a dictionary of the team's addresses) and because an export that always writes a derivative of a person's address is not the "omitted by default" the design promised. **In a monorepo this default does more work than it did in a dedicated repository** (the 2026-10-15 revision): the folder is readable by everyone who can read that repository, so the same flag that was a privacy nicety in a repository the operators control becomes the thing that keeps a team's addresses out of a tree their colleagues read (design §8).
- **P11. The backing location is three settings, the export writes and stages only its own folder, and the push is never a force.** (2026-10-15, the owner's mono-repo requirement.) `repo`, `path` (default `fugaro-project/`), `branch` (default `fugaro-project`, never the monorepo's `main` unless the owner writes it), carried as one `backing` object in the policy; the export writes **only** under `path`, stages **only** `path` (`TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath`), fetches and commits on the fetched tip and then pushes **plainly** — no `--force`, no `--force-with-lease`, no `+` refspec — and **fails loudly** when a change inside the folder makes the commit impossible instead of overwriting it. The commit is quiet: a `[skip ci]` marker, the fixed `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>` identity, and a sorted, stable tree. *Veto alternatives:* inherit the repository's default branch (a machine commit on the branch a monorepo most often protects, and one nobody chose); force the push after a fetch (the fastest way to delete a colleague's commit, and the reason `gitops.Repo.Push`'s forced-under-lease semantics are **not** reused); `git add -A` after writing the tree (one stray file in the export's working directory and the commit carries it). **D14 was the owner's decision about the branch and about the PR mode, and the owner answered it on 2026-10-15** — a dedicated branch by default (`fugaro-project`, writing only under `fugaro-project/`), never the monorepo's `main`, the PR mode a documented opt-in not built in the first slice; P11 is this plan's implementation of that ruling, which is the recommendation it already carried.

  **The fix round (2026-10-15) adds the five things the first revision left implicit, and each of them is now a test rather than a sentence:** **(a)** `path` has **one grammar**, written once in design §8 and pointed at from Task 6.2 — the repository root as `""` or `"."`, or a normalised, relative, `/`-separated, trailing-slash folder path with no `.`/`..`/empty segment and no `.git` segment in any case — with `TestBackingPathGrammar` carrying one row per case, and its **filesystem half** (a segment that resolves through a symlink; a case-variant collision) as its own tests, because the string half is the half a reader assumes is sufficient. **(b)** The conflict rule has a **detector**, without which the rule was unwritable as a test: the export records the **git tree hash of what it last wrote**, the marker itself excluded, in `<path>.fugaro-export.json`, and refuses — naming the paths, printing both hashes, committing nothing — when the fetched folder's tree differs from it or when a non-empty folder the export did not write carries no marker. **(c)** The two overrides are `--adopt` ("whose folder is this?" — a folder the export did not write) and `--force-regenerate` ("may I discard this edit?" — a folder whose marker does not match), and a scheduled or agent-run export may use neither. **(d)** The branch is **bootstrapped from the default branch's current tip, never orphan**, and the clone is **shallow, blobless and sparse** (`--depth 1`, `--filter=blob:none`, `git sparse-checkout set <path>`), so a monorepo costs one folder. **(e)** The credential is **the operator's own local git credentials — the SSH agent, the credential helper, or `gh auth` — and never the runner's git secret**, which no person can read and which a manual export therefore cannot use; the scheduled export is the one that will need a machine credential, and that is D7's deferred half. *Veto alternatives for the five:* normalise a `path` instead of refusing it (a folder the owner did not name); diff the folder against the parent commit instead of a marker (a person's committed edit and the export's own last write are indistinguishable that way, and a commit-less edit is invisible); an orphan branch (a reviewer cannot diff the folder against anything, and `git diff <default>..<branch>` reports the whole folder as added); a full clone (a monorepo's history, on the operator's laptop, for one folder); the runner's git secret (an authority the operator cannot inspect, for an action the operator runs by hand). **The PR mode's one wording, used by design §8, D14 and Task 6.2 alike: *a dedicated branch by default; the PR mode is a documented opt-in, not built in the first slice.***

## Review notes (where this plan disagrees with, or defers, a review finding)

**Read this first, because it is the round-2 review's own blocking finding about these notes (R2-1).** The round-1 notes that stood here said that everything not listed "was confirmed against the code and fixed as the review asked", and that claim was **false**: only the six blocking findings and one later one had been applied, and the round-1 status the round-2 review recorded was **22 findings open and 4 partly resolved**. This revision applies **every one of them**, and the follow-up account on the PR lists the edit behind each. The notes below are therefore only what this plan **does not** adopt as written — one line each, no claim that unlisted work was done.

- **Finding 2, partly.** The launcher's mint decision now follows the policy (Task 1.3 step 2). But the plan keeps the local mode as a **fallback when no policy exists**: an installation that has deliberately turned the budget off (design §5(1)) stays off. That is the design's own promise, not an oversight.
- **Finding 6 / D13.** Superseded by the owner's ruling (2026-10-15): the plan no longer recommends and no longer leaves anything open — see **D13 (ruled)** below. The review's implied preference for per-profile isolation became the *mitigation* the ruling names rather than a separate option to choose.
- **Findings 8, 9, 10 and R2-7 (the D6 shape).** The plan **changes, and #373 does not**: what actually landed is a single `PERRUNOK` boolean macro emitted by `rules.go`'s `perRunOK` that **deletes** `REPOPERRUN` and `GLOBALPERRUN`, so Task 0.3 now describes that rather than prescribing the `||` spelling. The review's reading ("one boolean, because a comparison against an unset side must not deny on its own") is adopted; its proposed spelling (`own.exists() ? own : (defaults.exists() ? defaults : global)`) is **not**, because that is a *cap selection*, while the implementation compares each side that is set — which is the semantics `Caps.PerRun` computes. One of the two had to change before #373 is reviewed against the plan; the plan is the one that changed.
- **Finding 16, partly.** The plan adopts a `policySince` launch gate **and** keeps `doctor`'s checks: the gate is a launch-time refusal, `doctor` is what tells an owner *before* a run fails. The review reads them as alternatives; they are complements, and 12a item 6 asks for the message, not only the gate. **Both now have an owning task (B5)**: the gate is Task 1.5 step 4 (the launcher's pre-launch read in `internal/cli/run.go`, with `cloneSince`/`recipes_skew.go:43`'s shape as the precedent), and the doctor half is Task 1.6 step 4's fifth check.
- **Finding 17.** Adopted as a **sequenced, owner-run** migration rather than a flag a repository could set: `init --repo` refuses baked workflows **only for new onboarding**, the owner migrates `belong` after `fugaro`, and the task says the refusal is sequenced and that no agent touches `belong`.
- **Finding 19, and the false claim that stood in its place (B3).** Task 1.6 step 2 used to seed `allow_repos` from the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes while P4 and 12a item 4 forbade exactly that, so the line below that listed finding 19 among the findings "with a task edit" was **false for it** — the same failure of a blanket claim the round-2 review caught in R2-1. The step is corrected rather than the claim softened, and the named edit is Task 1.6 step 2 (`TestProjectPolicyPublishNeverSeedsFromTheBucket`). The reason is two-fold, and the second is why this is a security fix and not a tidiness one: a slug cannot be turned back into a name (so seeding would enroll a repository the operator never named), and after Task 3.2's broadened `runs/` grant **every run of the profile can write those prefixes**, so the seed would be a path by which an enrolled repository enrolls anything it likes.
- **Finding 18.** Adopted: Task 4.2 does **not** reuse `init --repo --retire` (that flag is the config-transitions plan's own, R2) — it uses `--offboard` and #365's `Retirements`/`RetireSet`/`Orphans` helpers.
- **Findings 29 and 30 (testability).** Adopted in full: the refusal strings are exported from `internal/budget` so the CLI test can actually fail when one of them names a flag that does not exist, and every check's probe step names its `gcloud` line.
- **Finding 33, naming (and C2, the file list).** Adopted: `fugaro project repos ls|add|rm|sync`, and — corrected, because two earlier drafts said "**one file** for the group" and the later tasks' own Files lines contradicted them — the group is **three new files plus the existing helper file**: `internal/cli/project_cmd.go` (Task 1.6: `newProjectCmd()`, `policy publish` with `--delete`, `policy show`, the doctor checks), `internal/cli/project_enroll.go` (Task 4.1: `project sync` and enrollment), `internal/cli/project_export.go` (Task 6.1: `project export [--push]`), beside `internal/cli/project.go`, which **already exists** as the checkout/project *selection* helper (`checkoutProject`, `:30`) and stays what it is — it is not the command group's file, and Task 1.6's `newProjectCmd()` is registered in `internal/cli/root.go:75-78`. The file-structure table's 1.6 row says the same now.
- **R2-8(a) (the no-token case).** Adopted as the review asks — the `policySince` launch gate is what closes it — but the plan **disagrees with one word**: it is not a *bypass* to be refused at the runner (a run with no token object has no RTDB client at all), so the refusal is the launcher's, and SECURITY.md states it as a D10-class launcher choice. That is the same shape P3 already takes for the credential-less run **on a budget-off job** (B1): on a budget-on job the absent token object is a refusal at the runner (`budget_session.go:186-187`), and the gate's job is to refuse the launch, not to be the only thing standing between the run and the env ceiling. **Tightened after the follow-up security review of PR #379 (the M2 finding), and the owner can veto this half on its own.** The sentence above says a run with no token object has no RTDB client at all, so the runner cannot tell a *deleted* token from a project that published nothing — and a mode-off job of a project whose launcher minted a token therefore ran on the environment ceiling with no session, no cap and no kill switch when another run of the same repository took its token object. The launcher now also sets `FUGARO_BUDGET_TOKEN=1` as an **execution override** whenever it minted, and the runner refuses a missing token whenever that marker is set, whatever the job's own mode (and refuses a marked run whose job carries no budget backend at all, the state `init --repo` leaves behind when it was never re-applied). A job with no marker keeps today's behaviour exactly, so the legacy path this note is about is unchanged; what changes is that a *minted* launch is no longer read as a minted-nothing one. `internal/runner`'s `BudgetTokenEnv` is the whole marker, and `print`/`result.json` never carry it (only the fact, never a secret).
- **R2-14 (the Notes' own finding-6 line).** The review is right that it read as if the ruling were still the plan's to make; it is replaced by the D13 ruling above.
- **R2-9 (the `fake` provider in `providerForSlug`).** The finding is right that a kind set of `{github, bitbucket}` would refuse every `--provider fake` run, and the plan adopts its **second** suggestion rather than its first: the binding is scoped to runs the CLI resolved as local (`r.d.Local && r.d.Execution == ""`) instead of adding `fake` to the kind set. A `fake` run has no job and no bucket condition, so there is nothing for the binding to bind; adding the kind would make the binding answer a question that does not arise on that path. One line, because the reviewer may prefer the other half — both are written into Task 1.5 step 2 so the choice is visible rather than incidental.
  **Superseded in part, after the follow-up review of the phase-1 code.** The plan first wrote the second suggestion as "scope the binding to runs with a Cloud Run execution at all" (`backend.ExecutionFromEnv`), and that spelling is the fail-open direction: `Local`'s zero value means the cloud (`internal/backend`'s `platform.go`), and a job whose environment was edited after launch carries no execution name either — so a process **no backend claims** was exempted from the slug↔task binding, the remote's host and the allow-list while its git credential stayed scoped to whatever remote that environment named. What landed is the pair: `internal/runner`'s `admissionCheck` skips the checks only for a run the CLI resolved as local, and every other process — an unclaimed one in the cloud included — has them applied and fails closed. A `fake` run is local, so it still reaches neither check; the difference is what an *unclaimed* process gets.
- **Finding 10, the zero cap.** Adopted in the direction the review's own text implies: the step removes the cap with `--global --clear` rather than setting `--per-run 0` (which is a cap of zero, and `--clear` drops the daily cap too, so the step re-sets both). The plan **does not drop the step**, as the finding offered as an alternative, because "no global per-run cap" is exactly the state the fallback must be live-tested in.
- **Finding 32 and R2-16, the red steps.** Adopted for **every** task, not only the security-sensitive ones: each of the 44 tasks now ends with a `**Red step — Expected: FAIL**` line naming the test (or tests) it starts with and the command that must fail first. Three tasks — 3b.1 (a design document), 5.4 (a rebase of #363) and any other whose artifact is prose rather than behaviour — say in that line *why* their red state is the docs test instead; the plan does not pretend a design document can fail a Go assertion. The security-sensitive tasks the finding names (2.1, 3.2, 3.3, 5.1, 6.1) each name their failing test explicitly, and 3.2's is the golden-plan assertion that the project root's resource set changed.

**The blanket claim that used to close these notes is deleted, not reworded.** It said "nothing else is deferred: every other round-1 finding … and every deferrable of round 2 … has a task edit in this revision", and the verification review found items it does not hold for — the same failure of a blanket claim the round-2 review caught in R2-1, repeated in the paragraph that replaced it. **No claim is made here about findings not named above.** What the plan does *not* adopt is listed under **Accepted as deferred** at the end of this document, with a reason each; what it adopts is named inline, in the task that does the work, wherever a task depends on a finding. The notes above are only where the plan deliberately differs from a finding (R2-9, finding 10's alternative, finding 16's complement, R2-8(a)'s wording) or records a superseded one (findings 6/D13, 8 to 10/R2-7, 19, R2-14).

## Design amendments (what the plan changes in `docs/design/project-centric.md`)

The design is the authority for this plan, and **nine** of the plan's fixes are **changes to it**, not implementations of it. They are listed here, exhaustively, so the owner's assistant can apply them to the design in one pass and no implementer has to guess which document wins. **They are applied by Task 0.5** — a named, docs-only task in phase 0 that lands on `main`, with its own docs test asserting each amendment's load-bearing sentence is present. Until this round the section said "the owner's assistant can apply them" and named no task, which is the gap the review found: eight of the nine had an implementing task and none edited the document. The plan follows the amended text below; where a task cites the design, it cites it as amended.

1. **A D13 row in §12's decisions table** (§12, next to D4; Task 3.2 step 2, Task 3b.1). The owner ruled on **2026-10-15**: under the broadened `runs/` grant, **accept** the budget-token exposure, **document it in SECURITY.md beside D4's cache- and lock-poisoning regression**, **design a token channel in phase 3b** (a channel that does not live under a prefix every run can list), and **use separate profiles for repositories that must be kept apart** meanwhile. The design's decisions table gains the row with that date; the design's D4 paragraph gains the sentence that the token path is inside the grant; and §4.1's table gains the row that a profile is the isolation boundary for budget identity too. **The plan no longer treats this as open anywhere** — see **D13 (ruled)** below.
2. **D8 is superseded** (§12 D8, §4.4, §11 phase 4). The design's D8 kept the retirement work to a "core"; **the full config-transitions plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-14-config-transitions.md`, Tasks 1 to 9) ships on `main` in 0.7.5** (its core is PR #365, in flight), so phase 4 builds on the **whole** engine, including that plan's own Task 5 `--retire`. The design's D8 row becomes "superseded: the full plan ships".
3. **§5's definition of project-centric mode** (§5, §5(3); P3). The design defines the mode as the existence of `config/policy/shared`. **It is now: the rules-version marker `config/limits/policyRules` is at least `RulesVersion` **and** `config/policy/shared` exists** (R3), and a run with **no budget credential on a budget-off job** takes the mode-off path — the launcher's readiness gate is its refusal, because the run cannot read the marker at all without the credential. **On a budget-on job that same absence is a halt, not the mode-off path** (B1, `internal/runner/budget_session.go:174-175`,`:186-187`), so the design must state the mode-off case as the *job's own mode being off* rather than as a missing credential, or the design and the plan disagree about what the mode is.
4. **A profile carries its provider, and it is required — jobs are per (profile, provider, account)** (§4.1, §4.1(1); Task 3.1 step 2, Task 3.2 step 1). The design says "one job and one job service account per (profile, account)". That is only well-defined once a profile pins **one** provider: the provider is part of the slug (`task.Slug(provider, repo)` hashes the provider and the canonical name, `internal/task/task.go:187`), part of the job's identity and part of the git credential the job holds, so a job cannot serve repositories of two providers. **One provider per profile, and the job set is one job per (profile, provider, account)** (R2-11 settles the cardinality the design's §4.1 leaves ambiguous).
5. **The provider is not a job environment variable** (§4.1(3), §12a item 2; Task 3.2 step 1, Task 3.3 steps 1 and 2). The design's item 1 has the runner derive its profile and account "from `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, which the runner already reads" — a derivation the plan makes real — and item 2 forbids taking the provider from an overridable env var. Together they mean **the provider must come from the job name too**, and the design's "which the runner already reads" is itself wrong (it reads it through `backend.ExecutionFromEnv`, §12a item 1's own drift note).
6. **Admission and the profile halt apply to project jobs only; a legacy job keeps the phase-1 contract** (§4.1, §4.4; Task 3.3 step 1). The design's phase 3 assumes every repository has moved onto a project job. The plan's phases are ordered so they have not — phase 4 migrates, and D3 keeps baked workflows per-repository until it does — so the runner must tell the two job shapes apart and apply the phase-1 rules to the legacy one. **A project job's name encodes the profile, provider and account** (§4.1; Task 3.2 step 1), and the design names neither the spelling nor how a run recognises one; the plan fixes both (a match against the names **computed** from the policy's set, never a parse).
7. **The rules-version marker** (§5, §11, §12a item 6; Tasks 0.4, 1.1, 1.2, 1.6, 1.7). The design has no rules-version marker and relies on `doctor` alone for version skew, which is a fail-open. The design gains: the marker `config/limits/policyRules`, **written after `PutRules` succeeds** in `fugaro init --firebase`; "a published policy implies the rules are deployed" as a property of `policy publish`; a `policySince` **launch gate** in `fugaro run` for the release skew (**Task 1.5 step 4**, B5; the constant is `policySince = "0.8.0"`, the release the model ships in); and the halt message for an **older CLI's `init --firebase`** that redeploys pre-phase-1 rules while the marker stays.
8. **D3 and the `belong` note** (§12 D3, §4.4, §11 phase 4; Tasks 4.4, 4.5, 2.3, 4.6). The design's D3 drops baked workflows after phase 4. As written that breaks the owner's own production installation on upgrade: `belong` holds `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb`, both baked, and it is **not** this plan's to migrate. The design says instead: the refusal is **for new onboarding**, the owner migrates `belong` **after** the model is proven on `fugaro`, and **no agent ever touches `belong`**. The design also carries **D2's release note for `belong`** (the `secrets set` default changes there too) — and, with §11, the release plan: **phase 0 on `main` in 0.7.5's line, phases 1, 1b and 2 as 0.8.0, and phases 3 to 6 as 0.9.0 together with the compute backend** (R4 as amended by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15, R6).
9. **D5 is amended by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15: Bitbucket is not first-class in phase 3** (§12 D5, §2, §4.5, §11's phase-3 live check, §13; Task 0.5 step 9, Tasks 3.0 step 2, 3.5 step 3, 3.7). The design's D5 row promises a **project-wide Bitbucket access token** after a verification task, and its §4.5 carries the design's own branch for the other outcome ("If the verification fails, Bitbucket projects stay on the per-repository path and the owner is told before phase 3 starts"). **The owner ruled the verification on 2026-10-15 — "cannot be scoped"**: a Bitbucket credential cannot be scoped to one project's repositories. So D5's row becomes **"Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path in phase 3: no Bitbucket credential can be scoped to a project's repositories (owner ruling 2026-10-15), and the project-wide token this decision assumed does not exist"**; §4.5 **states the ruling** in place of leaving the scoping UNVERIFIED and says the shared jobs phase 3 builds serve **GitHub** repositories; §13's entry for the Bitbucket token scope is **settled by that ruling**, not UNVERIFIED; §2's "Bitbucket is first-class in phase 3 (D5)" sentence is corrected the same way; and §11's phase-3 live check is corrected to the **GitHub path** — there is no shared Bitbucket job to check, and the Bitbucket sandbox keeps its per-repository job and its own token. The runtime-fetch fallback §4.5 names stays UNVERIFIED-and-unadopted (it reintroduces §4.1's shared-service-account exposure). **The plan no longer treats D5 as open anywhere.** This is the ninth amendment — it was written into Task 0.5's step list by PR #386 and is listed here too, because this list is the authority Task 0.5's steps are written from.

**What the plan does not change, deliberately**: the design's decisions D1a to D12 stand except where the nine amendments above rewrite them, its phases keep their order, and the plan adds no phase (3b is a slot the owner schedules, see below). This plan's own decisions (P1 to P11) are the ones a reviewer vetoes; the amendments above are the ones the *design* needs.

**The 2026-10-15 revision's backing-location change is not a tenth amendment: it is written into the design by the revision itself, in the same commit as the plan.** The nine above are changes a *later* task applies (Task 0.5, on `main`), which is why they are listed rather than made. This one is different in kind: the owner's requirement changes what phase 6 *is* (`repo`/`path`/`branch`, the folder's fence, a plain push, D14), phase 6 ships on `next` (R4), and splitting it into "the plan says one thing, the design another, a task reconciles them later" would leave the two documents disagreeing for as long as the revision is unreviewed. So `docs/design/project-centric.md`'s §8, §11 phase 6 and §12 (D14's row) are edited here, together with this plan's phase 6, and the two are read as one change. **One consequence to state rather than discover at a merge:** Task 0.5 edits the same §12 decisions table on `main` — D13's row next to D4, and (from PR #386's Task 3.0) D8's and D5's — while this revision adds D14's row at the end of that table on `next` — **the later merge keeps both sides' rows**, since no edit touches another's line, and Task 0.5's rows are applied whichever order the two land in.

## D13 (ruled 2026-10-15): the budget token under the broadened `runs/` grant

**The owner ruled on 2026-10-15. The ruling is option 1 of the three below, plus a phase 3b task that designs option 3.** It is recorded here in the ruling's own terms, because it overrides what this section said while the decision was open.

**The finding.** Phase 3 (Task 3.2) replaces the per-slug bucket condition with one condition per prefix, because one job service account now serves every repository of a profile (design §4.1). D4 accepts the consequence the design listed: within a profile a run could poison another repository's cache, forge or steal a lock, or rewrite a queued `task.json`. **But `runs/` is not only the queue**: the launcher writes each run's budget token to `runs/<slug>/<run>/budget-token` (`internal/budget/token/object.go:15`), and that token *is* the run's RTDB identity (`token.Claims` carries `fs` — the slug — and `fr` — the run id). A run that can list and read `runs/**` can therefore **take a queued run's token and act as that run in the database**: read `config/policy/repos/<that slug>`, read that slug's caps and kill switches, and write that run's ledger, reserve and spend. That is a different class of harm from cache poisoning — a **budget-identity theft across repositories** — and it is not in D4's accepted list. It also interacts with `CheckRunExecution` (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go:88-91`): the same grant lets a run rewrite another run's `launch.json`/`result.json`, which Task 3.4 and SECURITY.md state, and which is *not* part of the open question.

**The ruling, in full:**

1. **Accept and document.** Phase 3.2 ships the broadened `runs/` grant with the token exposure accepted. **SECURITY.md states it immediately after D4's cache- and lock-poisoning regression**, in these words: within a profile, a run can read and take another *queued* run's budget token and act as that run until the token's own expiry (about an hour, `token.ExpiryFX`), so **profiles are the isolation boundary for budget identity too**, and **repositories that must not share a budget identity use separate profiles**. `docs/design/bucket-iam.md`'s prefix table gains the token path and why it is inside the grant, and the **0.9.0** release note repeats it (phase 3 is 0.9.0 under the owner's release ruling of 2026-10-15, R6 — see the 0.9.0 Highlights line at the top).
2. **Design the channel in phase 3b — Task 3b.1**, a new task in this plan (see below), whose subject is a token channel that **does not live under a prefix every run of a shared job can list**. It is design work with its own security review, not code in phase 3.
3. **Separate profiles meanwhile.** Until 3b lands, the mitigation is the one the paragraph names: two repositories that must not share a budget identity are put on different profiles, because a profile is a job and a job is a service-account boundary.

**What this removes from the plan:** the gate. Task 3.2 **no longer waits for a ruling and no longer describes its step 2 as provisional** — the paragraph above is the final wording SECURITY.md gets. The phase-3 introduction, the audit's 3.2 row, the security-sensitive table and P9 all say "D13 ruled 2026-10-15" instead of "open", and **no task in phases 0 to 6 is held by D13**. Task 3b.1 is scheduled by the owner, not by phase 3: it blocks nothing before it.

## Owner rulings of 2026-10-17 on phase 3

**The owner answered the open phase-3 questions on 2026-10-17. Nothing below is approved work beyond what the owner already approved: phase 3 stays unmerged drafts until 0.8.0 is cut and its tasks are scheduled as before. Each ruling names the task it changes, and the original text of every task is kept where a ruling supersedes it.**

1. **`fugaro logs` and `fugaro diagnose` KEEP refusing a run whose Cloud Run execution no longer exists.** No bucket-only fallback is added. The refusal is `cloudEnv.checkExecution` (`internal/cli/cloud.go`), which ties an execution to the run's own job through `gcp.CheckRunExecution` (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go`) and refuses an execution it cannot tie. Task 3.4 step 6 and its test pin it on shared jobs.
2. **A Claude OAuth launch onto a shared project job is REFUSED UP FRONT, with a clear message.** The refusal happens before the execution is created. It is a small code change and is **Task 3.4a** below (numbered 3.4a so no task is renumbered).
3. **`FUGARO_GITHUB_APP_ID` (non-secret) is ADDED to the shared job's environment** (the constant is `providers.EnvGitHubAppID` in `internal/gitprov/providers/providers.go`, which the per-repository path already sets at `internal/infra/spec.go`, in the workflow environment and again in the build environment). The private key stays a Secret Manager secret (Task 3.3 step 6). Written into **Tasks 3.5 and 3.6** (step 4 of each).
4. **A v1 policy node is migrated by `fugaro project policy publish --delete` and a republish. There is no migration code.** The flag is `--delete` on `policy publish` (`internal/cli/project_cmd.go`, `deleteProjectPolicy`), and today's `projectpolicy.Version` is 1 (`internal/projectpolicy/version.go`). Written into Task 3.1 step 6.
5. **`policySince` names 0.8.5.** Today the constant reads `"0.8.0"` (`internal/cli/recipes_skew.go`); the owner ruled it names **0.8.5**. Per the constant's own comment it is edited by the release that ships the model, so the edit is made in that release's change and verified by `docs/release.md`'s "Before you tag" checklist, **not by this docs change**. Recorded at Task 1.5 step 4.
6. **The bucket-layout limit is ACCEPTED, and documented for 0.8.5.** A profile separates **secrets and the job's service-account identity, not bucket objects**: the bucket conditions of Task 3.2 are per prefix (`runs/`, `cache/`, `locks/`) and are the same for every profile's service account, because the layout has no profile segment (D4, D13). So D13's interim mitigation below ("two repositories that must not share a budget identity are put on different profiles, because a profile is a job and a job is a service-account boundary") is true of secrets and identity and **not** of the objects under those prefixes; the text is kept as written above and this ruling bounds it. A **profile segment in the bucket layout is a later breaking change**, not part of 0.8.5 or phase 3. Task 3.2 step 2's SECURITY.md and `docs/design/bucket-iam.md` wording carries the limit; **SECURITY.md is edited in the change that ships it, not here** (this change is docs-only under `docs/`).

## Phase 3b: the budget-token channel (one task, scheduled by the owner; its output ships in **0.9.0**, R6)

**Phase 3b is a slot the owner schedules, not part of the 0-to-6 sequence**: it is where a D13 option-3 token channel is designed, and it blocks nothing in phases 0 to 6 (the ruling above is what lets phase 3 ship without it). It is written here so the channel has a home and a task, rather than being a sentence in a decisions table.

**Which release the seal ships in — an owner decision, not a ruling of the split.** The seal itself is designed and answered (`docs/design/budget-token-channel.md` §7.1, D19 — "the seal ships first, on its own", 2026-10-15), and it ships **with phase 3, in 0.9.0**, **unless the owner pulls Slice A earlier**. **Slice A is the runner's side** (`takeBudgetToken` unsealing before `checkNames`, `BackendFromEnv`'s strict parse of the marker's value, and the runner's refusal to echo that value plus its registration of the key with its redactor — the half D19 words as "runner and image first, launcher second") and it is a **runner-first change that is safe to release on its own**: the envelope's version byte distinguishes a plaintext object, so a launcher that does not seal yet is still readable, and no grant changes with it. The **launcher-seals slice** (the mint writing a sealed object, and the marker's value becoming the key) and the **`tokens/` move with its prefix condition** are **not earlier than 0.9.0** — the move lands in the same change as the grant that reads it, Task 3.2's, which is D18's and D19's ordering. Recorded here as the owner's decision line so the plan and the design agree; it is **not** one of R1 to R6, and the heading's own default above ("its output ships in **0.9.0**, R6") is the release this line qualifies.

### Task 3b.1: the token channel design — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `docs/design/budget-token-channel.md` (new), `SECURITY.md` (a pointer once it lands).

1. **The subject, exactly:** a channel by which a run learns its budget token that is **not** an object under a prefix every run of the same shared job can list. The candidates, each priced: a signed URL minted by the launcher and handed to the run as an override; a Secret Manager secret created by the launcher, read only by the run's own service account (which needs the IAM conditions Task 3.0 step 4 probes — **if that probe failed, this candidate is out and the document says so**); a `tokens/<run-hash>` prefix whose IAM condition names one run; or taking the token off the object store entirely and having the run exchange its own Cloud Run execution identity for an RTDB one.
2. **What it must not do:** put the credential back into a launcher-overridable env var (D10, 12a item 2), or widen any service account's grants beyond the prefix it needs.
3. **Its output is a design document with the options compared, a recommendation, and the migration of the token object out of `runs/`** — and it is reviewed by opus on its own (the security-sensitive flag below), because it changes the same boundary D13 accepted.
4. **What it changes when it lands (recorded now, not designed now):** `internal/cli/launch_budget.go` (the mint), `internal/budget/token/object.go` (the object key and its `TakeObject`), the Terraform in `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/project/`, the runner's bootstrap and the SECURITY.md paragraph D13 wrote. **It lands in 0.9.0**, with the rest of phases 3 to 6 and the compute backend (the owner's release ruling of 2026-10-15, R6): the token move is one of the things that ruling names as no longer 0.8.0's, so the design this task writes is written against 0.9.0.

**Tests:** none of its own (it is a design document; its *implementation* is a later plan). **Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` (docs only). **Depends on:** 3.2 (the grant it narrows) and 3.0 step 4 (the Secret Manager IAM answer, which kills or keeps one candidate). **Commit:** "design: the budget-token channel outside the runs prefix". It is **SECURITY-SENSITIVE — independent opus review**, and the owner schedules it: nothing in this plan waits for it.

**Red step (finding 32, R2-16): this task has none of its own, and here is why.** It produces a design document, not behaviour, so no Go test can fail before it and pass after. Its red state is the **docs** test it does have: the document does not exist, so `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` fails until it is written — `Expected: FAIL` → `Expected: PASS`. The implementation it designs carries its own red steps in the plan that ships it, and this document states them as that plan's acceptance criteria.

## Global Constraints

From the design:
- **A phase merges alone.** Each phase's tasks are complete without any later phase: no task may read a file, node, flag or Terraform output that a later task creates. Where a phase *wants* something a later phase brings (phase 1's profile admission, phase 3's project jobs), it says so and ships the narrower thing (P6).
- **No repository is ever in a state where a shared job serves it without an allow-list match** (design §4.3 "Ordering"): the allow-list and the runner's checks ship in phase 1, and phase 3's shared jobs come after.
- **Project-centric mode requires Firebase and the budget backend** (D11). An installation that wants the budget mode off stays on the per-repository path; that is a permanent second code path and the design accepts it (§5).
- **Launchers stay trusted** (D10, accepted): the runner cannot remove the launcher's ability to override a job's env, and no phase claims to. `FUGARO_RUN` itself is set as a `ContainerOverride` (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go:110`). SECURITY.md states it **in phase 1**, from the phase-1 evidence in **Check 49 step 6** (a live `FUGARO_RUN` override on a real job — finding 14: the paragraph does **not** wait for Task 3.0's phase-3 probe, which was a forward edge and is gone), and Task 3.0 step 3's U2 probe then decides only what the *phase-3* text may add.
- **`fugaro validate` is offline** (design §11): it cannot see the routing resolution or the policy unless it is given a project config that can read them. What it prints when it cannot — "unresolved offline" — is a *requirement*, not a wording choice (Task 1b.3).
- **The RTDB policy replaces only the ceiling; `fugaro.yaml`'s layers still only tighten it** (12a item 13). `internal/runner/policy.go`'s merge (`:90`) is not inverted, and no phase makes a repository able to loosen the project policy.
- **Every owner-run step in this plan is marked `OWNER-RUN` and no agent performs it**: rules deploys, Terraform applies, `fugaro init --repo`, secret entry and every live check. Live checks run on installation `fugaro` and a sandbox repository only, **never the `belong` project**.
- **Every check's shell steps hard-code the project and refuse an empty variable**, and they carry **Check 43's full guard** (`docs/gcp-live-checklist.md:1158-1192` on `main`): three hard stops — `P` is exactly `fugaro-dev` with the variables non-empty, `fugaro doctor --project fugaro` reports that project, and the checkout's origin is `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` — plus `--project fugaro` on **every** `fugaro` command and `--project "${P:?}"` on **every** `gcloud` command, re-run from a fresh shell before each step. Task 0.4 step 1 spells the block once; every check task 49 to 56 and 61 requires it **verbatim**, not "like Check 48" (R2-6, round-1 findings 31 and 30).

Project rules (carried over from this repository's existing plans):
- Every task's own test run is in the foreground, `-count=1`, and `-race` where the package's existing tests use it.
- Fakes only in tests: no live RTDB, GCP or Cloud Run call from a test. The RTDB emulator (`internal/budget/rules/emulator/`) is the fake the rules tests use, and `internal/gcpfake` is the fake the Cloud Run paths use.
- **Security-sensitive tasks are flagged `SECURITY-SENSITIVE — independent opus review`** in their own heading, and are not merged on a self-review: phase 0's D6 rules change (**0.3**), phase 1's rules and policy node (**1.2**), the runner's fail-closed read (**1.3**), **the RTDB URL and API key that decide whether a published policy can be read at all (1.4)**, the slug binding's first half (**1.5**), **`policy publish`'s marker refusal and the allow-list seed (1.6)**, phase 2's project-scope keys (**2.1**), phase 3's shared-job IAM and bucket conditions (**3.2**) and the runner's slug binding, admission and fail-closed checks (**3.3**), **the token-channel design (3b.1)**, phase 5's OAuth pool (**5.1, 5.2**), phase 6's export (**6.1**). R2-13: 1.3's heading, 1.4's absence from this list, and 1.6's and 3b.1's absence were the gaps.
- **Every task has a red step.** Each task states the test (or the command) it expects to **FAIL** before its change and to pass after, as `- [ ] Step N … **Expected: FAIL**`, the shape `docs/plans/2026-10-13-project-secrets.md` uses (finding 32, R2-16). A task whose change cannot be made to fail first is written as one whose *test* can, or it says why not — an implementer is never told only which tests will exist.
- Docs must match behavior: `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` stays green throughout. A docs test that reads the changed text is updated in the same commit as the text.
- `fugaro verify build` / `fugaro verify test` are the only build and test entry points for this repository's own conventions; a task runs them before it is considered done.
- **No task may edit `.github/workflows/`** (a Fugaro-run constraint: GitHub refuses Fugaro's push of workflow changes). A phase that wants a CI change describes it in its PR description instead.

## Review Focus (mutation-proof notes)

1. **The global per-run cap becomes a fallback in the code and not in the rules, or the reverse.** Pinned by `TestPerRunFallsBackToGlobal` (`internal/budget/model_test.go`), by the emulator suite's `TestNullCapDenies` **re-pointed** (it asserts today that "no repository per-run cap" denies; under D6 with a global cap it must **allow**, and a per-run-only variant is the case that still denies — finding 8, and #373 has the Go mirror), and by `TestRulesParity`'s **generator**: `parity_test.go:175` is a randomized Evaluate-against-rules generator, **not** a case table, so the D6 case is added to `genCase` (a missing repository and defaults per-run cap with a global one set) rather than appended to a list (finding 8). The mutation that matters: `fallback()` (`internal/budget/rules/rules.go:341-346`) keeps returning null for a missing repository cap, so the rules deny while `model.go` computes a fallback — a run that halts with a message about a cap the owner did set.
2. **A refusal message names a flag that does not exist.** Pinned by `TestRefusalMessagesNameRealFlags`, which runs every `ReasonNoCap` detail string through the real flag set (`internal/cli/budget.go:687-691`'s flags, as the command registers them) and fails on any `--x` in the text that the command does not define. The current four (`internal/budget/evaluate.go:190`, `:247`, `internal/budget/session.go:501`, `:511`) fail it before Task 0.1 and pass after.
3. **An api-key run is refused for a key nobody needs.** Pinned by `TestApiKeyRunWithoutAnthropicKeyStarts` (`internal/agent`) and `TestGatewayStartsWithoutAnthropicUpstream` (`internal/gateway`), plus `TestReadinessDoesNotDemandTheAnthropicKey` (`internal/infra`): a workflow whose pins all route through a provider must reach the agent with no `anthropic-api-key` version at all. The mutation: leaving `internal/infra/spec.go:591`'s mount unconditional in the *readiness* path (`internal/infra/readiness.go:100-158`) so the deploy is still blocked.
4. **A published policy is silently ignored.** Pinned by `TestRunnerReadsPolicyWhenTheNodeExists` and `TestRunnerUsesEnvCeilingWhenTheNodeIsAbsent` (`internal/runner`), which assert the *effective* per-run cap and the recorded `policy.sources`, not merely that a read happened. The mutation: reading `config/policy` (the parent) instead of `.../shared`, which succeeds under an admin client and fails under a run token's rules — the test runs the runner with a token-scoped fake.
5. **A missing or unparseable policy falls back to an empty ceiling.** The design's §5(3) fail-closed requirement. Pinned by `TestPolicyReadErrorHaltsTheRun` and `TestPolicyParseErrorHaltsTheRun`: the run ends with a message naming the node, and `spend.Mode` is never left at the *less* constrained value. The mutation: treating any read error as "absent".
6. **A repository runs on a job it is not allowed on.** Pinned by `TestRunnerRefusesSlugNotInAllowList` and `TestRunnerRefusesSlugMismatch` (phase 3) — both assert the refusal happens **before** a token is minted or a lease taken, which is checked by asserting the fake saw no token request and no reserve write. The mutation: doing the check after `openProvider` — whose **calls** are `internal/runner/runner.go:838` and `:848`; `:586` is that function's definition (R2-12).
7. **The bucket conditions stop covering what they covered.** Pinned by the golden-plan test (`internal/infra/tf/testdata/golden/`, regenerated by `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh`) plus a runner-level test that a job service account's condition still names `runs/`, `cache/` and `locks/`. The plan does **not** pretend this is not a regression: the test asserts the broadened condition (one condition on each prefix), and SECURITY.md says so (D4).
8. **A project-wide secret reaches a repository the owner did not opt in.** This is the ruling D2 reverses; what must not break with it is the **data-policy gate**: pinned by `TestAllowDataToStillGatesProviderRoutes` (`internal/gateway`), which asserts a provider route is refused for a repository whose canonical name is not matched by the route's written `allow_data_to`, whatever the key's scope is.
9. **The routing switch becomes a pattern.** D1a: `routing.claude` carries an explicit ID list. Pinned by `TestRoutingClaudeRefusesAGlob` (phase 1b) and by `TestRepoCannotWriteRouting` — the policy's `shared` node is operator-written and a repository's `fugaro.yaml` has no path to it.
10. **The export writes a secret value or a person's address.** Pinned by `TestExportContainsNoSecretValue` (the export is scanned for every value the fake Secret Manager holds) and `TestExportOmitsPeopleByDefault` (no `byPerson`, no kill-switch author without `--with-people`). **The 2026-10-15 revision adds the monorepo half**: the same omissions matter more when the folder sits in a repository the whole company can read, and the fixed commit identity (`TestExportPushCommitIsNotTheOperator`, item 14) is the same principle applied to the exporter's own name.
11. **The canonical-to-route ID map translates a model nobody pinned, or fails to translate one that was.** Pinned by `TestCanonicalMapRoundTrip` (phase 1b): for each catalog row with a Claude canonical ID, the rendered pin, the charged price and the recorded `serving_model`/`priced_as` all name the same model, and a Claude pin with no route resolves to the direct ID exactly as today.
12. **`cloneShipped` is flipped before Check 38 passes.** Pinned by the existing gate tests (`docs_checkout_test.go:123-132`, `image_refresh_steps_test.go:662`, `init_repo_clone_test.go:19`), which fail if the flip and the docs disagree — a task flipping it must land Check 38's evidence first, and Task 3.6 says where that evidence is recorded.
13. **An owner-run step is run by an agent, or a check's guard falls back to an ambient project.** Every check task's steps carry **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1), and every check 49 to 56 and 61 names only the sandbox or a scratch repository, never a `belong` one; a reviewer reads each `gcloud` line for a bare ambient project, and `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded` is a docs test over `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` asserting that every `## Check 48`-onward section carries **Check 43's three hard stops and its project pinning, not merely a `:?` guard** (**and it is the backstop over all ten sections — 48 to 56 and 61; each check's own task also writes a `TestChecklistHasCheckNN` for its own section, so the property is asserted where the section is written and again across all of them**): `P` is exactly `fugaro-dev` with the other variables non-empty, `fugaro doctor --project fugaro` is called and its `project:` line grepped, the checkout's origin is grepped against `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`, every `fugaro` command in the section carries `--project fugaro` and every `gcloud` command `--project "${P:?}"`. **Owned by Task 6.3** (round-2 review: it was named here as "(phase 3)" and no task created it — it can only be written once all of 48 to 56 exist, which is why its home is the last phase's docs task, and the review focus now points there).
14. **The export rewrites a file outside its folder, or forces a push** (2026-10-15, the owner's mono-repo requirement; design §8, P11). Pinned by `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath` — a scratch repository whose tree already carries a `README.md`, `src/app.go` and a CI file, asserted byte-identical afterwards, with `git status --porcelain` empty outside the folder and the commit's own `--name-only` listing only the folder — and by `TestExportPushNeverForces`, whose fake git recorder fails on any `--force`, `--force-with-lease` or `+` refspec and on any call that is not the plain `push`; the branch-moved case is `TestExportPushRebasesOntoAMovedBranch` (the out-of-band commit must still be an ancestor) and the conflict case `TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath` (the refusal names the paths and the branch's tip is unchanged). The mutation these exist for: writing the tree into a scratch clone that already holds the repository's own files and committing with `git add -A` or `git commit -a`, which passes every other test in this phase and rewrites a monorepo. `TestExportPushCommitIsNotTheOperator` pins the quiet commit (the fixed `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>` author and committer, the `[skip ci]` marker in the subject) — an export that commits under the exporting operator's own `git config` identity writes their name and address into a repository other people read. **Live witness: Check 61** (Task 6.5), which makes the same assertions by hand against a scratch repository. **The fix round adds three more mutations to this item, each with its own test:** a `path` that is normalised rather than refused (`TestBackingPathGrammar` plus the filesystem half's `TestExportPushRefusesASymlinkEscape`, `TestExportPushRefusesAGitDirectoryUnderPath` and `TestExportPushRefusesACaseVariantCollision`); an export that overwrites an edit it did not make because nothing told it the folder had changed (`TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath`, `TestExportDetectsACommittedEditUnderPath`, `TestExportRefusesWhenTheMarkerIsMissingOnANonEmptyFolder`, and the two overrides' own tests); and an export that authenticates with the runner's git secret, which no person can read and which a manual export has no business holding (`TestExportPushUsesTheOperatorsLocalCredentials`).

## File Structure

| Path | Responsibility | Task |
|---|---|---|

## Phase 0 — small fixes (five tasks, plus the owner's deploy; on `main` in 0.7.5's line, R6)

The design's phase 0 (§11). Nothing here shares a surface with anything else in the plan, and each of the five tasks is independently vetoable: the four flag names (0.1), the optional Anthropic key (0.2), D6 (0.3), the live check and the rules deploy it depends on (0.4, **OWNER-RUN**), and the design document's own amendments (0.5, docs only). The earlier count here said four and omitted 0.4, whose task is that check — the same class of stale count the review found in Task 6.3's backstop.

### Task 0.1: the refusal messages name flags that exist

**Files:** `internal/budget/evaluate.go` (+ `evaluate_test.go`), `internal/budget/session.go` (+ `session_test.go`), **a new exported detail list in `internal/budget`** (finding 29 — the CLI test cannot see unexported strings), `internal/cli/budget_refusal_test.go` (new), `internal/runner/gateway_test.go` (the gateway's own two strings).

**Files it touches, verified today:** `internal/budget/evaluate.go:190` and `:247`, `internal/budget/session.go:501` and `:511`. The four strings are `fugaro budget set --defaults --repo-daily` and `--defaults --repo-per-run`; the flags the command actually registers are `--global`, `--defaults`, `--repo`, `--daily`, `--per-run` (`internal/cli/budget.go:687-691`), so the two messages must become `fugaro budget set --defaults --daily` and `fugaro budget set --defaults --per-run`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: nothing new. Produces: no symbol — the four strings, and one test that keeps them honest.

1. Fix `internal/budget/evaluate.go:190` (`no daily cap is set for this repository and there is no default (fugaro budget set --defaults --daily)`) and `:247` (`... there is no default (fugaro budget set --defaults --per-run)`).
2. Fix the same two strings in `internal/budget/session.go:501` and `:511`. The second one (`:511`) also names `budget.per_run_usd`; keep that and the `--global --per-run` half, which is a real flag.
3. `TestRefusalMessagesNameRealFlags` (new file, `package cli`): the strings live in `internal/budget`, but the *flags* live in this package, so the test is here — **and the earlier draft's version of it could not fail, which is finding 29.** It iterated a hand-copied `refusalMessages()` table inside `package cli`; that table cannot see `internal/budget`'s unexported strings, so editing `evaluate.go`'s text back to `--repo-per-run` would leave the test green. **The fix is to make the table come from the package that owns the strings**: `internal/budget` exports them — `budget.RefusalDetails()` returning a located list (`{Site, Detail}` for the two `evaluate.go` sites and the two `session.go` sites), or exported constants if the reviewer prefers — and **`noCap`'s own caller list is what keeps it complete**: `noCap` (`internal/budget/evaluate.go:244-249`) is called by `Evaluate` and by `Session.Check` (`internal/budget/session.go:497-500`), and both are in the same package, so the exported detail list is built where the strings are. The CLI test then walks the real command (`newBudgetCmd()`, including the child commands' flags — `budget set --per-run` is a child's) and fails on any `--`-prefixed token the command does not define, after normalizing `budget.per_run_usd` (a config key, not a flag) out. The two gateway strings (`internal/runner/gateway.go:425-433`, `noCapDetail`) stay a **second** test in `internal/runner`, which has no `budget` command to check them against: it asserts they name `fugaro init --repo` and, from phase 1 on, `fugaro project policy publish` (finding 23, Task 0.3 step 6). **The red step is the point of the change**: with steps 1 and 2 reverted, `go test ./internal/budget/... ./internal/cli/...` must fail on the exported details, not pass on a stale copy.

```go
// internal/cli/budget_refusal_test.go
func TestRefusalMessagesNameRealFlags(t *testing.T) {
	defined := map[string]bool{}
	newBudgetCmd().Flags().VisitAll(func(f *flag.Flag) { defined["--"+f.Name] = true })
	// a nested subcommand's flags (`budget set --per-run`) are on the child
	for _, c := range newBudgetCmd().Commands() {
		c.Flags().VisitAll(func(f *flag.Flag) { defined["--"+f.Name] = true })
	}
	for _, r := range budget.RefusalDetails() { // exported by internal/budget, so it cannot drift
		msg := r.Detail
		for _, tok := range strings.Fields(msg) {
			tok = strings.Trim(tok, "(),;")
			if !strings.HasPrefix(tok, "--") || defined[tok] || configKey(tok) {
				continue
			}
			t.Errorf("%q names %s, which budget does not define", msg, tok)
		}
	}
}
```

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/budget/... ./internal/cli/... -count=1`.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestRefusalMessagesNameRealFlags` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

**Commit:** one commit — "budget: the four refusal messages name flags that exist".

### Task 0.2: `anthropic-api-key` is not mandatory

**Files:** `internal/infra/spec.go` (+ `spec_test.go`, `secretmounts_test.go`), `internal/infra/readiness.go` (+ `readiness_test.go`), `internal/agent/env.go` (+ `env_test.go`), `internal/runner/gateway.go` (+ `gateway_test.go`), `internal/gateway/{gateway.go,route.go}` (+ tests), `docs/multi-model.md`.

**In flight, and it changes the sites: #371 implements this task.** Its branch is the phase-0 fix done the way this plan asks, and it landed **one site differently and one site not at all**, both of which the plan now records so a reader does not look for them (R2-7, GROUP C — this is in-flight accounting, not a new requirement):

- **The mount is decided in `internal/infra/readiness.go`, not in `SecretMounts`.** #371 adds `infra.OptionalSecret(logical)` (`internal/infra/readiness.go:58-63`) — only `anthropic-api-key` is optional — and `dropMount` (`:65-75`) — **these are the only ranges the plan gives for either symbol: they are #371's unmerged branch as of this plan's writing, so read them from the branch after it lands rather than trusting a number here** — which removes the mount (and its env var, and its redaction entry) when **no version exists in either the repository's copy or the project-wide one**, so the job deploys anyway. Step 1 below is therefore the *shape* this landed in, and `Inputs.MountedSecrets` is the plan's name for the input: an implementer follows the branch, not the sketch.
- **`internal/gateway/validateRoutes` checks the upstream *kind*, not its key, and that did not change.** The earlier draft said the route-level message `routes need the anthropic upstream, not %q` "becomes" the missing-key message; it does not, and it must not (R2-7 / finding 11 — this is the claim the review called wrong). `validateRoutes` is about a route naming an upstream the gateway does not have, and #371 keeps it and adds a comment saying so (`internal/gateway/route.go:91-95`: *"routes need the Anthropic upstream's kind, never its key"*). What #371 adds is a **new constant** used by the per-call refusal, `missingAnthropicKey` (`internal/gateway/route.go:91`), emitted from the messages and token-count paths (`internal/gateway/forward.go`, `:16`). Step 4 is restated accordingly.

**The five sites, re-verified:** `internal/infra/spec.go:585-591` (the mount, in the `case "api-key"` branch of `SecretMounts`) **plus `internal/infra/readiness.go`** (the secret-version probe at `:100-158` and the optional-secret drop above — the design's "deploy blocked without a version"), `internal/agent/env.go:91-104` (the ≥4-byte demand for redaction), `internal/runner/gateway.go:74-76` (`up.APIKey = envLookup(r.d.Env, "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY")`), `internal/gateway/gateway.go:254-262` (the `case "anthropic"` startup check that no longer refuses, #371), `internal/gateway/route.go:88-95` (`validateRoutes`, which keeps checking the **kind**, #371).

**Interfaces.** Consumes: the version probe `readiness.go` already runs (`hasVersion`, `internal/infra/readiness.go:100`, and the per-logical-name results it already collects). Produces: `Inputs.MountedSecrets map[string]bool` — logical name → "an enabled version exists", filled by `init` before the spec is built, and read by `SecretMounts`. Nothing else changes shape.

1. `SecretMounts` (`internal/infra/spec.go:585`): the `anthropic-api-key` mount is dropped when no enabled version exists — and **the asking and the mounting are one call**, which is the trap this step has to avoid (finding 11): `SecretMounts`'s own comment says it "is the one place that decides it, for the job's spec **and for the secrets init asks for**" (`internal/infra/spec.go:569-571`), so a mount skipped for a missing version would mean **init never asks for the key at all**, and the version could never come into existence. **#371's shape is the one to follow** (R2-7): `SecretMounts` still returns the mount, so init still prompts for `anthropic-api-key` by logical name, and the drop happens one layer later — `infra.OptionalSecret(logical)` (true only for the Anthropic key) and `dropMount(ws, logical)` — **the same two symbols, at the ranges the bullet above cites (`internal/infra/readiness.go:58-63`, `:65-75`); the earlier draft gave a second, contradictory pair here (`:52-58`, `:60-72`), and since #371's branch is unmerged neither number can be verified against `main`, so the plan keeps one pair and says to read the branch** — which removes the env var, the `SecretIDs` entry and the redaction entry (rebuilding `runner.SecretEnvsVar`) when the version probe found no version in the repository's copy or the project-wide one. That keeps the version-existence fact at **deploy** time, which is what it is a fact about, so flipping the routing switch later never needs Terraform (design §6.1). `Inputs.MountedSecrets` is this plan's name for the same input; an implementer follows the branch. **One boundary the drop must respect, and the condition for it is not `spec.Gateway`** (B5's companion item, from the review): the key is optional *because the gateway holds it*, so `OptionalSecret` may waive the version only when the job will actually run with a gateway. **`agent.Spec.Gateway` (`internal/agent/env.go:45`) cannot answer that at deploy time** — it is a *run-time* value the runner fills in when it starts the gateway (`internal/runner/gateway.go:115`, `r.gwAgent`), and `Readiness` holds an `infra.RepoSpec`, which has no gateway field and never sees one. The deploy-time equivalent is the other two terms of the same predicate, `gatewayOn()` (`internal/runner/gateway.go:46-48`): the workflow's auth is `api-key` (the mount is only built in that branch, `internal/infra/spec.go:585-591`) **and the job's budget mode is not off** — because `gatewayOn()` is `cfg != nil && r.spend.On() && auth ∈ {api-key, vertex}`, and `spend.On()` is the mode. So the drop reads the mode and the auth from the spec it already has, and **it must be conservative in the direction that cannot fail open**: the mode is a launcher-overridable variable (D10, `internal/cli/exec.go:69`) and, from phase 1 on, a policy field (Task 1.3) — an installation's deploy-time mode is not a promise about a run's mode. A job deployed with the mode **off** therefore keeps the blocking finding and the mount, and `--no-build`-style re-deploys do not silently drop a key a later run needs; a job whose mode is on may drop it, and its failure mode (a run whose mode a launcher turned off, with no key in the agent's environment) is the D10 boundary SECURITY.md states in Task 1.5 step 5. A gateway-less `api-key` run needs the key in the agent's own environment, and there a missing version stays a blocking finding — otherwise the deploy succeeds and every run of it fails in the agent's environment instead.
2. The readiness gate (`internal/infra/readiness.go`): a missing `anthropic-api-key` is no longer a *blocking* finding, and the finding is **unconditional at deploy time** — the earlier draft said it should be raised "when the run's pins name no direct Claude model", and that cannot be computed (finding 11): the spec sees only the checked-in agent block, and the models a run uses come from the branch's `fugaro.yaml` and a task's `--model` override, neither known when the job is deployed (`internal/infra/spec.go:577-583`). So the finding is a **warning** whenever the key has no version, and its text names both cases it would break — `routing.claude`, or a direct Claude pin — so an owner routing everything through OpenRouter is not nagged by anything blocking, and the wording, not a guess about the pins, tells them what to check.
3. `internal/agent/env.go:88-105`: **both branches need the rule, and the earlier draft only had one (finding 11).** *Gateway present* (`:96-105`): the ≥4-byte demand applies only when the gateway holds a key — with the key absent the agent's environment is built without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and no error, since the gateway is the only holder and its per-call refusal is what names the fix. *Gateway absent* (`:92-93`): a direct run genuinely needs the key in the agent's own environment, so `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` stays in `secretNames` and **its absence is still an error** — this is the case the gateway-off path takes, and the error text names the fix rather than the variable. A direct Claude call with no key therefore fails on both paths, at the first call with the gateway and at environment build without it.
4. `internal/gateway/gateway.go:254-262`: the Anthropic upstream becomes optional — the `case "anthropic"` startup check stops refusing, and the comment says the key is a fact about data rather than about routing (this is #371's change, verbatim). **A direct Claude call with no key is refused with a clear message naming the fix**, and the refusal is a *per-call* error, not a startup error: a new `missingAnthropicKey` constant (`internal/gateway/route.go:91`), emitted from the messages and token-count paths (`internal/gateway/forward.go`). **`validateRoutes` keeps checking the upstream's kind and its message `routes need the anthropic upstream, not %q` stays as it is** — the earlier draft rewrote that message into the missing-key one, which is wrong: a route naming an upstream the gateway does not have is a different failure, and the kind check is what a routed run relies on (finding 11, R2-7). **The refusal must not become a silent route change**: a direct-Claude call with no key never falls through to a provider (that is phase 1b's opt-in and D1b's `direct` default).
5. `docs/multi-model.md`: the placeholder-key paragraph goes, and the "a key is needed only for the models that use it" rule is stated once.

**Tests:** `TestSecretMountsOmitsAbsentAnthropicKey` and `TestSecretMountsMountsPresentAnthropicKey` (`internal/infra`, table-driven over `MountedSecrets`); `TestApiKeyRunWithoutAnthropicKeyStarts` (`internal/agent`); `TestGatewayStartsWithoutAnthropicUpstream` and `TestDirectClaudeCallWithoutKeyIsRefusedByName` (`internal/gateway`); `TestReadinessWarnsInsteadOfBlocking` (`internal/infra`); `TestRunnerStartsWithNoAnthropicKeyWhenPinsAreRouted` (`internal/runner`).
