The last one is the end-to-end assertion: a run whose pins all name provider models reaches the agent, and one whose pins name `claude-*` fails at the first call with the message above.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/infra/... ./internal/agent/... ./internal/gateway/... ./internal/runner/... -count=1`.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestSecretMountsOmitsAbsentAnthropicKey`, `TestSecretMountsMountsPresentAnthropicKey`, `TestApiKeyRunWithoutAnthropicKeyStarts` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

**Commit:** one commit — "keys: neither the Anthropic key nor a provider key is mandatory".

### Task 0.3: the global per-run cap is the fallback (D6) — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**In flight: #373 implements this whole task, and it diverges from this task's spelling in one place — the plan now describes what landed** (R2-7, GROUP C). #373's six commits are D6's six steps: the model (`internal/budget/model.go`), the evaluator (`internal/budget/{evaluate.go,session.go}`), the rules (`internal/budget/rules/{rules.go,rules.json.tmpl}`, golden), the tests (the emulator suite with tag `firebase`, a Go mirror in `rules_test.go`), `fugaro budget show` (`internal/cli/{budget.go,budget_preview.go}`) and the m9 document. **The reviewer of #373 should read it against this task, and this task's step 2 is the one that changed**: #373 does **not** reuse `REPOPERRUN`/`GLOBALPERRUN` with an `||` — it replaces both with **one macro, `PERRUNOK`**, built by `perRunOK(depth)` (`internal/budget/rules/rules.go:346-375`), a single nested ternary whose semantics are exactly D6's: the repository's side is its own node else the default, the global cap bounds the run when it is set, a side that is not set does not bound the run, and with **none** of the three set the expression is `false` and the rules deny. Deleting the two macros is safe because `reserved`'s validator was their only caller (`rules.json.tmpl:28`), which is also why the template's whole diff is one line. The plan adopts that shape rather than fighting it: **one macro, one boolean, one place to test**.

**Files:** `internal/budget/model.go` (+ `model_test.go`), `internal/budget/{evaluate.go,session.go}` (+ their tests, D6's refusal sites, #373 step 2), `internal/budget/rules/rules.go`, `internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl`, `internal/budget/rules/testdata/rules.golden.json`, `internal/budget/rules/{parity_test.go,rules_test.go}`, `internal/budget/rules/emulator_test.go` (tag `firebase`), `internal/cli/{budget.go,budget_preview.go}` (+ tests, `budget show`), `docs/design/m9-budget-and-dashboard.md`, `docs/gcp-setup.md` (the owner's deploy order, #373's last commit).

**Verified today, on `main` — and #373 is the branch that changes every one of these** (R2-7): `internal/budget/model.go:209-224` — `PerRun` returns `ok == false` when the repository and the defaults have no per-run cap, and its comment says *"ok is false when either side is unset, because the rules deny when either comparison reads null"* (**on #373 it returns the global cap instead, and `RepoPerRun`/`GlobalPerRun` are split out as separate accessors**, `internal/budget/model.go:207-248`). `internal/budget/rules/rules.go:310-314` (`REPOPERRUN`, `GLOBALPERRUN` macros — **deleted on #373, replaced by `PERRUNOK`**) and `:343-346` (`fallback(own, def)` returns null when both are absent — **unchanged**). `internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:28` (the `reserved` validator comparing against both macros — the only site that does; `:32` is `released` and `:36` is `spent`, both comparing against `reserved`). `docs/design/m9-budget-and-dashboard.md:272` says the global cap *is* the default for every repository — which is what the code and rules do not do, and D6 makes it true.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: nothing new. Produces: `Caps.PerRun()`'s new contract (the global cap substitutes for an absent repository/default cap) and the same substitution inside the rules' `fallback()`.

1. `internal/budget/model.go`: `PerRun()` returns `(*c.Global.PerRunMicros, true)` when neither the repository's own cap nor the defaults set one, and keeps `min(repo, *c.Global.PerRunMicros)` when they do. Rewrite the comment: it now says the global cap is the fallback by owner decision D6 and that the rules mirror it. **A repository cap and the defaults still only tighten** — the global cap is a floor of *applicability*, never a loosening: an absent global cap stays `ok == false`.
2. **The rules half, as #373 landed it** (`internal/budget/rules/rules.go:346-375`): a new macro `PERRUNOK`, built by `perRunOK(depth)`, **replaces** `REPOPERRUN` and `GLOBALPERRUN` — whose only caller was `reserved`'s validator. The expression, in the shape that shipped:

```
(own.exists() || def.exists()
  ? (own.exists() ? delta <= own.val() : delta <= def.val()) && (global.exists() ? delta <= global.val() : true)
  : (global.exists() ? delta <= global.val() : false))
```

   `delta` is the run's lifetime outstanding after this write, written out rather than as the `NEW` macro so expansion stays a single pass (`:364-367`). Three properties this pins, each of which is D6: a repository with its own cap or a default is bounded by it **and** by the global cap when one is set (a global cap can only tighten); a repository with neither is bounded by the global cap alone (**the fallback**, the D6 direction); and with none of the three set the expression is `false`, so the rules deny (**fail closed**). **The earlier draft's justification for its `||` spelling was wrong on two counts** and both are recorded here: it claimed "the rules language has no ternary" when `fallback` (`:341-344`) and `val` (`:244`) already emit `? :` — and its `X <= REPOPERRUN || (REPOPERRUN == null && X <= GLOBALPERRUN)` spelling cannot express "the default is the fallback of the global one", which is the case a project with a global cap and a *default but no repository node* needs. #373's nested ternary does, which is the version of D6 the rules language actually wants. Steps 4 to 6 are what keep the two sides honest; the emulator, not the spelling, is the decider — and **if the emulator shows a missing node reading anything but false against a comparison, the task stops and reports** rather than shipping a rule that swallows the case.
3. `internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:28`: the `reserved` validator now names `PERRUNOK` instead of the two macros — **one line, which is the whole template diff** (#373). Regenerate `testdata/rules.golden.json` (`rules_test.go:15`) in the same commit. `:32` (`released`) and `:36` (`spent`) compare against `reserved`'s own balance and need no change.
4. `internal/budget/rules/parity_test.go`: **`TestRulesParity` (`:175`) is a randomized generator, not a case table — "add the case" means teaching the generator (finding 8).** `genCase` (`:109`) must produce the worlds D6 is about: a project with a **global per-run cap and no per-run cap on the repository and none in the defaults**, so the two deciders are compared on the fallback itself. `TestRulesParity` compares `Evaluate` against the deployed rules (not `PerRun`), so the assertion is the whole evaluate-vs-rules agreement on those worlds, which is what makes the fallback safe: `Evaluate` says allow at exactly the global cap and deny one micro more, and the rules must say the same. A case pinned by hand would still be worth having, but it is not what this test is.
5. `internal/budget/rules/emulator_test.go` (tag `firebase`): **`TestNullCapDenies` (`:665`) is the test that currently pins the opposite direction and it must change with D6 (finding 8)** — its "no repository per-run cap" sub-case asserts a deny, and under D6, with a global per-run cap set, that sub-case must flip to allow. Its "no defaults node" sub-case still denies, but it denies **through the daily cap**, so it needs a per-run-only variant that shows a missing defaults node with a global per-run cap present is allowed. Its "a zero global per-run cap" sub-case stays a deny — a zero cap is a cap of zero, not an absent cap (finding 10). Then add the new sub-case the fallback itself needs: a run token writes `reserved` for a slug with no `config/caps/repos/<slug>` and no defaults, and the write succeeds when `global/perRunMicros` is set — the fail-open direction, exercised against the real rules. **#373 lands this plus a Go mirror of the same table in `rules_test.go`** (`internal/budget/rules/rules_test.go`, +52 lines), so the expression is asserted both ways round: the emulator is the real decider and the Go mirror is what runs without Java/Node.
6. **The two message sites D6 makes wrong (finding 8).** `noCap` (`internal/budget/evaluate.go:244-249`) builds its refusal from `repoSide`, naming the repository and the defaults as what is missing; after D6 `PerRun()` fails **only when the global cap is absent**, so the message must name the global cap as the missing one whenever the repository and the defaults were both unset. The same correction goes to `noCapDetail` (`internal/runner/gateway.go:425-433`, used at `:326`), which today tells the user to "run `fugaro init --repo`" — after D6 the honest first fix, and under a published policy the only one, is `fugaro project policy publish` (Task 1.6), with `fugaro init --repo` named only as the legacy path (**finding 23**). Both sites are in this task's Files list, and neither is optional: a refusal that names the wrong missing cap sends the owner to the wrong screen.
7. **`fugaro budget show` must show the fallback, and its source (finding 8; D6 step 5).** `internal/cli/budget_preview.go` and `budget.go` render a repository's effective per-run cap — today from the repository node and the defaults — and Check 48 step 5 reads that output at the dashboard to confirm the fallback is in force. Without this step the plan asks the owner to observe something no command prints. The line must name the cap **and where it came from** (the repository, the defaults, or the global fallback), because "the global cap is the ceiling here" and "the repository has its own smaller cap" are different facts about the same number, and Check 48 step 5 cannot tell them apart from the amount alone. #373's `budget show` change is this step (+91 lines of tests, which the Tests paragraph below adopts).
8. `docs/design/m9-budget-and-dashboard.md:272` and its §6.4: the document now says what the code does — **#373 rewrites it, and `docs/gcp-setup.md` with it**, adding the owner's rules deploy order (`init --firebase <fp> --print-vars` read-only, then `init --firebase <fp>`, then a read-only `curl` of `/.settings/rules.json`), what the deploy widens, and the warning to upgrade the CLI first. Add one sentence naming D6 and the date, so the change of direction is recorded in the document that was wrong (it is there in #373).

**Tests:** the ones the steps above name (`genCase`'s fallback worlds in `TestRulesParity`, the reworked `TestNullCapDenies`, the message-site tests in `internal/budget` and `internal/runner`, and the `budget show` cases), plus `TestPerRunNoGlobalCapStillUnset` (an absent global cap, with no repository cap and no default, is still `ok == false`, so this cannot become "no cap at all"), `TestPerRunRepoCapStillTightens` (a repository cap below the global one still wins), and — #373's own, which this list adopts — the `budget show` cases (`internal/cli/budget_test.go`, +91 lines: the effective per-run cap **and its source**, which is the D6 step 5 the plan's Check 48 step 5 reads at the dashboard).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/budget/... -count=1`, then the emulator: `go test -tags firebase ./internal/budget/rules/... -count=1` (CONTRIBUTING.md:21; it *fails* rather than skips when the emulator is missing, so a task without Java/Node must run it in CI before merging — and this task does not merge without it).

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestRulesParity`, `TestNullCapDenies`, `TestPerRunNoGlobalCapStillUnset` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

**Commit:** one commit — "budget: the global per-run cap is the fallback (D6), in the rules and the code". Nothing else rides along: the rules template, the golden file, the code and the m9 document are one reviewable change (P1).

### Task 0.4: Check 48, and the rules deploy — OWNER-RUN, no agent performs any step

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`.

The design's phase 0 ends with an owner-run rules deploy (§2.5, D6). **An agent never deploys rules**: the security rules of a live database are the owner's to publish, and the deploy here changes what a run token may write.

Append `## Check 48: the global per-run cap as the fallback, and the rules deploy it needs (installation fugaro only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` plus its row in the summary table, with exactly these steps in this order:

1. **Setup, hard-coded and guarded — and this is the block every check 49 to 56 and 61 copies verbatim** (R2-6; round-1 findings 30 and 31). The earlier draft's "the two-line guard of Check 48" was weaker than what Check 43 already does, and it is now **Check 43's own block**, three hard stops, spelled once here and required *word for word* in every later check — not "guarded like Check 48". The shape, from `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md:1158-1187` on `main`:

```bash
# === Check NN: setup + guard. RE-RUN THE WHOLE BLOCK at the start of every
# step: it is idempotent, and nothing below the three hard stops runs. ===
P=fugaro-dev                 # HARD-CODED, never `gcloud config get-value project`
L=<the installation's region>
# ${VAR:?} everywhere: an empty variable is a hard error, never an empty
# argument gcloud resolves from its ambient configuration.
[ "$P" = fugaro-dev ] && [ -n "$L" ] \
  || { echo "Check NN stopped: re-run the whole setup block (P=$P must be fugaro-dev; L=$L not empty)" >&2; exit 1; }
fugaro doctor --project fugaro --all | grep -q '^project: .*(GCP fugaro-dev,' \
  || { echo "Check NN stopped: this is not the fugaro-dev installation" >&2; exit 1; }
git remote get-url origin | grep -Eq '[:/]edgeappinc/fugarosandbox(\.git)?$' \
  || { echo "Check NN stopped: this checkout's origin is not the sandbox" >&2; exit 1; }
```

   The three stops, and why each is an assertion rather than a print: **(1)** `P` is *exactly* `fugaro-dev` and every variable the check uses is non-empty — a skipped, partially pasted or hand-edited block stops there instead of reaching `gcloud` with an empty argument, which is the N1 trap (`gcloud` then falls back to the owner's ambient `*-prod` project); **(2)** `fugaro doctor --project fugaro` reports that project — the second stop is also the fourth, because `--project` disagreeing with the checkout's `project:` is **refused** (`internal/localcfg/select.go:163`), and a checkout that is not the sandbox cannot pass it; **(3)** the checkout's origin is `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`, never EdgeWeb's or EdgeServer's. **Then**: every `fugaro` command that takes the flag gets `--project fugaro` (the installation's *name*; the project is `fugaro-dev`) and every `gcloud` command gets `--project "${P:?}"` (plus `${L:?}`/`${R:?}` where used). `fugaro version` takes no flags and is the one exception. **Re-run the whole block from a fresh shell at the start of every step** — the later steps are minutes or days apart, and the variables are gone by then. Paste it into a file and run it with `bash`, not an interactive `zsh` (the checklist's own note, `:1190`): an interactive zsh does not honour the `#` comments unless `setopt interactive_comments` is set. **The `belong` stop is the point of stops 2 and 3 together**: this plan touches only installation `fugaro` and the sandbox checkout, `belong` holds edgeserver and edgeweb, **no agent runs any live step at all**, and the migration of `belong` is the owner's own work after the model is proven (Task 4.5, and the note in Global Constraints). **Record the state to restore**: `fugaro budget show --json` (the caps, defaults and mode), and the deployed rules' byte count from `init --firebase`'s own report.
2. **Owner-run deploy.** `fugaro init --firebase` on installation `fugaro` — the deploy of the generated rules (`internal/infra/firebasedb.go:206-219`, `PutRules`), the same step M9b's design names at `docs/design/m9-budget-and-dashboard.md:702`. **FACT:** the reported rules byte count, and that `init` read the deployed rules back (`internal/infra/firebasedb.go:215`).
3. **The new fallback, live.** With a **sandbox repository slug** that has no `config/caps/repos/<slug>` node and no `config/caps/defaults`: `fugaro budget set --global --per-run 2` (a real write), then one small sandbox run. **FACT:** the run's `policy` log line and `result.json`'s effective per-run cap, and that the run reserves rather than halting with `ReasonNoCap`. This is the mutation `TestRulesParity` cannot see: the emulator is a fake database.
4. **The refusal still refuses.** Remove the global caps with **`fugaro budget set --global --clear`** — **not** `--per-run 0`, which the earlier draft wrote and which does the opposite of what the step wants (finding 10, stated the same way as the Review notes): `--per-run` is a `Float64Var` defaulting to `0` (`internal/cli/budget.go:691`) and the amount is read with `flags.Changed(name)` inside `amount` (`:730` — the call, not `:721` `case o.global:` or `:731` `return nil, nil`; the daily amount is taken at `:740` and the per-run amount at `:743`), so `--global --per-run 0` **is** accepted and sets a cap of **zero** — not "not given", and no "nothing to set" refusal. A zero cap is a cap of zero: it denies every reserve, which would make this step pass for the wrong reason (the rules deny because the cap is zero, not because there is no cap, and the fallback under test is never exercised). `--clear` is the flag that removes a scope's caps (`:694` — `:692` is `--max-reserve`), and it drops the scope whole, so the restore is **step 1's recording** and never step 3's value (round-6 correction: step 3 sets `--global --per-run 2` and **no `--daily` at all**, so there is no `--daily` value of step 3's to re-set — step 6 puts back the caps from `fugaro budget show --json` as step 1 recorded them). Then run the same sandbox task once more. **FACT:** the halt message verbatim, and it must be **the global cap's message, not the repository one** — after D6 (`Task 0.3 step 6`) `PerRun()` fails only when the **global** cap is absent, so `noCap` (`internal/budget/evaluate.go:244-249`) must reach its `ScopeGlobal` branch and print exactly `no global per-run cap is set (fugaro budget set --global --per-run)`. Two things the FACT line therefore settles, and the earlier draft got both wrong:
   - **The flag it names is `--global --per-run`, not `--defaults --per-run`.** Both halves of `--defaults --per-run` are real flags — `--defaults` is the scope switch (`internal/cli/budget.go:688`) and `--per-run` is the amount (`:691`) — so that combination *parses*; the earlier draft's claim that `--defaults --per-run` "is not a flag at all" was wrong, and so was its replacement name (`--repo-per-run`, which no `budget set` anywhere registers). What makes `--defaults --per-run` the wrong message is the **scope**: it selects the defaults scope (`:724`), while the cap this step removed and the message is about is the **global** one. (The strings `--repo-daily` and `--repo-per-run` the shipped messages name today — `internal/budget/evaluate.go:190`,`:247`, `internal/budget/session.go:501`,`:511` — are not flags at all, and removing them is Task 0.1's whole change; naming one here would reintroduce exactly that class of bug and fail Task 0.1's own `TestRefusalMessagesNameRealFlags`.) A message that sends the owner to the defaults screen when the global cap is the missing one is the failure Task 0.3 step 6 exists to prevent.
   - **Which of the two refusal sites spoke is recorded**: the RTDB rules' deny (surfaced by the session as `Halt{Reason: ReasonNoCap}`, `internal/budget/session.go:488-504`) or the runner's own gateway refusal `noCapDetail` (`internal/runner/gateway.go:425-433`, used at `:326`) — the second is the one that also names the **fix**, and in phase 0 that fix is `budget.per_run_usd` in the project config / `fugaro init --repo`, while under a published policy (phase 1 on) it is `fugaro project policy publish` (finding 23, Task 0.3 step 6). This check runs in phase 0, so the expected runner-side text is the legacy one; the FACT line says which text appeared.
   Either way it must be a *refusal*, not a silent no-cap run. A run that succeeds here is the fail-open direction going too far and the deploy is rolled back (step 6).
5. **The dashboard and `watch`.** `fugaro budget show` and `fugaro watch --json` on the same project. **FACT:** the per-run line for a repository that has only the global cap — it must show the global value as effective, not `—`.
6. **Restore.** Put back the caps step 1 recorded (`fugaro budget set` with the recorded values), confirm `fugaro budget show --json` matches step 1's output, and run one ordinary sandbox task to confirm the sandbox still runs.

End with: "**Paste back:** every FACT line above, never a secret value and never a key value. Never run this against `belong`." **If the rules cannot express the existence-guarded fallback (Task 0.3 step 2), this check is not written and phase 0 stops**: the owner is told, and D6 is re-decided rather than half-shipped.

**Tests:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1`.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck48`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 48` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck48`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

**Depends on:** Task 0.3 (its own change is what this check deploys). Nothing else in the plan reads it.

### Task 0.5: the design document carries the nine amendments this plan makes — docs only

**Files:** `docs/design/project-centric.md` (the nine amendments listed under **Design amendments** above), `internal/cli/docs_design_amendments_test.go` (new).

**Why this is a task and not a paragraph.** The **Design amendments** section above lists nine places where the plan **changes** the design, and it said "so the owner's assistant can apply them to the design in one pass" — which named no task, no commit and no test. That is the gap the review found: eight of the nine amendments had an implementing task (`Task 3.2 step 2` and `3b.1` for D13, `4.2` for D8, `1.1`/`1.2`/`1.6` for the marker, `3.1`/`3.2` for the profile's provider, `3.3` for the provider-in-the-job-name rule, `4.4`/`4.5` for D3 and `belong`, and `3.0`/`3.5`/`3.7` for the D5 ruling the owner made on 2026-10-15), but **nothing edited the document**, so the design would still say what the plan had corrected — and the first amendment, the **D13 row in §12's decisions table**, is the one the owner ruled on 2026-10-15 and whose absence is most visible.

It lands in **phase 0, on `main`**, with the rest of the docs-only work: the design is `main`'s document, the amendments describe what phases 1 to 6 (on `next`) will do, and an owner reading `main` must not find a design that contradicts the plan that implements it. It touches **no code and no Terraform**, and it depends on nothing.

1. **§12's decisions table gains D13**, next to D4: the date **2026-10-15**, the finding (the broadened `runs/` grant also exposes `runs/<slug>/<run>/budget-token`, an RTDB identity), the ruling (accept; document in SECURITY.md beside D4's cache- and lock-poisoning regression; design a token channel in phase 3b; separate profiles meanwhile), and a pointer to Task 3b.1 as the design work and to `SECURITY.md` for the paragraph. **D4's own paragraph gains the sentence that the token path is inside the grant**, and **§4.1's table gains the row that a profile is the isolation boundary for budget identity too** — the three edits are one amendment and are made together.
2. **D8's row becomes "superseded: the full plan ships"** (R2): the config-transitions plan's Tasks 1 to 9 ship on `main` in 0.7.5, its core is #365, and phase 4 builds on the whole engine including that plan's own Task 5 `--retire` — so the design's "core" is deleted rather than caveated.
3. **§5 and §5(3) restate project-centric mode** (R3): the rules-version marker `config/limits/policyRules` at `RulesVersion` **and** `config/policy/shared`, and a run with no budget credential **on a budget-off job** takes the mode-off path whose refusal is the launcher's readiness gate — **a budget-on job in the same state halts** (B1, `internal/runner/budget_session.go:174-175`,`:186-187`), which the design must say too, or it restates the fail-open.
4. **§4.1 and §4.1(1) gain the profile's provider** (R2-11): one provider per profile, and the job set is one job per **(profile, provider, account)**.
5. **§4.1(3) and §12a item 2 gain the provider-is-not-an-env-var rule**, and §4.1(1)'s "which the runner already reads" is corrected: the runner reads `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` through `backend.ExecutionFromEnv`, not directly.
6. **§4.1 and §4.4 gain the legacy-job carve-out**: admission and the profile halt apply to project jobs only, a legacy job keeps the phase-1 contract, and §4.1 gains the name spelling and the compute-and-match rule (never a parse).
7. **§5, §11 and §12a item 6 gain the rules-version marker**: `config/limits/policyRules` written after `PutRules` succeeds, "a published policy implies the rules are deployed", the `policySince` launch gate (Task 1.5 step 4), and the halt message for an older CLI's `init --firebase`.
8. **§12 D3, §4.4 and §11 phase 4 gain the `belong` note** (D3's drop is for new onboarding; the owner migrates `belong` after the model is proven; no agent ever touches it) and the release plan (**phase 0 on `main` in 0.7.5's line; phases 1, 1b and 2 as 0.8.0; phases 3 to 6 as 0.9.0 with the compute backend** — R4 as amended by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15, R6). **The design carries no release statement anywhere today** (checked 2026-10-15 against `origin/next`), so this amendment is what writes the split into it, beside §11's phase list and D2's and D3's rows.
9. **D5 is amended by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15: Bitbucket is not first-class in phase 3** (§12 D5, §2, §4.5, §11's phase-3 live check, §13; Tasks 3.0 step 2, 3.5 step 3, 3.7). The design's D5 row reads *"Bitbucket — **First-class in phase 3**, via a project-wide Bitbucket access token after a verification task (§4.5)"*, and §4.5 carries the design's own branch for the other outcome: *"If the verification fails, Bitbucket projects stay on the per-repository path and the owner is told before phase 3 starts."* **The owner ruled the verification on 2026-10-15 — "cannot be scoped": a Bitbucket credential cannot be scoped to one project's repositories** — so the design gains, in the ruling's own terms: **D5's row becomes "Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path in phase 3: no Bitbucket credential can be scoped to a project's repositories (owner ruling 2026-10-15), and the project-wide token this decision assumed does not exist"**; **§4.5's paragraph states the ruling** instead of leaving the scoping UNVERIFIED, and says that the shared jobs phase 3 builds serve GitHub repositories; **§13's entry for the Bitbucket token scope is marked settled by that ruling**, not UNVERIFIED; **§2's "Bitbucket is first-class in phase 3 (D5)" sentence is corrected the same way**; and **§11's phase-3 live-check sentence ("Live check on the Bitbucket sandbox, plus a GitHub repository for the GitHub-specific parts") is corrected to the GitHub path** — there is no shared Bitbucket job to check, and the Bitbucket sandbox keeps its per-repository job and its own token. The runtime-fetch fallback §4.5 names stays UNVERIFIED-and-unadopted: it reintroduces §4.1's shared-service-account exposure, and Check 52's U3 (Task 3.0 step 4) says only whether it is even available. **The plan no longer treats D5 as open anywhere** — Task 3.0 step 2, the §13 map and "Accepted as deferred" all read the ruling.
   Each amendment is written **verbatim from the list above**, which is the authority: a sentence the list does not contain is not added, and a sentence it contains is not softened. The amendments section itself gains a line saying it is applied by this task, so a reader of the plan knows which document to change and when. **The decisions table is the landing place, not only the prose** (M1): §12's table is edited *as a table* — **D13's new row**, **D8's row replaced by "superseded: the full plan ships"**, and D2's and D3's release and `belong` notes attached to their existing rows (item 8) — so an owner who reads only the table sees the plan's changes there, and the test asserts the two row strings, not only the paragraphs around them.

**Tests:** `TestDesignCarriesThePlanAmendments` (new, `internal/cli/docs_design_amendments_test.go`, in the `docs_*_test.go` convention): it reads `docs/design/project-centric.md` and asserts that each of the nine amendments' load-bearing strings is present — the D13 row and its `2026-10-15` date in §12's table, `superseded: the full plan ships` on D8's row, **D5's ruling on its row and in §13 (`cannot be scoped`; `stays on the per-repository path`) and §11's corrected live-check sentence**, the marker's path beside the mode definition, `(profile, provider, account)` in §4.1, the "never an environment variable" rule for the provider, the legacy-job carve-out, the `policySince` gate, and `belong`'s "no agent ever touches it" sentence. It is a **presence** test, not a fidelity test, for the same reason the price-catalog and project-secrets plans' docs tests are: prose cannot be diffed by a Go assertion, and a reviewer reads the diff.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1`.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the design text, and its red state is a docs test: write `TestDesignCarriesThePlanAmendments` first, before editing the design. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`, on the D13 row and the D8 sentence at the very least, which is the substance of the amendment rather than a missing symbol. Then apply the nine amendments and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. No line of this task runs live and no agent touches a cloud resource: it edits one Markdown file and adds one test.

**Depends on:** nothing. **Commit:** "design: the nine amendments the plan makes, and the D13 and D5 rows the owner ruled".

## Phase 1 — policy as data (seven tasks; release **0.8.0**, R6)

The design's §5 and §4.3. **What this phase does and does not remove** (design §11): a change to a policy **value** (mode, per-run ceiling, allowed models, prices, `allow_repos`, and 1b's routing) stops needing `init --repo`; a change to what a job **mounts** (a secret, a provider key, a named account, a launcher or operator grant) still needs per-repository Terraform until phase 3. An installation that never publishes a policy is untouched, which is what makes this phase mergeable alone.

### Task 1.1: the policy object, its paths and its version

**Files:** `internal/projectpolicy/{policy.go,paths.go,match.go,version.go}` (new), `internal/projectpolicy/*_test.go` (new). Nothing reads it yet — this task is dead code plus tests, which is deliberate: it is the shape a reviewer vetoes cheapest before Task 1.2 changes the rules.

**In flight: #372 implements this task (and Task 1.2) on `next`, and it has no rules marker** (R2-7, GROUP C). #372 ships `internal/projectpolicy` with the policy object, the paths, `MatchRepo` and the version, and it **exports `task.ValidProvider`** (`internal/task/task.go:169`) — which is exactly what step 3 below needs instead of the unexported `providerRE`, so the citation here is #372's, not a plan sketch. What it does **not** have is `PathRulesMarker`, `RulesVersion` or the `init --firebase` write, and **Task 1.3's fail-closed rule depends on all three** (it gates the whole "is this installation's rules deployed" question on the marker). **Where the marker lands: as a follow-up commit on #372, or as a `Task 1.2a` that merges before group E** — either is acceptable and the plan does not care which, but an implementer of Task 1.3 who finds the symbol missing **must stop and report under this plan's own rule** rather than inventing a marker path. The rule for the reviewer: **#372 is not "Task 1.1 + Task 1.2" until the marker's symbol and its write exist**; until then it is those two tasks minus the safety mechanism that makes Task 1.3's halt correct. Both files this affects are named in Task 1.2's own list (`internal/projectpolicy/paths.go` here, `internal/infra/firebasedb.go` there).

**Interfaces.** Produces (consumed by Tasks 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.6, 1b.1, 3.1, 5.1, 6.1):

```go
package projectpolicy // imports internal/rtdb and internal/task only: never internal/budget, internal/runner or internal/cli

const Version = 1

// Paths (P2; the caps' own helpers are internal/budget/model.go:44-80 and stay there)
func PathShared() string            { return "config/policy/shared" }
func PathRepo(slug string) string   { return "config/policy/repos/" + seg(slug) } // the $slug node
func PathAdmin() string             { return "config/admin" }

// PathRulesMarker is the rules-deployment marker. fugaro init --firebase
// writes it AFTER PutRules returns, never before it and never beside the
// other node writes — the rules are the deploy's last action
// (internal/infra/firebasedb.go:206-221), so a marker written earlier
// would survive a failed rules deploy and halt every budget-on run. It is
// readable by a run token under BOTH the old rules and the new ones, because it lives under
// config/limits — whose TOKEN read the old template already grants
// (internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:15). It is what lets the runner
// tell "the node is absent" (legacy path) from "the rules deny me" (halt)
// before the owner has deployed phase 1's rules: see Task 1.2 step 4 and
// Task 1.3 step 1. RulesVersion is the value init writes.
func PathRulesMarker() string { return "config/limits/policyRules" }

const RulesVersion = 1

// Policy is the readable node, decoded tolerantly: an unknown key is ignored
// (so a newer policy does not break an older runner on a field it does not
// know) and Version is the only thing that can refuse it (P8). Every field
// carries its json tag, and `budget` is all-or-nothing: see Decode.
type Policy struct {
	Version   int       `json:"version"`
	Budget    Budget    `json:"budget"`
	AllowRepos []string `json:"allow_repos"`
	Providers map[string]Provider `json:"providers,omitempty"`
	Prices    map[string]Price    `json:"prices,omitempty"`   // model_prices, the owner's overrides
	Routing   *Routing  `json:"routing,omitempty"`            // phase 1b writes it; nil = direct (D1b)
}
type Budget struct {
	Mode          string   `json:"mode"`
	PerRunUSD     float64  `json:"per_run_usd"`
	AllowedModels []string `json:"allowed_models"`
	MaxRunTokens  int64    `json:"max_run_tokens"`
}

// MatchRepo matches a canonical "<provider>/<owner>/<name>" against an
// allow-list pattern (P5). One implementation, used by the runner and the CLI.
func MatchRepo(pattern, canonical string) bool
func Canonical(provider, repo string) (string, error)

// Decode returns (p, found, err): found=false is the absent node (today's
// behaviour), err is a node that exists and cannot be used (fail closed).
func Decode(raw []byte) (*Policy, bool, error)
```

1. `paths.go`: the three path helpers plus `PathRulesMarker`/`RulesVersion`, with `seg`-style escaping copied from `internal/budget/model.go:44` (a slug is escaped the same way `/config/caps/repos/$slug` escapes it, so the two agree). The marker's path must stay under `config/limits`: **it is the one node a run token can read under the rules that predate this phase**, which is the whole point of it (Task 1.2 step 4).
2. `policy.go`: the type above plus `Decode`, which returns `found=false` for an empty body and an error for a body that does not parse, carries a non-integer or unknown `version`, or whose `allow_repos` holds a pattern `Canonical`/`MatchRepo` rejects. **Unknown JSON keys are ignored** (unlike the version-gated env vars the price catalog's P4 describes): a policy field a newer CLI writes must not brick an older runner mid-flight, and `version` is the one thing that may. **That tolerance is bounded by two rules that keep a typo from becoming a silent zero ceiling**: every field of `Policy` and `Budget` carries its explicit `json` tag spelled as the documented key (`per_run_usd`, `allowed_models`, `max_run_tokens`, `mode`, `version`, `allow_repos`), so the documented spellings are the only ones that decode; and a `budget` object that **has keys but none of the four documented ones** is an error, not an all-zero `Budget` — the round-2 review caught that the earlier wording ("carries none of its four documented keys") made `"budget": {}` an error, which is the opposite of what the tolerant path must do and what `TestDecodeAcceptsAPartialBudgetAndAnEmptyOne` asserts. The rule is therefore: an **empty** `budget` object is "the policy sets no budget" (all-zero, valid, indistinguishable from the key being absent), and a **non-empty** one that misses all four documented keys is `TestDecodeRefusesABudgetWhoseKeysAllMiss`'s error. Without the first, a hand-authored or cross-version policy written in camelCase decodes to a zero per-run cap and a zero token ceiling — a ceiling that would be enforced; without the second, the same file is indistinguishable from `"budget": {}`, which is what the tolerant path must still read as "the policy sets no budget". A budget that carries *some* of the keys stays valid: absent keys are zero, exactly as `fugaro.yaml`'s own `budget:` block behaves.
3. `match.go`: `Canonical(provider, repo)` produces `"github/acme/app"` from a provider kind and an `owner/name` — by **calling `task.CanonicalRepo` and validating the provider kind with `task.ValidProvider`** (`internal/task/task.go:169`, **exported by #372** — the earlier draft named the unexported `providerRE` at `:179-182`, which this package cannot call; finding 21, R2-7) rather than re-implementing the lower-casing, the `.git` trim or the provider check, so the allow-list and the slug identity agree by construction and there is one spelling of a repository in the codebase (P5). `MatchRepo` matches `*` within one segment only (`github/acme/*` matches `github/acme/app`, not `github/acme/a/b`; `*/acme/*` is legal, a bare `acme/*` is not — P5).
4. `version.go`: `Version`, and the two skew messages as constants (the runner's refusal and the CLI's refusal), so Task 1.3 and Task 1.6 print the same text.

**Tests:** `TestPathsEscapeASlug`; `TestRulesMarkerPathIsUnderConfigLimits` (the marker's path stays readable under the pre-phase-1 template, asserted by reading the template's own tree in the test — if it ever moves out of `config/limits`, the whole safe-ordering argument in 1.2/1.3 breaks and this test says so); `TestDecodeAbsentIsNotFound`; `TestDecodeIgnoresUnknownKeys`; `TestDecodeRefusesBadVersion`; `TestDecodeRefusesBadAllowRepoPattern`; `TestDecodeReadsTheDocumentedSnakeCaseKeys` (the four budget keys as the docs spell them, asserted field by field — the test that would have caught the missing tags); `TestDecodeRefusesABudgetWhoseKeysAllMiss` (`"budget": {"perRunUsd": 5}` is an error, not a zero ceiling) and `TestDecodeAcceptsAPartialBudgetAndAnEmptyOne` (`{"mode":"observe"}` and `{}` are both valid); `TestMatchRepo` (a table: the wildcard cases, the cross-provider case, the two-`fugarosandbox` case of P5); `TestCanonical`; `TestCanonicalAgreesWithTaskSlug` (for a mixed-case `Owner/Name.git`, `Canonical`'s output and the pair `task.Slug` hashes are the same string, and `MatchRepo("github/acme/*", canonical)` admits the repository whose slug is `task.Slug("github", repo)` — the property P5 exists to guarantee).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/projectpolicy/... -count=1`. **Commit:** "projectpolicy: the policy object, its paths, its version and its matcher".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestPathsEscapeASlug`, `TestRulesMarkerPathIsUnderConfigLimits`, `TestDecodeAbsentIsNotFound` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1.2: the read rules — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl`, `internal/budget/rules/rules.go`, `internal/budget/rules/testdata/rules.golden.json`, `internal/budget/rules/{rules_test.go,parity_test.go,emulator_test.go}`, **`internal/infra/firebasedb.go`** (+ its test, step 7 — the marker write lives with the rules deploy).

**Verified today:** the template's `config` block is `internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:9-21` — `caps/global` and `caps/defaults` `.read: "TOKEN"`, `caps/repos/$slug` and `kill/repos/$slug` `.read: "TOKEN && auth.token.fs == $slug"`, and no `.write` anywhere under `config` (the root `.write` is `false`, `:3-4`; admin writes are IAM-only, `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/firebase/iam.tf:52-58`). The `TOKEN` macro is `internal/budget/rules/rules.go:292-293`; the slug claim is `auth.token.fs`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: Task 1.1's paths (as literals in the template, and as a `rules.go` helper that **must agree with them** — the parity test asserts the template's paths equal `projectpolicy.PathShared()` etc., so the two cannot drift). Produces: `TestRulesParity`'s new cases and the generated rules.

1. Add to `rules.json.tmpl`'s `config`, beside `caps`:

```json
"policy": {
  "shared": { ".read": "TOKEN", ".write": false },
  "repos": { "$slug": { ".read": "TOKEN && auth.token.fs == $slug", ".write": false } }
},
"admin": { ".read": false, ".write": false }
```

**Per child, never on `config/policy` itself** (design §5(2)): a `TOKEN` read on the parent cascades to every child and would make `repos/$slug` and the admin node readable by every run token. `config/admin` carries `.read: false` in the *rules*, and **the rules are not what keeps it private**: launchers hold `roles/firebasedatabase.viewer` (`deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/firebase/iam.tf:43-49`) and an IAM read bypasses the rules, so 12a item 7 applies — **no secret-adjacent value in `config/admin`**, ever, and the section says so.
2. **Where the tests go matters here, and the earlier draft got it wrong.** `TestRulesParity` (`internal/budget/rules/parity_test.go:175`) is **not** a case table: it is a randomized Evaluate-against-rules generator behind `//go:build firebase`, and it covers lease and release **writes** only — it cannot carry a read-path case. The read-path rows belong in the emulator's own tables: `TestAllowedPaths` (`internal/budget/rules/emulator_test.go:143`) and `TestDenialTable` (`:338`), under tag `firebase`, asserting that a run token **can** read `config/policy/shared`, **can** read `config/policy/repos/<its own slug>`, **cannot** read another slug's node, **cannot** read `config/policy` (the parent), **cannot** read `config/admin`, and can read the marker `config/limits/policyRules` **under both the old and the new template** (a second table run against the previous golden — the row that keeps the safe ordering true). The non-emulator golden test is `rules_test.go`'s `TestRulesGolden`. **Without `-tags firebase` none of the read-path assertions run**, so the command below says `-tags firebase` explicitly and the task does not merge without it.
3. Regenerate `testdata/rules.golden.json` in this commit, and add the assertion that the template's policy paths equal `projectpolicy.PathShared()`/`PathRepo("x")` — a task renaming a path updates both or fails.
4. **The ordering, and the window it has to close** (this is the finding that shapes the task; the earlier draft of this plan got it wrong and said so). Until the owner deploys these rules, a run token's `GET` of `config/policy/shared` is **permission-denied**, not absent: the template's root is `".read": false` (`internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:3`) and nothing under `config` grants `policy` a read. So a runner that treated "I can't read it" as "the node is absent, carry on" would be defeated by the rules it cannot read, and a runner that treated every non-absent error as a halt (Task 1.3's first draft) would **halt every budget-on run on every installation whose rules had not been redeployed** — including `belong`, whose deploy is the owner's alone and whose lifetime is not this phase's to schedule. A release rolls out everywhere at once; a rules deploy does not. The plan therefore does three things, in this order:
   - **The marker makes the two states distinguishable.** `fugaro init --firebase` writes `config/limits/policyRules = RulesVersion` (Task 1.1) **in the same deploy that puts these rules in place, and only after `PutRules` has succeeded** (`internal/infra/firebasedb.go:206-221`; the rules deploy is the deploy's last act and the marker's write is the act after it — Task 1.2 step 7 states the failure direction). It needs **no new rule**: it sits under `config/limits`, whose TOKEN read the *old* template already grants (`internal/budget/rules/rules.json.tmpl:15`; `:13` is `config/caps/repos/$slug`), so it is readable by a run token before and after this phase's deploy, and unreadable only on an installation that never deployed rules at all. A worker's Task 1.4 test asserts the marker's path stays there.
   - **The reader is gated on the marker, not on the deploy order.** Task 1.3 step 1 reads the marker first: marker absent or lower than `RulesVersion` ⇒ this installation's rules predate this phase ⇒ the policy cannot be readable **and cannot have been published** (step 3 below) ⇒ the run takes the legacy path and a permission-denied read is not an error; marker present ⇒ permission-denied on `config/policy/shared` is a genuine anomaly ⇒ **halt**. That is the fail-closed direction without a window: an old installation runs as today, a new one cannot be silently denied.
   - **`policy publish` refuses to publish before the deploy.** `fugaro project policy publish` (Task 1.6) reads the marker with the operator's IAM credentials — IAM reads bypass the rules — and refuses when it is absent or older than `RulesVersion`, with the fix in the message (`fugaro init --firebase`). This is what makes "a published policy implies the rules are deployed" true **by construction** rather than by luck, and it is why the runner may halt on a denied read at all. The design's order (rules deploy, runner rollout, publish, §5) is per installation; this makes the *release* safe on an installation that has not run any of the three.
   - **The per-installation order is still the owner's** (Task 1.7): rules, then the runner image, then the publish. Nothing in this task or 1.3 requires that order for safety any more, but it is the order that keeps the failure messages good.
5. **D6's smaller window, stated the same way** (Task 0.3): a new runner computes the global fallback while old rules deny the reserve it needs. That is still a **refusal**, not a bypass, so it is safe but noisy — the owner deploys the phase-0 rules **before** the run that computes the fallback — that ordering is Task 0.4's (its step 2 is the deploy, and every run in Check 48 happens after it) and nothing in the plan depends on it beyond the noise: a run against the old rules refuses, it does not bypass.
6. **The run token is not only a reader** (12a item 12 — the design's §4.3 lists what a run token writes, and this change must not disturb it): it writes `runs/$slug/$run/...` (`:22-55`), `spend/$day/global/{counted,spent,notional}` (`:57-70`), `agents/$slug/$run` (`:145-172`) and `outcomes/$day/$slug/$run` (`:175-188`), the outcome node being write-once (`!data.exists()`). The policy edit is **additive and confined to `config`**, and the emulator's read and write tables gain one row per existing write above asserting it is still granted (Task 1.2 step 2's placement note) — so a later edit to the `config` block cannot quietly take away a run's ability to record its own outcome or its own spend. No existing row is renumbered or removed, and `TestWritesOnlyAtNumericLeaves` (`internal/budget/rules/rules_test.go:178`) must accept the new `.write: false` nodes rather than counting them as leaves.
7. `fugaro init --firebase`: write `config/limits/policyRules` (`projectpolicy.PathRulesMarker()`, value `RulesVersion`) **as the action after `PutRules` succeeds** (R2-4). `internal/infra/firebasedb.go:206-221` plans the node writes first and **queues the rules deploy last** — its own comment says so ("The rules: last, so they go live on a database that is marked and configured") — and the acts run in order. The marker is therefore **not** one more `d.put` beside the others and **not** "in the same step as `PutRules`": the natural reading of that earlier wording would leave a renamed, failed or declined rules deploy with the marker already present over the **old** rules, and the runner would then read the marker, be denied `config/policy/shared`, and **halt every budget-on run** on an installation with no policy published — finding 1's failure, reintroduced by the fix for it. The marker is appended to `d.acts` **after** the rules act, and its `do` runs only once `PutRules` has returned nil; on any rules-deploy failure the marker is not written at all. The failure direction is deliberate and stated in the code's comment: **rules without a marker costs a publish (recoverable, safe), a marker without rules costs every run (unrecoverable in the field)**. **In flight (R2-7): #372 ships this task's `config/policy` rules without the marker write, so this step is the follow-up #372 needs** — see the note under Task 1.1 for where it lands (a commit on #372, or a `Task 1.2a` before group E). `:215` is the **pre-deploy read of the live rules** (`d.c.Rules`, feeding the `sameJSON` comparison) — it is not a read-back of what was deployed, and Check 48 step 2 and this step must not describe it as one (deferrable, R2-4). **Tests:** `TestInitFirebaseWritesTheMarkerAfterTheRules` (the acts are ordered: the rules act precedes the marker act), `TestInitFirebaseLeavesNoMarkerWhenTheRulesDeployFails` (a `PutRules` that returns an error ⇒ no marker put, asserted on the planned acts and on the executed calls), and `TestInitFirebaseSkipsTheMarkerWhenTheRulesAreAlreadyRight` — with `sameJSON(live, gen)` true there is no rules act at all, and the marker is written only when it differs from `RulesVersion` (an already-marked installation re-runs `init --firebase` and writes nothing).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/budget/... -count=1` (this runs the golden and unit tests only — **none** of the read-path rows) **and** `go test -tags firebase ./internal/budget/rules/... -count=1` (the emulator, which is where they live; it fails rather than skips without Java/Node, CONTRIBUTING.md:21). **Commit:** "rules: config/policy is readable per child, config/admin is not readable by a run token".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestRulesParity`, `TestAllowedPaths`, `TestDenialTable` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1.3: the runner reads the policy and fails closed — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/runner/projectpolicy.go` (new), `internal/runner/policy.go` (+ `policy_test.go`), `internal/runner/budget_session.go` (the client, the marker read and **the single token take** — finding 2), `internal/cli/launch_budget.go` (+ tests: the launcher's mode decision follows the policy), `internal/infra/spec.go` (the API key, with Task 1.4).

**Verified today:** `internal/runner/policy.go:63-120` — `resolvePolicy` builds the ceiling from `r.d.Spend` (the job env), merges the default branch's file layer and the run's own, and **treats the ceiling as optional** (`:66-72`: `Mode` only when non-empty, `PerRunUSD` only when `> 0`); `:108-113` falls back to `pricing.Embedded()` when the mode is on and no prices were loaded. `internal/runner/budget_session.go:25-52` is where the RTDB URL and the Firebase key come from (`RTDBURLEnv`, `FirebaseAPIKeyEnv` at `:29-31`), and `:128`/`:180` are where the slug keys the session. **Three facts the earlier draft of this task missed, all verified:** the launcher mints and stores a run token only when the *local* budget mode is not off (`internal/cli/launch_budget.go:38-40` — `budgetBackendOn` returns false for `BudgetOff` or no URL, and the mint is at `:174-188`); the token exchange needs `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` (`budget_session.go:66-71`), not just the URL; and `token.TakeObject` **deletes** the token object as it reads it (`internal/budget/token/object.go:76`, and its own comment says a token left behind could be replayed), so a policy read that takes the token and a session that takes it again cannot both succeed.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: `projectpolicy.Decode`/`PathShared`/`PathRulesMarker`/`RulesVersion` (1.1), the RTDB URL **and API key** the job env carries (1.4), the run's own token minted by the launcher (and the one take Task 1.3 shares with the session). Produces: `Spend` and `policy.Effective` computed from the policy instead of the env when the node exists; `runstore.PolicyRecord.Sources` gains `policy` as a source name; and a `bootstrapBudget`-owned token take that both the early read and the later session use.

1. `projectpolicy.go`: `func (r *run) projectPolicy(ctx context.Context) (*projectpolicy.Policy, bool, error)` — **three outcomes, not two**, because the rules that make the node readable may not be deployed yet (Task 1.2 step 4):
   - **Marker absent, or below `projectpolicy.RulesVersion`** ⇒ this installation's rules predate phase 1 ⇒ no policy can have been published here (Task 1.6 refuses to publish without the marker) ⇒ **`found=false`, the legacy path, and a permission-denied or missing `config/policy/shared` read is not an error**. This is what keeps a release from halting `belong`, `fugaro` and every other installation whose rules deploy is the owner's own step.
   - **Marker present and `config/policy/shared` absent (empty body)** ⇒ installed, not adopted ⇒ `found=false`, today's behaviour exactly (P3).
   - **Marker present and the node exists but cannot be read** (permission denied, network, or `Decode`'s error: unparseable, bad `version`, bad pattern) ⇒ **halt** with a message naming the node, the marker and the fix. **The message must name the one cause that is not a tampering case** (R2-8(b), deferrable): an **older CLI's `fugaro init --firebase`** redeploys the pre-phase-1 rules (its `sameJSON` differs from the new template, so it *does* redeploy) and leaves the marker in place, so the policy that was published is now unreadable and every budget-on run halts. The text is `the deployed rules are older than the marker config/limits/policyRules: re-run 'fugaro init --firebase' with fugaro >= <version>` — with the version substituted from `RulesVersion`'s release, and a `docs/project-layer.md` line saying the same, because an owner who reads the halt must be able to fix it without the plan in hand. `fugaro doctor` reports the same skew before a run fails (**Task 1.6 step 4's fifth check**, `policy-rules-skew`, B5), and the `policySince` launch gate refuses the *launch* rather than the run in the **release-skew** case the gate covers (a policy whose `version` is above the binary's — Task 1.5 step 4; the marker's skew is this halt's, not the gate's) — **owned by Task 1.5 step 4** (B5): the gate is a `fugaro run` pre-launch refusal that reads the policy with the operator's credentials, which is exactly what step 4 already does, and the earlier citation of "Task 1.6 step 3" named `policy show`, a read-only printer that refuses nothing. A denied read here is a genuine anomaly: the marker is written by the same deploy that grants the read, so "denied" means the rules were changed out from under a published policy, which is exactly the case that must not fall back to a wider ceiling.
   The client is the one `internal/rtdb` builds from `RTDBURLEnv` + `FirebaseAPIKeyEnv` + the run's token. **The three outcomes above are reachable only when that client exists, and the earlier draft of this step stated a rule that could never fire** (round-2 review, high): the credential the marker read needs is the very credential whose absence the rule was about. `internal/budget/session.go:175-199` is the proof — `token.Exchange` is the first thing `Open` does (`:188`, the exchange at `:66-71` of `budget_session.go` is what needs `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY`) and `rtdb.New` (`:214`, `:50` in the listing) builds the client from the resulting token, so **a run with no URL or no key has no RTDB client and cannot see the marker**; "marker present ⇒ halt" is dead code for exactly the run it was meant to catch. The rule is restated on what the run can observe:
   - **The credential is present and the session opens** ⇒ the marker read above runs in full: marker absent/older ⇒ legacy; marker present + node absent ⇒ today's behaviour; marker present + node exists but denied or unparseable ⇒ **halt**. This is the fail-closed direction, and it is the only one the runner can implement.
   - **The mode-off path is entered by one condition only: the job's own budget mode is off** (B1; `internal/runner/budget_session.go:163-166` — `if !r.spend.On() { return nil }` is the whole of the silent path, and it is decided from the job's environment *before* any credential is consulted). A **missing `FUGARO_RTDB_URL`, a missing `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, or a missing token object does not join it on a budget-on run**, and the earlier draft of this step said they did — a fail-open in the same family as R2-3's. The shipped code stops such a run: with the mode on, a missing key is `the budget backend is configured (FUGARO_RTDB_URL) but FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY is not set` (`:174-175`) and `token.TakeObject` returning `token.ErrNoToken` is `the run's budget token is not in the runs bucket … launch the run again` (`:186-187`), both *after* the mode check at `:164`. Those two are the shipped failure classification this task must not weaken, and the policy read must not reinterpret either as "no policy here". So the absences a *credential-holding* agent can arrange — deleting the key from the job's environment, or consuming the token with a first take — are refusals, not a way to drop to the env ceiling. What genuinely reaches the mode-off path on a budget-on installation is a job **launched** without the backend, and the gate for that is the **launcher**, which holds the operator's credentials and is trusted (design §2.2's threat model: the run is the untrusted party): Task 1.4's readiness gate is **blocking** once `config/policy/shared` exists and refuses the job that cannot read it — in two shapes, because a job's deployment has two: a job that does not exist yet is left undeployed (`Missing{MissingFirebase, Deployed: false}`), and one that already exists, which P7a never un-deploys, **refuses the apply outright** (the review of PR #379 found the earlier wording overstating what P7a allows: with only the first shape, an existing job was deployed anyway and the gate was a warning after the fact) — and Task 1.3 step 2 makes the launcher mint a token whenever a policy exists, mode off included. What the runner must never do — and what the marker still prevents — is treat **a denial it can see** as an absent node. **The corollary for the mode-off job, stated here because the implementation of PR #390 turned on it** (B1/M2, the follow-up fix round): a job whose budget mode is **off** and whose environment carries `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` but **no** `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` takes the mode-off path and not the shipped no-API-key refusal at `:174-175` — with the mode off there is nothing to enforce, the mode-off path is entered by the job's own mode being off, and the launcher mints a token on an off-mode job **only when a policy exists** (step 2), so a missing key there is an environment deployed without the backend, not a published policy hidden behind a credential. The refusal for that job is Task 1.4's readiness gate, exactly as for a job with no URL at all; the same absence on a budget-on job still stops the run on `:174-175`.
   - **An exchange or read failure halts. It is never the mode-off path** (R2-3 — **the earlier draft said the opposite and that was a fail-open**, the round-2 review's high finding). Today a budget-on run whose exchange fails stops: `startBudgetBackend` returns "the budget backend refused the run's identity" (`internal/runner/budget_session.go:205-213`, the `budget.Open` switch) or halts `HaltBudgetTokenExpired`/`HaltBudgetUnavailable` (`:200-207`). Under "the exchange fails ⇒ mode off", a network blip or an expired token during the policy read would drop the run to the env ceiling — possibly mode off, with no gateway and no cap — on an installation that **has published a policy**. That is a regression of a shipped property, not a simplification. The policy read therefore reuses the session's own failure classification, in one place: the marker read, the policy read and `budget.Open` share the single token take of step 2 and map their errors exactly as `startBudgetBackend` does — `token.ErrTokenExpired` ⇒ `bootstrapHalt(HaltBudgetTokenExpired, …)`, `budget.ErrGraceExpired` ⇒ `bootstrapHalt(HaltBudgetUnavailable, …)`, anything else ⇒ the refusal `the budget backend refused the run's identity: …`. A permission-denied or unparseable `config/policy/shared` read taken while the credential works is in the same class (step 1's third outcome): **halt**. The rule the implementation states in one sentence: *the mode-off path is entered by the job's own mode being off, never by an absence of a credential and never by a failure the run sees* (B1: an absent key or an absent token object is a refusal on a budget-on run, `budget_session.go:174-175`,`:186-187`).
2. **One token take, shared** (finding 2). `internal/cli/launch_budget.go`'s decision changes (`budgetBackendOn`, `:38-40`): a token is minted when Firebase is configured **and either the local mode is not off or a policy exists** — the launcher can see the policy with its own IAM credentials in the same call it already makes for the advisory check (Task 1.5 step 4), so the mode decision follows the policy rather than the operator's local mode. On the runner side, the read moves into the session's own bootstrap: `TakeObject` is called **once** and its result handed to both the policy read and the budget session (`budget_session.go`'s `:128`/`:180` path takes the object today). The task states the invariant in the code's comment: **`TakeObject` is called exactly once per run**, because a second call finds nothing and a token kept in memory is the same token.
3. `resolvePolicy`: when the policy exists, the **ceiling** is built from it instead of from `r.d.Spend`:

```go
ceiling := policy.Layer{MaxRunTokens: c.MaxRunTokens, AllowedModels: c.AllowedModels}
if pp != nil {
	ceiling = policy.Layer{Mode: pp.Budget.Mode, PerRunUSD: pp.Budget.PerRunUSD,
		MaxRunTokens: pp.Budget.MaxRunTokens, AllowedModels: pp.Budget.AllowedModels}
	c.Prices = pp.Prices          // the owner's overrides, replacing FUGARO_MODEL_PRICES
	c.Cap = /* µ$ from PerRunUSD */
}
```
   **Only the ceiling moves** (12a item 13): the default branch's `fugaro.yaml` layer and the run's own `cfg` are still merged at `:90` and can still only tighten. `s.Cap = c.Cap` at `:100` keeps working because the policy's cap becomes `c.Cap` too.
   **Correction (the fix round after the PR #379 security review, H1), because the sketch above says "instead of", and that is whole-key replacement.** A policy that names no `budget` key at all — one published only for `allow_repos` — replaced the whole ceiling with one whose mode was `""`, so no session opened and the environment's cap, the RTDB caps **and the global and repository kill switches** were all dropped: a fail-open, not a tightening, and the review's probe ran an enforcing installation's job with a $50 cap and the global kill switch on, successfully, with no budget record. The code replaces **key by key**: `envCeiling` builds the ceiling from the job environment (the shipped `:66-72` rules unchanged), `policyCeiling` overrides only the keys the node actually names (`mode`, `per_run_usd`, `max_run_tokens`, `allowed_models`, `model_prices`), and `policy.Sources` records `policy` only for those keys, so `result.json` names the right owner per key. A `budget` block that is present but names no mode is refused by the decoder and by `policy publish` (`internal/projectpolicy`'s `validBudget`), because such a block cannot be told from a typo.
4. `runtimeVersion` skew (P8): a policy whose `version` is not `projectpolicy.Version` halts with the shared message — the runner never guesses. (The marker's own version, `RulesVersion`, is separate and answers a different question: *can I read the policy at all*, step 1.)
5. The policy's `allow_repos` is stashed on the run for Task 1.5 (one read, one decode: `projectPolicy` is memoized like `defaultBranchFile` at `internal/runner/policy.go:30-51`).
6. `recordPolicy` (`internal/runner/policy.go:167`) names the source: `policy.sources` carries `policy=config/policy/shared` for the moved keys, so a run's `result.json` says where its ceiling came from. `fugaro diagnose` and `report` read that field unchanged.

**Tests:** `TestRunnerReadsPolicyWhenTheNodeExists`, `TestRunnerUsesEnvCeilingWhenTheNodeIsAbsent`, `TestRunnerWithoutTheMarkerTreatsADeniedReadAsLegacy` (the finding-1 regression test: marker absent + permission denied ⇒ the run proceeds on the env ceiling), `TestRunnerHaltsOnADeniedReadWhenTheMarkerExists`, `TestRunnerWithNoCredentialTakesTheModeOffPath` (**mode off** and no URL / no key ⇒ no session, no marker read, the env ceiling — the unimplementable "halt without the API key" assertion the round-2 review caught is replaced by this one, and the refusal is asserted where it can be: `internal/infra`'s readiness gate, `TestReadinessBlocksAJobThatCannotReadAPublishedPolicy` in Task 1.4), `TestRunnerWithTheModeOnAndNoCredentialHalts` (B1's regression test, and the sibling of `TestRunnerHaltsWhenTheExchangeFails`: the job's env says the budget is on but carries no `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, and `TestRunnerWithTheModeOnAndNoTokenHalts` for a bucket whose `TakeObject` returns `token.ErrNoToken` — both must stop the run through the shipped paths, `budget_session.go:174-175` and `:186-187`, and a run that proceeds on the env ceiling fails with "the budget was on and the run ignored the missing key"), **`TestRunnerHaltsWhenTheExchangeFails`** (R2-3's sibling, and the red step of this task: with the URL, the key and a token object all present, the exchange server answers 500 — or the token is past its hour — and the run must **halt** (`HaltBudgetTokenExpired` for the expired token, the "refused the run's identity" refusal for the 500), *not* proceed on the env ceiling; the test asserts the halt reason and, on the regression the earlier draft described, fails with "the run fell back to the env ceiling after a failed exchange"), `TestRunnerHaltsWhenTheMarkerReadFails`, `TestPolicyReadErrorHaltsTheRun`, `TestPolicyParseErrorHaltsTheRun`, `TestPolicyVersionMismatchHaltsTheRun`, `TestTokenIsTakenOnce` (the policy read and the session share one take; the fake asserts exactly one `TakeObject`), `TestLauncherMintsATokenWhenAPolicyExistsAndTheLocalModeIsOff` (in `internal/cli`, finding 2), `TestPolicyCannotLoosenTheBranchLayer` (a branch `fugaro.yaml` asking for a *higher* cap is still ignored, `policy.Ignored` records it), `TestPolicyPricesReplaceTheEnvOverrides`. The runner's existing fakes serve the node (`internal/gcpfake` has no RTDB; the budget tests' `internal/rtdb` test server is the pattern to reuse — if it is not reachable from `internal/runner`'s tests, put the policy read behind a `Deps` field the test sets).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/runner/... -count=1`. **Commit:** "runner: the project policy replaces the ceiling key by key, and a policy that cannot be read halts the run".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestRunnerReadsPolicyWhenTheNodeExists`, `TestRunnerUsesEnvCeilingWhenTheNodeIsAbsent`, `TestRunnerWithoutTheMarkerTreatsADeniedReadAsLegacy` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1.4: the RTDB URL **and API key** reach every job (D11) — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/infra/spec.go` (+ `spec_test.go`), `internal/infra/readiness.go` (the Firebase requirement's message), `internal/cli/launch_budget.go` (with Task 1.3 step 2: the mint decision).

**Verified today:** `internal/infra/spec.go:755-768` — `FUGARO_RTDB_URL`, `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` and `FUGARO_BUDGET_MODE` are written **only when the deploy-time budget mode is not off**, and the mode is read from the operator's local config (so the design's warning is exact: a job deployed with the mode off can never read the policy). **The earlier draft of this task wrote only the URL**, which is not enough: the token exchange needs the API key as well (`internal/runner/budget_session.go:66-71`, `FirebaseAPIKeyEnv` at `:30`), and a policy read that has a URL and no key fails exactly where it must not — at the hallt in Task 1.3 step 1.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: the installation's Firebase endpoints (`lc.Endpoints`), the project's Firebase API key. Produces: a job env that carries **both** whenever Firebase is configured, whatever the mode.

1. `budgetEnv` (`internal/infra/spec.go:732`): write `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` **and** `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` whenever the installation has them, and keep `FUGARO_BUDGET_MODE` conditional on the mode (the mode is no longer only the deploy-time mode once a policy exists, and Task 1.3 reads the policy's mode; the env stays as the fallback for the absent-node case, unchanged).
2. The readiness gate (`internal/infra/readiness.go`): **Firebase becomes mandatory in project-centric mode** (D11). The finding is a `warning` until a policy exists (an installation that has not adopted the model is not blocked) and *blocking* once `config/policy/shared` exists — read through the operator's client at init time, so the message is "the project has a policy: this job must carry `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` and `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, run `fugaro init --repo`". **The plan does not try to detect "project-centric mode" any other way**, and it does not lift the mode-off code path: an installation that wants the budget off stays on the per-repository path (design §5(1)), permanently, which `fugaro doctor` states.
3. **This is a security-relevant task, not a plumbing one** (the review says so and it is right): it decides whether a published policy can be read at all. Round-2 review restated what the failure looks like, because the runner cannot detect it: a job **deployed with the budget mode off**, carrying no key while a policy exists, **runs on the environment ceiling** (Task 1.3 step 1's mode-off path — the runner has no way to read the marker without the credential; the same job with the mode **on** halts instead, B1), so this task's **readiness gate is the refusal** (`init --repo`, and `doctor` when Task 1.6 step 4 gives it the check — it reads `infra.Readiness` today only through `init --repo`: blocking once `config/policy/shared` exists — a job that does not exist yet stays undeployed, and one that does exist, which P7a never un-deploys, refuses the apply) and the launcher's mint (Task 1.3 step 2) is what makes the job able to read the policy at all. It is listed for independent opus review with Task 1.3.
4. `docs/`: the mode-off caveat in `docs/multi-model.md` and `docs/project-layer.md` gains one sentence: the budget mode off is not available in project-centric mode.

**Tests:** `TestBudgetEnvWritesRTDBURLAndAPIKeyWhenFirebaseIsConfigured` (both variables, mode off included), `TestBudgetEnvKeepsModeOff`, `TestReadinessBlocksAJobThatCannotReadAPublishedPolicy`, `TestReadinessWarnsBeforeAPolicyExists`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/infra/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** Task 1.1's path constant (for the readiness message) and nothing else in this phase. The round-2 review is right that the earlier "it is independently revertible" line contradicted the audit, which recorded a dependency on 1.3; the two are reconciled like this. The *dependency* runs the other way — Task 1.3's reader consumes the two variables this task writes — but the two tasks **share one file**, `internal/cli/launch_budget.go` (this task's Task-1.3-step-2 half, the mint decision), so neither merges alone: they are PR group E together. The audit rows for 1.3 and 1.4 now say exactly that instead of a backward edge. **Commit:** "spec: the job carries the RTDB URL whenever Firebase is configured".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestBudgetEnvWritesRTDBURLAndAPIKeyWhenFirebaseIsConfigured`, `TestBudgetEnvKeepsModeOff`, `TestReadinessBlocksAJobThatCannotReadAPublishedPolicy` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1.5: `allow_repos` and the slug binding's first half — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/runner/{runner.go,project.go}` (+ tests), `internal/cli/run.go` (+ `run_layer_test.go` or a new `run_allowlist_test.go`).

**Verified today (and the earlier draft of this task was wrong about two of these; the review caught it and the code confirms it).** `bootstrap` reads `task.json` at `internal/runner/runner.go:797-803`, **then** calls `openProvider` at **`:833-849`** — `:586` is that function's *definition*, not the call — then clones at `:851-855`, reads `fugaro.yaml` at `:1015-1019` and resolves the policy at `:993`. So "before `openProvider`" is a real place *after* `task.json` is known (`:803`) and *before* any git credential exists (`:833`), and it is where this phase's checks have to live: `resolvePolicy` (`:993`) is far too late, because the clone — and the token it needed — has already happened. **The provider before the clone is not available from `fugaro.yaml`**: `config.Git.Provider` is read at `:1015`, after it. What exists at `:833-849` is `r.d.ProviderKind` (the `--provider` flag, defaulting to `$FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER`, `internal/cli/exec.go:69`) or `gitprov.KindForURL(origin)` — both env-derived and launcher-overridable, which is exactly what 12a item 2 forbids. The slug comes from `FUGARO_RUN` (`internal/cli/exec.go:157`,`:168`, `runstore.ParseRef` at `internal/runstore/runstore.go:409`) and keys the locks (`internal/runner/lockcache.go:76`), the cache (`:188`) and the budget session (`internal/runner/budget_session.go:128`,`:180`); `task.Slug(provider, repo)` is `internal/task/task.go:179`. **Nothing checks that `task.Slug(provider, spec.Repo)` equals the slug**: today the per-repository job's bucket condition is what makes a mismatch fail (design §2.2).

**Interfaces.** Consumes: Task 1.1's `MatchRepo`/`Canonical`, Task 1.3's memoized policy. Produces: `func (r *run) admissionCheck(ctx context.Context) error`, and the CLI's advisory twin `func allowListRefusal(p *projectpolicy.Policy, provider, repo string) error` in `internal/cli`.

1. **Placement, in `bootstrap`'s own order**: `task.json` (`:797-803`) → **the marker and policy read (Task 1.3)** → **this check** → `openProvider` (`:833-849`) → the clone. Stated as an ordered list because the order *is* the security property: the check must run before a credential exists and before the clone, and the policy must be readable before the check can use it. `resolvePolicy` keeps its later place — it merges the policy as the ceiling once `fugaro.yaml` has been read (`:993`), and it must not be the place the policy is first read.
2. **The provider, derived without asking any overridable source (12a item 2, finding 3).** The check needs a provider to compute `task.Slug(provider, spec.Repo)`; phase 1 does **not** have one in the job env it can trust.
