It derives it from the pair it *can* trust — the full slug the run was given (`FUGARO_RUN`, which is also what the budget token's `fs` claim and the job's bucket condition bind to) and the repository `task.json` names:

```go
// providerForSlug is the provider kind whose Slug of repo is slug. One job
// serves one provider, and the slug is a hash of (kind, canonical repo)
// (internal/task/task.go:187), so exactly one kind can match. It is
// derived, never read from an env var a launcher can override (12a item 2).
func providerForSlug(slug, repo string) (kind string, ok bool) {
	var found []string
	for _, k := range jobProviderKinds { // github, bitbucket
		if s, err := task.Slug(k, repo); err == nil && s == slug {
			found = append(found, k)
		}
	}
	return cmp.Or(found...), len(found) == 1
}
```
   **`jobProviderKinds` is the Cloud Run kinds, and the `fake` kind must not be refused by the binding (R2-9, deferrable).** `fugaro exec --task-file … --provider fake` is a real path (`internal/cli/exec.go:150-163`, and the flag documents `fake`), the repository's own runner and end-to-end tests use it, and it computes its slug with `task.Slug("fake", repo)` — so a binding over `{github, bitbucket}` alone would refuse every fake run. The two ways to keep it honest are: include `fake` in the kind set the *binding* iterates (a fake run is local, has no job and no bucket condition to bind to, so admitting it costs nothing), or scope the whole binding to the runs the CLI resolved as **local**. **The plan takes the second, in the form the implementation gives it** — the checks are skipped for `r.d.Local && r.d.Execution == ""` and for nothing else, where `Local` is the platform the CLI resolved (no backend claims the process) rather than "no execution name is set": `Local`'s zero value means the cloud (`internal/backend`'s `platform.go`), and a job whose environment was edited after launch carries no execution name either, so scoping this by the execution name alone exempted a process **no backend claims** from the binding, the remote's host and the allow-list while its git credential stayed scoped to whatever remote that environment named (the follow-up review of the phase-1 code, and the R2-9 note below). The binding exists because a job's identity is what an admission rests on, and the `fake` provider stays available to the tests either way — a `fake` run is local, so it still reaches neither check. An implementer who takes the first must still refuse two matches; the point of the step is that the choice is made here, not discovered when the e2e tests start failing.
   Zero matches ⇒ refuse ("`FUGARO_RUN` names a slug that is not this repository under any provider kind"). Two matches ⇒ refuse (a hash collision, or a new kind added without a domain separator — `task.Slug` already separates with `"\x00"`, so this is a programming error made visible instead of invisible). A `--provider`/`FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER` value that disagrees with the derived kind is also refused — the flag may only tighten. **Phase 3 does this differently and better** (the job name carries the profile and the provider, Task 3.2/3.3); this is the phase-1 form, and it is what makes phase 1 safe to merge before any project job exists.
3. Then the two refusals, both before `openProvider`:
   - `task.Slug(providerKind, spec.Repo) == <the slug FUGARO_RUN carries>` — implied by the derivation above and re-checked explicitly, naming both, with the sentence that the per-repository job's bucket condition used to be the only thing standing in for it.
   - `MatchRepo(p.AllowRepos, Canonical(providerKind, spec.Repo))` — refuse otherwise, naming the pattern list and `fugaro project repos add`.
   - When the policy is **absent** (no marker, or a marker and no node — Task 1.3 step 1), only the slug binding and the provider derivation are checked: an installation that has not published a policy has no allow-list, and inventing "empty means deny" there would break every installation on upgrade. **Empty `allow_repos` *inside* a published policy is deny-all**, and `policy publish` seeds it (P4), so the two cases cannot be confused.
4. `fugaro run` (the CLI, `internal/cli/run.go`) checks the same thing before launching and refuses with the same message, reading the policy with the operator's credentials. **It is advisory and the docs say so** (design §4.2/§4.3): a launcher can call `jobs.run` directly, which is why the runner's check is the one that counts.
   **Owner ruling 2026-10-17: `policySince` names 0.8.5** (it reads `"0.8.0"` at `internal/cli/recipes_skew.go` today; see "Owner rulings of 2026-10-17 on phase 3", item 5). **The `policySince` launch gate lives here too (finding 16, B5 — this step is its owner; the earlier "Task 1.6 step 3" citation named `policy show`, which refuses nothing).** In the same pre-launch read, the launcher compares the policy it just read against what the binary can run and refuses **before** creating the execution: a policy whose `version` is above `projectpolicy.Version` (P8's release skew) refuses the launch with the message `fugaro run` already prints for a stale binary. **The rules-version marker is deliberately *not* part of this gate** (round-6 review: an earlier revision had it refuse on a marker below `RulesVersion` too, which contradicted Task 1.3 step 1, P3, line 7 and phase 1's merge-alone claim). A marker absent or below `RulesVersion` is Task 1.3 step 1's legacy path — `found=false`, no policy read, no refusal — and `policy publish` refuses to publish without the marker (Task 1.6), so an unmarked installation has **no policy for this gate to compare** and must launch exactly as it does today. Refusing there would halt every launch on an installation whose rules deploy is the owner's own step (`belong`, `fugaro` and every installation that has published nothing), and with `RulesVersion = 1` "below the marker" is indistinguishable from "absent", so there is no version of the clause that keeps P3 true. The marker's own skew — rules older than the marker, the state an older CLI's `init --firebase` leaves — is a *read* failure whose owners are Task 1.3 step 1's halt and `doctor`'s `policy-rules-skew` (Task 1.6 step 4's fifth check), not the launcher. The shape is `recipes_skew.go:43-70`'s: a `policySince` constant next to `cloneSince = "0.7.5"` (`:44`), the comparison, and one message naming both versions and the fix (`fugaro upgrade`). **The constant's value is `policySince = "0.8.0"`** — the release this model ships in (R4 as amended by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15, R6: **phase 1, which is where the policy model itself ships, is in 0.8.0** — the phases that move to 0.9.0 are 3 to 6 and the compute backend, and none of them changes this constant), which is what the gate is *for*: a binary from `main`'s 0.7.5 line must refuse a 0.8.0 policy rather than misread it, and the constant is a **release**, like `cloneSince`, not the policy's integer `version` or the marker's `RulesVersion`. It is bumped by the release that ships the model and by nothing else, and the message quotes it beside the policy's own `version`. **The gate is deliberately the launcher's and not the runner's** (R2-8(a)): the runner with no credential cannot read the marker at all, so a refusal placed there would be the fail-open Task 1.3 step 1 removed — the gate is what closes the no-token case, and SECURITY.md states it as a D10-class launcher choice. It is advisory in the same sense the allow-list check is (a launcher can call `jobs.run` directly) and **that is sufficient here**, because the case it covers is an operator's own binary lagging its own policy, not an adversary.
5. `SECURITY.md`: the launcher-override boundary lands here (D10, accepted): one paragraph saying that a launcher can override a job's environment, that the budget token deliberately does not travel that way, and **that the pointer is overridable too** — clearing `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` (or pointing it at another database) sends the run down the env-ceiling path, because the mode *is* the node and the pointer to the node is a launcher's to clear (finding 27; design §4.2 lists the pointer among the overridable settings). The paragraph says exactly what an execution's overrides expose **from the phase-1 evidence in Check 49 step 6** (a live `FUGARO_RUN` override on a real job), not from a phase-3 probe — the earlier draft made this paragraph wait for Task 3.0's probe, which was a forward edge into phase 3 and is gone (finding 14).

**Tests:** `TestProviderForSlugDerivesEachKind`, `TestProviderForSlugRefusesNoMatchAndAmbiguity`, `TestRunnerIgnoresAnOverriddenProviderEnvAmongAgreeingKinds` (a `FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER` naming the *other* kind is refused, not obeyed), `TestRunnerRefusesSlugMismatch`, `TestRunnerRefusesSlugNotInAllowList`, `TestRunnerChecksBeforeTheTokenAndTheLease` (the fake asserts **no** credential request and **no** reserve write, and that the refusal happens before the calls at `internal/runner/runner.go:838`/`:848` — `:833` is the function's first line, not the call, R2-12), `TestRunnerWithoutAPolicyChecksOnlyTheBinding`, `TestEmptyAllowListInAPublishedPolicyDeniesEverything`, `TestRunRefusesBeforeLaunch` (CLI), `TestRunRefusesAPolicyNewerThanTheBinary` and **`TestRunLaunchesWithNoPolicyPublished`** (B5: the gate's one skew direction, asserting that **no execution was created** in the fake and that the message names both versions and the fix; the second is the round-6 correction — a marker **absent or below `RulesVersion`** with no policy node is Task 1.3 step 1's legacy path, so `fugaro run` **creates the execution** and refuses nothing, which is what `TestRunRefusesAMarkerBelowRulesVersion` used to pin the other way), `TestPolicySinceGateIsNotOnTheRunnerPath` (a run launched directly through `jobs.run` — no `fugaro run` — still runs, which is the D10 statement that the gate is the launcher's, not a second runner-side refusal).

### Task 1.6: `fugaro project`, the doctor checks and the drift note — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/cli/project_cmd.go` (new) (+ `project_policy_test.go`), `internal/cli/root.go`, `internal/cli/doctor.go` (+ tests), `internal/cli/upgrade_cloud.go` (+ tests), `plugin/skills/setup/**`, `plugin/skills/working/**`, `docs/project-layer.md`, `docs/multi-model.md`, `docs/checkout.md`, `SECURITY.md`.

**Verified today:** **there is no `fugaro project` command group**; `internal/cli/project.go` already exists and is project *selection* (`checkoutProject` at `:30`), so the group gets its own **command files** — this task's is `project_cmd.go`, and Tasks 4.1 and 6.1 add `project_enroll.go` and `project_export.go` beside it (C2) — and the root registers every command at `internal/cli/root.go:75-78`. `upgrade`'s drift note is `internal/cli/upgrade_cloud.go:162-177` (`jobConfigCopyNote`), and `doctor`'s severities are `internal/cli/doctor.go:39-46`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: Task 1.1's `Policy`/`Decode`/paths, the RTDB client `fugaro budget set` uses (`internal/cli/budget.go:953` `runBudgetSet`, `openBudget` at `:160`), the operator's local config (`providers`, `claude_accounts`, the budget ceiling and `model_prices`) and the bucket config reader. Produces: `newProjectCmd()` and three subcommands.

1. `newProjectCmd()` (`Use: "project"`, registered in `internal/cli/root.go:75-78`): `policy publish`, `policy show`, `show`, and a `repos` subcommand with `ls`, `add` and `rm`. The plan puts `repos add` here, in phase 1, not phase 4 (P4 and 12a item 3): between phases 1 and 4 a new repository would otherwise be unenrolled, and this one-liner is the interim path. **`sync`, `repos ls` beyond the allow-list and the discovery surface stay in phase 4** (Task 4.1).
2. `policy publish`: builds the policy from the operator's file — the M9a budget (`mode`, `per_run_usd`, `allowed_models`, `max_run_tokens`), providers (name, base URL, auth style, **the secret's name, never a value**, models, `allow_data_to`, `route_fee_pct`, and from the price catalog `require_zdr`/`price_policy` when those have merged), `model_prices` overrides, `allow_repos`, and (1b.1) `routing` — writes it with `PutIfMatch` (the CAS `runBudgetSet` already uses, `internal/cli/budget.go:1011`) and refuses to write a `version` above its own (P8). It prints the diff and the seeded allow-list, and it **seeds only when the node is absent or `--seed-repos` is passed** (P4), seeding **only from the operator's own `repos:` map and the shared config's repository list**, each converted with `Canonical`. **It does not seed from the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes** (B3; P4 and 12a item 4 say so and this step contradicted them): a slug is not invertible — a readable prefix plus a truncated hash of `(provider, canonical repo)` (`internal/task/task.go:183-196`), with no provider in `task.json` (`:45-79`) — so the only way to turn one back into a name is to read that run's `task.json` and test `task.Slug(kind, repo)` for each provider kind, which is Task 4.1's *seen* question ("what has run"), not this one ("what to enroll"). Seeding from the bucket would enroll a repository the operator never named, and under Task 3.2's broadened `runs/` grant (D13) the prefixes are **writable by every run of the profile**, so an enrolled repository's run could mint `runs/<anything>/task.json` and enroll itself by the time the next `policy publish` runs. `project show` and `project repos ls` (Task 4.1) print the seen set instead, where the operator can read it and add a name deliberately. **`policy publish --delete` is part of this step, not an afterthought** (B5; Check 49 step 8's restore path requires it, and it is the only way back to the env ceiling once a policy exists): it removes `config/policy/shared` (and the `config/policy/repos/*` nodes the publisher wrote) with the same `PutIfMatch` CAS, prints what it removed, and refuses **without `--yes`** when runs are in flight — a repository that is mid-run keeps the policy until it finishes. **`--delete` goes through the same rules-marker gate as a publish**: with no marker this CLI cannot know a policy of this shape belongs to this installation, and an absent marker is an installation that never deployed the rules a policy is read under. Until it is deleted the node governs, so a deletion that half-succeeds (the shared node gone, a `$slug` node left) is refused rather than left behind: the child nodes are deleted first and the shared node last, and the deletion of the last one is what makes the mode off again (P3). **The order is children first, the shared node last, and the reason is what a launch sees in between** (the round-B ruling, B5): the shared node's existence *is* project-centric mode, so a launch that reads it mid-delete takes the policy's tighter ceiling, while deleting the shared node first would leave every launch that lands in the window with no policy at all — a brief, silent drop to the looser ceiling the job's environment carries, which is the fail-open direction P3 exists to close. It also refuses to delete a node published by a **newer** CLI, for P8's reason in the other direction. **It writes names, never values**: every secret-shaped field is a Secret Manager name — a provider's key, the git credentials, and (Task 3.3) the GitHub App key — read from the operator's config as names, with the values never loaded on this path at all, which `TestPolicyCarriesNoSecretValue` asserts by building the node from a config whose fake Secret Manager holds known bytes and scanning the published body for them.
3. `policy show` / `project show`: the effective project — caps (global, defaults, per-repository), the policy, the allow-list, the repositories seen, each with its source; `--json` for `watch`-style consumers. It prints the allow-list minus the seen repositories (unenrolled-but-running) and the reverse (allowed but never seen), which is the pair an operator needs.
4. `fugaro doctor`: new checks — `policy-published` (info: whether a policy exists and its version), `policy-newer-than-binary` (warning, P8), `policy-vs-jobs` (warning when a policy is published and the installation's jobs still carry an old runner image: the trap of 12a item 6, detected with the image digests `image refresh` already compares), `allow-list-empty` (warning when a policy exists whose `allow_repos` is empty), and (B5) **`policy-rules-skew`** (warning when the deployed rules are **older than the marker** — the state an older CLI's `fugaro init --firebase` leaves behind by redeploying the pre-phase-1 template while `config/limits/policyRules` stays, `Task 1.3 step 1`'s third outcome: the policy becomes unreadable and every budget-on run halts). The check reads the marker and compares it with `projectpolicy.RulesVersion`, prints the same sentence the halt prints — `the deployed rules are older than the marker config/limits/policyRules: re-run 'fugaro init --firebase' with fugaro >= <version>` — and is a **warning and not an error**, because the fix is the owner's `init --firebase` and `doctor` cannot perform it. This is the check finding 16's "doctor is the complement of the gate" is about: the gate refuses a *launch*, this reports the state *before* one, and 12a item 6's message is the same text in `upgrade`'s drift note.
5. `upgrade` (`internal/cli/upgrade_cloud.go:162-177`): the drift note gains the policy sentence — after a policy is published, `upgrade` says that a job's **env copy** of the ceiling is now inert for the keys the policy governs, so the drift it reports is no longer a reason to re-run `init --repo`, and what *does* still need one (mounts) is listed.
6. The skills: `plugin/skills/setup/**` (the setup skill describes `init --repo`'s policy questions and must now say they are answered once per project) and `plugin/skills/working/**` (the working skill's `fugaro.yaml` policy section). Both keep their security text; `fugaro update-skills` distributes them.
7. Docs: `docs/project-layer.md` (the layer is now what tightens the policy), `docs/multi-model.md` (the prices come from the policy when published), `docs/checkout.md` (unchanged, but the phase-3 note is added there in Task 3.6), and SECURITY.md's Task 1.5 paragraph.

**Tests:** `TestProjectPolicyPublishWritesTheNodeAndSeedsTheAllowList`, `TestProjectPolicyPublishDoesNotReseedARemovedRepo`, `TestProjectPolicyPublishNeverSeedsFromTheBucket` (B3's regression test: the fake bucket holds `runs/<a slug Task.Slug can invert>/task.json` for a repository the operator's config does not name, `--seed-repos` is passed, and the published `allow_repos` must not contain it; a policy that does fails with "the seed came from the bucket's runs/<slug>/ prefixes, which every run of the profile can write"), `TestProjectPolicyPublishSeedIsOnlyTheOperatorsRepos` (the seeded set is exactly the canonical names of the config's `repos:` plus the shared config's list, compared as a set), `TestProjectPolicyPublishRefusesAVersionItCannotWrite`, `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRemovesChildrenThenTheSharedNode`, `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRemovesChildrenBeforeTheSharedNode`, `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRefusesWithoutYesWhileARunIsInFlight`, `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRefusesTheTypedNameWhileARunIsInFlight` (the terminal case, which is where the run-in-flight gate can otherwise be walked past) and `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRefusesWithoutTheRulesMarker` (B5's `--delete`), `TestProjectPolicyDeleteRefusesANewerPolicy`, `TestPolicyCarriesNoSecretValue` (no value of any secret the fake Secret Manager holds appears in the published bytes — the rule 12a item 7 and Task 3.3 depend on), `TestProjectPolicyPublishRefusesAPastedKeyWithoutEchoingIt` (a provider `.secret` holding a pasted `sk-…` key is refused with the field path and **without** the value, which is a key: no node written), `TestProjectShowPrintsEnrolledAndSeen`, `TestDoctorPolicyChecks` (**all five**, including each severity, and `policy-rules-skew` asserted on the redeployed-older-rules fixture: marker at `RulesVersion`, rules at the pre-phase-1 template ⇒ warning, with the halt's own sentence in the body), `TestUpgradeNoteSaysTheEnvCopyIsInert`, and the docs tests for the skill and doc edits.

### Task 1.7: Check 49, the rules deploy and the adoption order — OWNER-RUN

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`.

Append `## Check 49: policy as data, end to end (installation fugaro only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row, with **the order the design fixes: rules deploy, runner rollout, `policy publish`** (design §5), and exactly these steps:

1. **Setup and guard** — **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1: the three hard stops, `--project fugaro` on every `fugaro` command, `--project "${P:?}"` on every `gcloud` command, re-run from a fresh shell at the start of every step), run from a file. **Record the restore**: `fugaro budget show --json`, `fugaro project show --json` (before: nothing), and the sandbox's `fugaro.yaml` `project:` and pins.
2. **The rules deploy.** `fugaro init --firebase` on installation `fugaro`. **FACT:** the rules byte count, that init read them back, and — the new part — that no `config/policy/shared` node exists yet (`fugaro project show` says "no policy").
3. **The runner rollout, and the two halves it actually needs (finding 15).** `fugaro upgrade --cloud` and `fugaro image refresh` do **not** carry Task 1.4: `upgrade` only *reports* drift (`internal/cli/upgrade_cloud.go:162-177`) and `image refresh` patches a job's **image** through the API (`internal/cli/image_refresh_steps.go:241-304`). Task 1.4's change is a **job environment** change — `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` and `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY` — and the only path that writes a job's env is `fugaro init --repo` (OWNER-RUN). So the rollout is two owner steps in this order: **release** a runner image carrying Task 1.3, then **`fugaro init --repo` on the sandbox repository** to apply the env, then let the image land (`fugaro upgrade --cloud`, or `fugaro image refresh`). **FACT:** the job's env as `gcloud run jobs describe` reports it (the URL and the key's presence, never its value), then one ordinary sandbox run's `policy.sources` — with no policy published it must name the **ceiling from the env**, exactly as before, and the run must succeed. This is the "nothing changes until you publish" half of P3, and it is the step that fails if Task 1.4's `FUGARO_RTDB_URL` regressed the mode-off path. **The design's "adoption costs one `init --repo` per repository" is this step**, and a rollout that skips it is a rollout whose runs never see the pointer at all.
4. **Publish.** `fugaro project policy publish` on installation `fugaro`, with a per-run ceiling the sandbox run can notice (for instance `per_run_usd` different from the job env's). **FACT:** the printed diff, the seeded `allow_repos` (and that it names `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`'s canonical form, not a bare name — P5), and the node's `version`.
5. **The policy governs.** One sandbox run. **FACT:** `result.json`'s effective `policy` and its `sources` (they must name `config/policy/shared`), and the run's per-call charge agreeing with the published ceiling rather than the job env's — the mutation Task 1.3's unit test cannot see, because this is a real token against real rules.
6. **It fails closed.** Point the sandbox's `fugaro.yaml` at a repository the allow-list does not name (a temporary edit on the sandbox branch, reverted in step 8), and run. **FACT:** the refusal verbatim, and that **no git token was minted and no lease was taken** — visible as the absence of a `credential` line and of a reserve in `fugaro budget show`. Then, on the same run, confirm the **slug-mismatch** refusal by launching with a `FUGARO_RUN` override naming another slug (a launcher can: `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:110`): the run must refuse rather than run. **Record which layer refused, because on a phase-1 legacy job it is not this phase's binding (R2-10, deferrable).** The override naming another slug fails at `ReadTask` (`internal/runner/runner.go:797-803`): the per-repository job's bucket condition denies another slug's `task.json`, so the run stops before `admissionCheck` ever runs — the step passes, but for the bucket condition's reason, not the binding's. The FACT line therefore says which refusal text appeared, and **the live test of the binding itself belongs to Check 53 step 6**, where the job is a project job with no per-slug bucket condition and the admission check is the only thing that can refuse. Until then the binding is covered by Task 1.5's unit tests, which is why this step does not claim to prove it.
7. **The CLI is advisory and says so.** `fugaro run` on the same unenrolled repository prints its own refusal before launching; and the docs read (`SECURITY.md`, `docs/project-layer.md`) names the launcher-override boundary rather than claiming a fix.
8. **Restore**: revert the sandbox `fugaro.yaml` edit, `fugaro budget set` with step 1's recorded values, confirm `fugaro project show --json`'s caps match, and run one ordinary sandbox task to confirm the sandbox still runs. **Leave the policy published only if the owner intends to keep the model**; otherwise `fugaro project policy publish --delete` (Task 1.6 step 2 already ships it — B5 — so the restore path has a command and not an outline) and re-run one task to confirm the env ceiling governs again.

End with: "**Paste back:** every FACT line above, never a secret value and never a key value. Never run this against `belong`."

**Tests:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1`.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck49`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 49` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck49`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

**Depends on:** Tasks 1.2 (the rules it deploys), 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.6. **Owns the phase's one owner-run step.**

## Phase 1b — the routing switch (four tasks; gated on two other PRs; release **0.8.0**, R6)

The design's §6.2. **The gate is real and is not this phase's to control**: #359's phase 2 (the `anthropic/<id>` claim and `IsAlias(model, claimed)`) and the price catalog (#361) must be merged. **If they are not merged when phase 1 ends, this phase waits and phases 2 to 6 proceed** — nothing in them reads the routing switch (P7). The switch is *delivered* by the policy (Task 1.1's `Routing` field) but *written* only by the operator, in the local operator config or the project layer, and a repository can never write it (D1a).

### Task 1b.1: `routing.claude` — the object, its validation and its delivery

**Files:** `internal/config/config.go` (+ the operator's section), `internal/localcfg/localcfg.go` (+ tests), `internal/config/{providers.go,validate.go}` (+ tests), `internal/projectpolicy/policy.go` (+ test), `internal/cli/project.go` (publish).

**Verified today:** `ClaudePatterns` forces Claude direct and `CheckModelPattern` refuses a provider pattern overlapping it (`internal/config/providers.go:331-344`, `:205-220`); `pricing.IsAlias` refuses the `anthropic/` prefix and `~` (`internal/pricing/alias.go:30-44`), and `CheckPins` refuses the pin (`internal/config/pins.go:27-32`); the provider's `openness` is `AllowDataTo` (`internal/config/providers.go:164` `OpenToEveryRepoReason` — an absent `allow_data_to` means every repository, which is exactly why D1b's safeguard must require it to be **written**).

**Interfaces.** Consumes: Task 1.1's `projectpolicy.Policy.Routing`; #359's claim mechanism. Produces: `config.RoutingClaude{Via string; Models []string}` and `localcfg.Config.Routing`, plus `RoutingClaudeFor(pins, providers) (route string, model string, ok bool)` — one resolver used by the pins renderer, the price lookup and `validate`.

1. `config.RoutingClaude` with `Via` in `direct|openrouter|auto` (empty ⇒ `direct`, D1b) and an **explicit list of canonical Claude IDs** in `Models` — never a pattern: a glob, a bare prefix or an empty list with `via: openrouter` is refused by validation with the message naming D1a and the merged ruling it comes from.
2. Validation: `openrouter` and `auto` resolve to a provider **only when that provider's `allow_data_to` is written**, as a list or the explicit `["*"]` (design §6.2). An absent `allow_data_to` with `via: openrouter` is refused at config load, naming `allow_data_to` and the provider.
3. Definition sites: the operator's local config and the **project policy node** (`config/policy/shared`, which `policy publish` writes), and **nowhere else** — a repository's `fugaro.yaml` has no `routing` key, which the existing `TestRepoCannotAddProvider`-style test asserts for this key too. **Amended 2026-10-15 (the round-2 review of PR #414):** this step used to name the project layer as the second site. The layer is not one in this release — its decode is strict, so a `routing:` key there is **not accepted but refused loudly** (`localcfg.ParseProjectLayer`) — and D1a's end state for the layer is a later slice. What the layer does carry, today and through this phase, is its `claude_models` claim: one explicit `anthropic/<id>` per route entry, merged into the run's own routes on every launch, which is the interim path the switch does not replace. So a claim in the layer is a route the config defines, never a second place the switch is written.
4. `policy publish` copies `Routing` into `config/policy/shared`; `project show` prints the objects and the IDs.

**Tests:** `TestRoutingAbsentIsDirect`; `TestRoutingRefusesAGlob`; `TestRoutingRefusesOpenrouterWithoutAllowDataTo`; `TestRepoCannotWriteRouting`; `TestPolicyCarriesRouting`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/config/... ./internal/localcfg/... ./internal/projectpolicy/... -count=1`. **Commit:** "config: routing.claude, an explicit ID list, absent means direct".

A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1b.2: the canonical-to-route ID map

**Files:** `internal/pricing/catalog/gencatalog/main.go` (+ `main_test.go`), `internal/pricing/catalog/catalog.json` (regenerated), `internal/pricing/{catalog_apply.go,pricing.go}` (+ tests), `internal/config/pricetable.go`, `internal/runner/{agentenv.go,spend.go,gateway.go}` (+ tests), `scripts/gen-price-catalog.sh`.

**Verified today:** the gateway never rewrites a body, so a Claude model has a different ID per route (`claude-sonnet-5-5` versus `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`) and nothing translates between them (design §2.4); `stagePins` returns nil when the gateway is off (`internal/runner/agentenv.go:54-57`) and renders the pins at `:54-77`; the price catalog's rows are keyed by the provider's model ID (`internal/pricing/catalog`).

**Interfaces.** Consumes: the catalog's rows and the switch (1b.1). Produces: a `canonical` field on a catalog row and one resolver, `pricing.RouteIDFor(canonical string, route string) (string, bool)`, used by the pin renderer, the price lookup and the recorded `serving_model`/`priced_as`.

1. The generator: the allowlist gains the Claude rows **only under the opt-in the catalog plan's D8 defers to this phase**, and each Claude row carries `canonical_id` (the direct ID, `claude-sonnet-5-5`) beside its own key (the route's ID, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`). A row whose canonical ID is missing, or two rows claiming one canonical ID on one route, is a hard error — the same class of refusal the catalog's Task 1 already has.
2. `RouteIDFor` and its inverse: given a canonical ID and a resolved route, the ID to send and the ID to price. **The same function is what `serving_model`/`priced_as` are recorded with**, so a bill, a charge and a record name the same model — the design's requirement (§6.2) and Review Focus 11's mutation.
3. `stagePins` (`internal/runner/agentenv.go:54-77`): with the gateway on and `routing.claude.via` naming this Claude ID, the pin renders the route's ID; otherwise the canonical ID, exactly as today. **With the gateway off nothing changes**: `stagePins` still returns nil (routing exists only with the gateway, D11 makes that the project-centric mode).
4. The price lookup and the gateway's route resolution use the same resolved ID, so a Claude pin routed through OpenRouter is priced from the catalog's row for that route (the catalog plan's P5 builder) and never from the Anthropic table.

**Tests:** `TestCanonicalMapRoundTrip` (Review Focus 11), `TestGeneratorRefusesDuplicateCanonicalID`, `TestStagePinsUseTheRouteIDOnlyWhenOptedIn`, `TestClaudePinWithoutOptInIsUnchanged`, `TestRoutedClaudeIsPricedFromTheCatalogRow`.

**Amended 2026-10-16 (the follow-up run on PR #422) — step 1 is not done, and this is why.** No Claude row is shipped in `internal/pricing/catalog/catalog.json`: regenerating it from the checked-in snapshot (`scripts/gen-price-catalog.sh -from internal/pricing/catalog/testdata/openrouter`) produces identical bytes, because the generator's rows come from that snapshot and it holds no Claude model. The allowlist's own comment (`scripts/gen-price-catalog.sh`, "There is still no anthropic/<id> row here") already records this: the phase-1 allowlist is the DeepSeek family plus one Qwen and one Moonshot row. So the generator's Claude-row rule and `canonical_id` are implemented and tested — a key under the `anthropic/` prefix must carry the direct spelling, a row that writes a route ID there is refused, and two rows claiming one canonical ID is a hard error — but no row uses the field yet.

**Consequence, stated so a reader is not misled:** with the gateway on and a Claude pin the switch routes, the call is priced from an explicit `model_prices` entry for the route's ID (the owner's, or the policy node's) and never from the direct Anthropic table — `TestRoutedClaudeIsPricedFromTheRouteRowNotTheDirectRate` (internal/runner) pins exactly that, including the refusal when the owner has priced nothing. The catalog's own Claude row, and with it the plan's fifth named test, is the one thing the plan asks for that this branch does not deliver: `TestRoutedClaudeIsPricedFromTheCatalogRow` has no row to be priced from, so the test that pins the same rule is `TestRoutedClaudePinEndToEnd` (the whole run through the policy path) with the route's row written as `model_prices` in the fixture. The four other named tests exist as named, and `TestRoutingResolutionHasOneSite`/`TestRoutingHasOneResolver` were rebuilt as AST checks per the review.

**Decision for the owner:** either (a) land the measured snapshot of the three Claude routes and add those rows plus `TestRoutedClaudeIsPricedFromTheCatalogRow`, or (b) accept the `model_prices` path as this release's answer for a routed Claude and drop that row from the plan's test list. This run took neither — inventing prices in a shipped catalog would charge real money at a number nobody measured, and removing the plan's line is the owner's call, not this branch's.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/pricing/... ./internal/config/... ./internal/runner/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 1b.1 and the merged catalog. **Commit:** "pricing: the canonical-to-route ID map, one resolver for pins, prices and records".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestCanonicalMapRoundTrip`, `TestGeneratorRefusesDuplicateCanonicalID`, `TestStagePinsUseTheRouteIDOnlyWhenOptedIn` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 1b.4: Check 50 — OWNER-RUN

Append `## Check 50: a Claude senior through a provider route, and whether its cache markers survive (installation fugaro only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row. Steps, guarded with **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1), with the restore:

1. Setup, guard, and the recorded state (the sandbox's pins, `routing.claude`, the job env's prices, the key's credit).
2. The refusal, unclaimed: with no `routing.claude`, a `anthropic/<id>` pin is refused at every surface. **FACT:** the refusal text.
3. The claim: `routing.claude: {via: openrouter, models: [claude-sonnet-5-5]}`, published. **FACT:** `fugaro validate`'s resolution line, and that a repository cannot write it (an attempt to add `routing:` to the sandbox's `fugaro.yaml` is refused).
4. Two runs of the same small task, one direct (no switch) and one routed. **FACT:** the reviewer's own `model call` lines — `serving_model`, `priced_as`, `cache_read`/`cache_write_5m` token counts and `charged_micros` — plus `cost.model_usd` and `cost.reported_usd` for both, and the bill for the day.
5. **The conclusion that is the point of the check:** whether the routed run reports cache **reads** at all. A routed Claude senior that never reports one is charged at the full input rate, which is the difference between list price and several times list price (design §6.2). **FACT:** both token counts, and one sentence on the cost difference.
6. The `auto` case: `via: auto` with and without an Anthropic key version. **FACT:** which route each run took, and that with no key the run does not fall through silently.
7. Restore: the switch removed (`policy publish` without it), the pins put back, one ordinary sandbox task confirming the sandbox still runs.

**Paste back:** every FACT line, never a key or a token. **If step 5 shows the markers do not survive, the design's conclusion does not change but the docs do** — Task 1b.3's paragraph gains the measured number, and the switch's help text says what it costs. **Depends on:** 1b.1 to 1b.3.

Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. ## Phase 2 — project-scope keys by default (four tasks; release **0.8.0**, R6)

The design's §6.3 and D2. **This phase changes a security default** and says so: #343's rule was "nothing shared until the owner says so" (`docs/design/project-secrets.md` §3(b), and its *Veto alternatives* (b) at `:57`); D2 makes storing at project scope *be* the opt-in. What it does **not** change is the data-policy gate: a provider's `allow_data_to` still decides which repositories' code the gateway may route to that provider, at runtime, and it still does **not** keep the key out of a shared job's environment (design §6.3).

### Task 2.1: `secrets set` stores at project scope — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/cli/secrets.go` (+ `secrets_test.go`), `internal/cli/initsecrets.go` (+ tests), `internal/infra/readiness.go` (messages), `SECURITY.md`.

**Verified today:** `secrets set` resolves a repository by default (`resolveSecretRepo`) and stores `gcp.SecretID(r.slug, name)` (`internal/cli/secrets.go:214-286`); `--project-wide` is the opt-in (`:76`, `checkScope` at `:85-92` — its doc comment starts at `:80`) and its eligibility runs through `projectSecretRefusal` (`:293-296`: `allow_data_to` must list at least one repository) and `repoMayUseProject` (`:500`). `gcp.ProjectSecretID(fugaroProject, logical)` is `internal/backend/gcp/names.go:154`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: #343's machinery unchanged (the containers, the precedence, `ResolvedSecretID`). Produces: a new default in `secretsOptions` (`scope` derived from the flags), and the same store/read paths.

1. **Scope resolution becomes explicit and defaults to project.** `fugaro secrets set <name>` with neither flag stores the **project** copy; `--repo` (or `--repo <slug>`) stores the repository copy and is kept until phase 3, where a repository copy loses its job to mount it. `--project-wide` stays as an accepted synonym of the default so existing scripts and the skills do not break, and `--repo` + `--project-wide` stay mutually exclusive (`:87`).
2. **The reserved names are swept in; the named Claude accounts keep their own rule, and it is already the right one** (D2; **finding 12 corrected the earlier draft here**). `anthropic-api-key`, the git credentials (`config.ReservedSecrets`' names except the Claude OAuth namespace) and provider keys are eligible project-wide **without the per-name `allow_data_to` list** — `projectSecretRefusal` (`internal/cli/secrets.go:293`) no longer fires for them. The earlier draft said `claude-oauth-token-*` "keeps its refusal"; that is wrong in both directions. **On `main`, a *named* Claude account's token is already project-wide-eligible when `claude_accounts.<name>.allow_data_to` lists a repository** (`internal/cli/secrets.go:321-324`, the `ClaudeAccountBySecret` branch, refused through `projectSecretRefusal` when the list is empty), and only the **default** account's `claude-oauth-token` is refused by name (`claudeOAuthAccountRefusal`, `:202` — the earlier citation `:196` was the function above it). Phase 5 does not re-decide this: it **replaces** the fixture with person-owned secrets and references, so what this step must do is keep the named-account path working (it is the interim shape phase 5 generalises) and say in SECURITY.md that a named account's `allow_data_to` is the only gate until then. Task 5.1 step 1 is rewritten to match: it no longer claims the token "is refused project scope".
3. What "reaches every enrolled repository" means is written in the code's message and in SECURITY.md, in one sentence: **inside a shared job a project secret has no IAM boundary between repositories — the profile is the boundary** (design §4.1, §6.3), and `workflows.<n>.secrets` cannot be shared across a job's mounts (they become named project secrets on a profile, or stay on the per-repository path).
3b. **The gate D2 actually turns on, named here because the earlier draft never did (finding 13).** Three eligibility sites decide project scope, and the new default has to be right at all three rather than only in the flag's default:
   - **Mount eligibility** is `infra.ProjectSecretCandidate` (`internal/infra/spec.go:899-925`), which asks `config.AllowedForProjectSecret(lc.ProjectSecrets, logical, repo)` — **absent or empty means nobody** — then a named Claude account's `AllowsData`, then a provider's. D2 requires `ProjectSecretCandidate` to accept a **reserved** name with no list (today only `project_secrets` reaches it, and a reserved name that is not in `project_secrets` is refused), because the rules being swept in are exactly the reserved ones; this is the site the *plan* uses, so it is the one a wrong default silently breaks.
   - **The write path** is `secretsSetProjectWide` (`internal/cli/secrets.go:314-334`): reserved names need a non-empty `project_secrets.<name>.allow_data_to` today, provider keys are accepted unconditionally, named Claude accounts need their own list. Step 2 above is the change; this step names the function so an implementer edits it rather than the message that wraps it.
   - **The listing** is `projectSecretAllowed` (`:514-529`), which must answer the same question as `ProjectSecretCandidate` for `secrets ls --project-wide`'s ALLOWED column — its doc comment already says "the same answer the plan uses", and a D2 change that touches the first and not this one makes `ls` claim a scope the plan would not resolve.
   - **A workflow's own secret stays repository scope (finding 13).** `secrets set` with no flag defaults to project **only for a name that has a project-wide meaning**: a reserved secret, a provider key or a named Claude account. Anything else — a repository's own application secret — is refused project scope today (`:326`, *"has no project-wide meaning"*) and must keep today's repository-scope behaviour rather than being swept into the new default. The default is "project where project exists", not "project always".
   - **`--repo` is a string flag and the bare form needs `NoOptDefVal` (finding 13).** `--repo` is registered as `StringVar` (`internal/cli/secrets.go:75`), so `fugaro secrets set --repo` alone parses as `--repo ""` and cannot mean "this checkout's repository" without `cmd.Flags().Lookup("repo").NoOptDefVal = <the resolved slug>`. Step 1's "`--repo` (or `--repo <slug>`)" is only true once that is set; the test below covers the bare form.
   - **`secrets ls`'s default scope is stated, not implied (finding 13).** The listing follows the same default as `set` (project), with `--repo` for one repository's view, and its help text says which container each row shows, so the change of default is visible in the one command an operator runs to check it.
   - **"Every enrolled repository" is undefined before a policy exists (finding 13).** `allow_repos` lives in `config/policy/shared`, which an installation may not have published; the phrase in step 3 and in SECURITY.md therefore means "every repository a *published* policy admits" and, with no policy published, "every repository the local config knows" — the phase-1 legacy path, where a project secret reaches the repository's own job by the pre-D2 resolution. Write both halves; a sentence that says only "every enrolled repository" is unreadable on an installation that has published nothing, which is every installation on the day this ships.
4. `init --repo`'s secrets stage (`internal/cli/initsecrets.go`): a name the project already covers stops being asked for, which extends #343's 40i to the new default. **The prompt text says the value will be stored project-wide** unless `--repo` is passed, because the prompt is where a person learns the blast radius.
5. `SECURITY.md`: the D2 paragraph — the default, what it reverses, the profile as the boundary, and the data-policy gate's unchanged role.

**Tests:** `TestSecretsSetDefaultsToProjectScope`; `TestSecretsSetRepoFlagStoresTheRepoCopy`; `TestProjectWideIsStillAccepted`; `TestAnthropicKeyNeedsNoAllowDataToList`; `TestOAuthTokenStillRefusesProjectScope`; `TestInitRepoDoesNotAskForAProjectCoveredSecret`; `TestAllowDataToStillGatesProviderRoutes` (Review Focus 8, in `internal/gateway`); and finding 13's four: `TestProjectSecretCandidateAcceptsAReservedNameWithNoList` (`internal/infra`), `TestSecretsSetBareRepoUsesThisCheckout` (the `NoOptDefVal` half), `TestSecretsSetKeepsRepoScopeForAWorkflowSecret` (a repository's own application secret is still refused project scope and stores per repository), `TestSecretsLsDefaultsToProjectAndSaysSo`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... ./internal/gateway/... -count=1`. **Commit:** "secrets: project scope is the default (D2)".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestSecretsSetDefaultsToProjectScope`, `TestSecretsSetRepoFlagStoresTheRepoCopy`, `TestProjectWideIsStillAccepted` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. ### Task 2.2: a project-scope key mounts on the repository's job until phase 3

**Files:** `internal/infra/spec.go` (+ `secretmounts_test.go`), `internal/infra/readiness.go` (+ tests).

**Verified today:** `SecretMounts` (`internal/infra/spec.go:585-600`) takes the logical name and resolves the secret ID elsewhere (`Inputs.Secrets`, `:273`, and `ProjectSecrets`/`ProjectSecretCandidates` from #343, `:287-291`); `SecretID`/`ProjectSecretID` are `internal/backend/gcp/names.go:143-154`.

1. A name that resolves to the **project** copy mounts the project's secret ID on the repository's job (`gcp.ProjectSecretID`), which #343 already supports; this task makes it the *default* resolution rather than the opted-in one, and pins it with a test that the mounted ID is the project's when no repository copy exists.
2. Readiness: a project-covered name is never reported missing (`internal/infra/readiness.go`'s `resolveProject`, `:100-158`), and the fix line names `fugaro secrets set <name>` (now project scope) rather than `--repo`. **"Project-covered" is `infra.ProjectSecretCandidate` (`internal/infra/spec.go:899-925`), the same function Task 2.1 step 3b changes** (finding 13): this task reads the answer, it does not re-decide it, and the test below asserts the two agree — a mount that `readiness.go` calls covered must be the one `SecretMounts` resolves to the project's ID, or a job deploys pointing at a secret that is never mounted.
3. The phase-3 hand-off, stated in the file's comment: when the repository stops having its own job (phase 3), the same resolution feeds a **profile's** mount list instead, and the repository copy's precedence survives only on the per-repository path.

**Tests:** `TestProjectSecretMountsOnTheRepoJobWithoutARepoCopy`, `TestRepoCopyStillWins`, `TestReadinessNamesTheProjectScopeFix`.
