## Phase 3 — shared project jobs for clone workflows (eight tasks; release **0.9.0**, R6)

The design's §4 and §4.1. **This is the phase that removes per-repository provisioning for clone workflows**, and it is the phase that must not break the ones that have not moved yet: a legacy per-repository job keeps the phase-1 contract (Task 3.3 step 1), and **D13 was ruled on 2026-10-15 (R1)**: the budget token's exposure under the broadened `runs/` grant is **accepted and documented in SECURITY.md beside D4**, Task 3b.1 designs the replacement channel, and repositories that must stay apart use separate profiles meanwhile — there is no gate left here. **It is also the phase that states a regression**: one job service account per (profile, provider, account) means the bucket conditions can no longer name one slug, and a run — an untrusted agent, unlike a launcher — could poison another repository's cache, forge or steal a lock, or rewrite another repository's queued `task.json` (design §4.1's table; `SECURITY.md:36` lists exactly those among the properties 0.7.0 gained). D4 accepts it and documents it. Terraform today lives at `deploy/terraform/gcp/{roots,modules}` — `roots/{installation,firebase,repo}` and `modules/{installation,firebase,repo,workflow}` — and a repository's job, its service account and its bucket conditions come from `modules/workflow/{job.tf,sa.tf}`, with the launcher's `run.jobs.runWithOverrides` role in `modules/installation/iam.tf:26-31` and `modules/workflow/job.tf:93-102`.

### Task 3.0: the four UNVERIFIED probes, written and answered first — OWNER-RUN, and a go/no-go — Check 52

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`.

Design §13 lists what was never settled from the code, and this phase is the one that needs four of them. **They are answered before any phase 3 code is written**, because one of them decides whether the phase is shaped the way the design says: the Secret Manager IAM question decides whether a runtime fetch is even a fallback (design §4.5, step 4 below). **The Bitbucket question is not run as a probe: the owner ruled it on 2026-10-15 — "probe U1: cannot be scoped"** — and the ruling settles it in the direction the design's §4.5 already names for a failed verification (*"If the verification fails, Bitbucket projects stay on the per-repository path and the owner is told before phase 3 starts"*), so the check records the ruling as a FACT in a step with no commands and **no step of it creates or handles a credential**. Append `## Check 52: the four probes phase 3 depends on (installation fugaro, sandbox only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row, with **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1) and these steps:

**What no step of this check may touch, stated before the steps** (R2-6; round-1 findings 22 and 31, escalated — **this is the `belong` safety rule of the whole plan, and it is not negotiable**): **no agent creates a credential, no agent runs a live step, and no step of this check creates a token for the `edgeappinc` workspace or probes any repository that is not a scratch repository created for the probe** — and today no step does either, because U1 is answered by ruling and the check creates nothing. `edgeappinc` is the owner's production workspace: it holds `edgeserver` and `edgeweb`, which the `belong` project runs, and **no agent and no probe ever touches them** — the migration of `belong` is the owner's own work after the model is proven on `fugaro` (Task 4.5). The earlier draft's "a Bitbucket **workspace** access token for the sandbox workspace … plus `git ls-remote` against a repository **outside** the intended scope" is exactly the reach that must not happen: a workspace token in that workspace is a token over production repositories, and asking what it can reach asks by using it. The scope is therefore split, and the split is the point — one side of it is now empty (U1 needs no probe, and this check creates no credential) and the rule it carried still stands:

- **GCP-side probes run in installation `fugaro`'s own project** (`fugaro-dev`, the guard's hard-coded `P`), because that is the project the model is about and the answers must transfer to it (the service-account quota, the bucket policy, the IAM condition, the secret reference). They act on the sandbox's **own** job, service account, bucket and secrets — never on another installation's.
- **There is no credential- or repository-scope probe at all, and that is the ruling's other half.** U1 — the one question that would have needed a token — is **answered by the owner's ruling (§2 of the check)**, so this task's check creates **no token, workspace, project or scratch repository and reads or writes no Bitbucket repository**. Had it stayed open, that probe's scope would have been scratch repositories created for it, in a **dedicated scope holding no production repository** — a Bitbucket **project** access token on a project the owner creates for the probe, or a **scratch workspace** holding only probe repositories — and never a production workspace, `edgeappinc` in particular. **The answer the earlier draft held out as the cheap way out is the answer the owner ruled**: "cannot be scoped", so Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path and no token ever points at production.

1. **Setup and guard** — Check 43's block verbatim (Task 0.4 step 1), plus the recorded state (the sandbox job's env, its service account and its bucket condition; **the kind of any credential the owner already holds**, named by kind only). The guard's three stops are what make "sandbox checkout, `fugaro-dev`, nothing else" true before a single `gcloud` line runs.
2. **U1 — answered by the owner's ruling; this step has no commands, and the check writes the ruling as its FACT.** **OWNER RULING, 2026-10-15 — "probe U1: cannot be scoped": a Bitbucket credential cannot be scoped to one project's repositories.** That is the branch the design's §4.5 already names for a failed verification, so **Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path** and each repository keeps its own `bitbucket-token`; **the token, the project, the scratch repositories and the `git ls-remote` reach the earlier draft ordered are not run at all** — no agent creates a credential, and no step of this check touches Bitbucket. **What the ruling does to the phase, in the same words the checklist carries (§2 of the check):** a shared job holds one credential for every repository it serves, so **phase 3's shared project jobs serve GitHub repositories**; the provider is a field of a profile, so a Bitbucket profile is simply one no repository is moved onto, **plan Tasks 3.1 to 3.7 proceed as written**, and the one step the ruling lands in is **Task 3.7's live check** — its Bitbucket half (step 2 there, whose whole question was the reach of a project-wide token a shared job would hold) has no object any more, so Check 53 runs the GitHub path and its FACT says so. **The fallback the design names** — a runtime fetch by secret-name prefix with an IAM condition — is **not** adopted by the ruling: it reintroduces the shared-service-account exposure of §4.1, so it stays a decision for later, and **step 4 below (U3)** says only whether it is even available. **The two design records that still carry the pre-ruling text** — D5's row (*"Bitbucket first-class in phase 3, via a project-wide Bitbucket access token after a verification task"*) and §13's entry listing the token scope as UNVERIFIED — **are corrected by the D5 amendment this task adds to the plan's design-amendments list**, applied with the others by Task 0.5; the checklist says the same, so the two documents read one way.
3. **U2 — does a Cloud Run **secret reference** work inside a container env override?** The API type has a `ValueSource`. One **`POST …/jobs/<JOB>:run`** attempt on the sandbox job — **`gcloud run jobs execute` has no `--overrides` flag** (its container flags are `--args`/`--update-env-vars`), so the override exists only on the API’s `jobs.run`, which is the call the check spells (review I4/I5) — with a `ContainerOverride` env var whose value is a **secret reference**, one `gcloud run jobs update --update-secrets` attempt that sets the same reference in the job’s template, and one plain execution with no override at all for comparison. **The probe’s lines are spelled here, not left to the implementer (finding 30 — a security-relevant probe whose command is "ask the API somehow" is not a check)**, and they name the *real* secret-reference surface (round-6 review: the earlier draft’s `--update-env-vars="PROBE_REF=sm://…"` creates no `ContainerOverride` at all and stores the literal string `sm://…` in the environment, which answers a question the phase does not ask):

   - **The execution-level override** — `POST https://run.googleapis.com/v2/projects/${P:?}/locations/${L:?}/jobs/${SANDBOX_JOB:?}:run`, with the body `{"overrides":{"containerOverrides":[{"env":[{"name":"PROBE_REF","valueSource":{"secretKeyRef":{"secret":"${PROBE_IN:?}","version":"latest"}}}]}]}}` (the checklist spells it as a `curl -sS -X POST` that takes the access token from a command substitution, so it is never printed or stored). This is the override Cloud Run merges over the job’s template for that execution alone, and it is the shape a launcher could write (D10). **Its response is an Operation**, so the execution’s name is read from the operation’s own `metadata.name` — never from a `--limit=1` listing, which a concurrent run can race (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go:103-107`, `op.Metadata.Name`) — and **a refusal is an answer, not a stop**: the API’s error body is `{"error":{code,message,status}}` (the status is *inside* `.error`, so a top-level `.status` reads null), its message is the FACT, and the steps below still run. The same is done for the run **response with no operation name at all** (a transport or parse failure): it is recorded and the check goes on, rather than stopping on `${OPERATION:?}`.
   - **The template-level reference** — `gcloud run jobs update "${SANDBOX_JOB:?}" --project="${P:?}" --region="${L:?}" --update-secrets="PROBE_REF=${PROBE_IN:?}:latest"`. `--set-secrets`/`--update-secrets` is the CLI’s secret-reference flag; `--update-env-vars` is not one, and `sm://` is not Cloud Run syntax. **This changes the Terraform-managed job’s template, so it is drift until the Restore removes it (review M6)**: do not run `fugaro init --repo` between here and the Restore.
   - **The comparison run** — the same call with `{"name":"PROBE_REF","value":"plain-literal"}` instead of `valueSource`, so the probe also shows what the override looks like when it carries a value rather than a reference. **Its own response is printed too** (the API’s error, or the operation), because what the comparison does is one of the step’s FACT inputs.

   `P` and `L` come from the guard of step 1 (`P` hard-coded to `fugaro-dev`), `SANDBOX_JOB` is the sandbox installation’s own, and the two secrets are **the check’s own throwaway pair** (`fugaro-probe-in`, inside U3’s prefix, and `fugaro-other-probe`, outside it — never a real secret and never a value any Fugaro job uses); every variable is `${VAR:?}` so an empty one stops the line rather than pointing the command at a default project. **FACT:** whether the override is accepted and whether the value arrives (the length read back, never the value); **and the D10 counterpart — whether an execution’s own overrides are visible to the container** (the runner already reads its execution’s template for `FUGARO_RUN`, `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:420-430`; what is unverified is whether the *override* is what the template reports). **An execution these calls start is expected to end as a *failed* run, and that is not the probe’s answer**: the base image’s `CMD ["fugaro", "exec"]` runs the run `FUGARO_RUN` names, and only the launcher sets that (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go:82-95`), so a bare execution refuses with `run "" must look like <repo-slug>/<run-id>` (`internal/cli/exec.go:60`, `:171`) — the failure of these executions’ own tasks is recorded as the expected outcome, not as the answer. **This step’s answer is what the *phase-3* text in SECURITY.md’s launcher-override paragraph is allowed to add, and nothing more** (the paragraph itself lands in phase 1, Task 1.5 step 5, from Check 49 step 6’s evidence — finding 14): if the override is visible, the text says so; if it is not, the text says only that the API merges overrides and the runner cannot see them.
4. **U3 — Secret Manager IAM conditions on a name prefix.** **Spelled out for the same reason as U2 (finding 30), and in the shape the check actually runs (review C1, C2):** the condition names Secret Manager resources by **project number**, never by project ID (`projects/<NUMBER>/secrets/<id>` — a condition written with `projects/fugaro-dev/...` never matches, and the owner would record a false "not honoured"), and it is placed on the **project** (`gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding "${P:?}" --member="serviceAccount:${PROBE_SA:?}" --role="roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor" --condition="${PROBE_COND:?}"`), not on one secret — a condition on a single secret proves nothing about a read of another, which is the out-of-prefix half of the probe. The condition’s `resource.type` term accepts **both** Secret Manager types (`secretmanager.googleapis.com/Secret` **and** `…/SecretVersion`), because `secretmanager.versions.access` is evaluated on a **SecretVersion**: a term naming only `…/Secret` would deny the inside read and the owner would record a false "not honoured"; the type term is the one part that rests on documentation, so the check re-issues the grant without it before any denial is recorded. The two reads run **as a dedicated probe service account the check creates** (`${PROBE_SA:?}`, with `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` granted to the owner for the impersonation), never as the sandbox job’s own account, and read only the check’s two throwaway secrets; the inside read must **succeed** after a poll window (IAM propagation) and the outside read must be **denied**, and only once the inside read has succeeded. **FACT:** the two exit statuses, **which condition was in place when the inside read succeeded** (`with-type` or `no-type` — a success only under `no-type` says the type string was what failed, not the name prefix), whether the condition is honoured for `secretmanager.versions.access`, and what the denial looks like. If it works, it is a *possibility* for the design’s §4.5 fallback and for phase 5’s per-person grants; if not, both designs say so.
5. **U4 — the quota and bucket-policy ceilings** (design §13): Google's default service-account quota (about 100 per project) and the runs bucket's IAM policy size at scale. **FACT:** the sandbox project's current service-account count against its quota, and the sandbox bucket's policy binding count. The current model exceeds the SA quota at about 60 repositories and the project-job model does not — this step is what records that the sandbox still has headroom.
6. **Restore**: the job’s template back as step 1 recorded it (the reference U2(b) put in it removed, and the template diffed against the baseline before and after — over `.spec.template`, since the two `gcloud run jobs update` calls bump `metadata.generation`), the project-level conditional grant removed (with whichever condition is bound), the token-creator grant removed, the probe service account deleted, the check’s two throwaway secrets deleted, the mode-700 scratch directory removed, and one ordinary sandbox task confirming the sandbox still runs. **Nothing in it creates or revokes a credential, and there is nothing to restore on the Bitbucket side**: U1 is answered by ruling (§2), so there is no scratch repository to delete, no project to remove and no token to revoke. The Restore’s evidence is the `--plan-only` run in the sandbox checkout as well as the diff.

End with: "**Paste back:** every FACT line above, never a token, key or secret value. **Never run this against `belong`, never create a `edgeappinc` workspace token, and never point a probe at edgeserver or edgeweb** — and the check names no repository at all today: U1, the one question that would have needed a scratch one, is answered by the owner's ruling (§2)." **A step that creates a credential, or probes a repository this check did not create, stops the check and is reported to the owner** — not fixed in place. **A step that fails does not stop the phase silently**: it is reported to the owner and the affected task is re-scoped in the same PR, in writing.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck52`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 52` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint|ChecklistHasCheck52' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck52`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). **The pattern names the test (round-7 review), and it has to**: `-run` selects a test by its *name*, and `TestChecklistHasCheck52` holds neither `Docs` nor `Lint` — the bare docs pattern reports `ok` without ever running it, so the red step would be green for the one reason a red step must not be green, and a green first run is exactly what this step exists to disbelieve. The name itself stays as it is: `TestChecklistHasCheckNN` is the convention the plan and `docs/design/compute-backend.md` fix (`TestChecklistHasCheck33` and `TestChecklistHasCheck40` carry it), so the command names the test rather than the test renaming itself to match a pattern. Every other `TestChecklistHasCheckNN` in this plan (48 to 54, the red steps of Tasks 0.4, 1.7, 1b.4, 2.4, 3.7 and 4.5) has to be selected the same way when it is written, by its own name. Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

### Task 3.1: profiles — the policy's shape and the workflow's selection

**Files:** `internal/projectpolicy/{policy.go,profiles.go}` (+ tests), `internal/config/{config.go,validate.go}` (+ tests), `internal/infra/spec.go` (the workflow's `profile`), `internal/cli/project.go` (`policy publish`).

**Interfaces.** Consumes: Task 1.1's `Policy`. Produces: `projectpolicy.Profile{Provider, CPU, Memory, TimeoutClass string, Secrets []string, AllowRepos []string}` and `Policy.Profiles map[string]Profile`, plus `config.Workflow.Profile string` defaulting to `standard`.

1. `profiles` in the policy: the shareable unit is "a job with these properties" (design §4.1). `standard` must exist; a policy that names a profile in `workflows.<n>.profile` and does not define it is refused **at publish time** and at runner start (fail closed, the Task 1.3 path).
2. **A profile carries its provider, and it is required** (finding 4; a **design amendment**, see that section). One Cloud Run job cannot serve a GitHub repository and a Bitbucket repository at once, because the provider is part of the slug (`task.Slug(provider, repo)`, `internal/task/task.go:187`), part of the job's identity and part of the git credential it holds — so "one job per (profile, account)" only means anything once a profile pins which provider it serves. The job key is therefore **(profile, provider, account)** — one provider per profile, settled (R2-11) — and Task 3.2 encodes the provider in the job's own name. The plan writes the **triple** everywhere for this reason, including the helper's signature: `jobIdentity()` returns `(profile, provider, account)`, not `(profile, account)`, so an implementer cannot satisfy the plan's text and the job's name with two different cardinalities (R2-11). A project with repositories on both providers defines two profiles (`standard`, `standard-bitbucket`) or two sets; the default `standard` names one provider, and a GitHub-only project never notices. The workflow's `profile` selects the profile, and a repository whose provider the profile does not serve is refused at admission (Task 3.3) with a message naming both.
3. `allow_repos` on a profile is the admission list the runner enforces in Task 3.3, and the *project's* `allow_repos` stays the enrollment list of phase 1: an empty profile list means "the project's list applies", never "everything". A profile's `allow_repos` may only name repositories of its own provider — a pattern whose provider segment disagrees with the profile's is refused at publish time, because it could never match.
4. `cpu`/`memory`/`timeout` are validated against Cloud Run's accepted forms (the same shapes Check 4 already exercised: `internal/backend/gcp/limits.go`), so a policy cannot name a job the API will refuse.
5. `policy publish` writes the profiles; `project show` prints them with the jobs each maps to.
6. **A v1 policy node is migrated by hand, and there is no migration code (owner ruling 2026-10-17).** If this phase's shape raises `projectpolicy.Version` above 1, the operator runs `fugaro project policy publish --delete` (the flag at `internal/cli/project_cmd.go`) and then republishes; the runner and the launcher keep refusing a `version` they do not know with P8's messages, and the message names this two-step fix. A test pins the message, not a migration.

**Tests:** `TestPublishRefusesAnUndefinedProfile`; `TestPublishRefusesAProfileWithoutAProvider`; `TestPublishRefusesAnAllowRepoPatternOfAnotherProvider`; `TestProfileDefaultsToStandard`; `TestProfileAdmissionFallsBackToTheProjectList`; `TestProfileRefusesAnInvalidResourceForm`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/projectpolicy/... ./internal/config/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 1.1, 1.6. **Commit:** "projectpolicy: profiles, and a workflow's selection of one".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestPublishRefusesAnUndefinedProfile`, `TestPublishRefusesAProfileWithoutAProvider`, `TestPublishRefusesAnAllowRepoPatternOfAnotherProvider` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 3.2: the project job module and the broadened bucket IAM — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/project/{job.tf,sa.tf,iam.tf,variables.tf,outputs.tf}` (new), `deploy/terraform/gcp/roots/installation/main.tf` (+ `variables.tf`), `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/installation/{iam.tf,bucket.tf,history.tf,outputs.tf}`, `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/installation/variables.tf`, `internal/backend/gcp/names.go` (+ `names_test.go`: `ProjectJobName` **only** — there is no `ParseProjectJobName`, R2-5 and step 1), `internal/infra/tf/testdata/golden/*.plan.json`, `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh`, `internal/infra/generated_root_test.go`, `SECURITY.md`, `docs/design/bucket-iam.md`.

**The token exposure is ruled, not open (finding 6; D13, ruled 2026-10-15).** This task changes the `runs/` bucket grant, and that grant also exposes `runs/<slug>/<run>/budget-token` (`internal/budget/token/object.go:15`) — another queued run's RTDB identity. **The owner accepted that on 2026-10-15 (R1)**: the module ships the grant with the exposure documented in SECURITY.md beside D4, Task 3b.1 designs the channel that removes it, and repositories that must not share a budget identity use separate profiles meanwhile. There is **no gate**: this task's steps 2 and 5 are the accepted shape, and the per-profile-isolation alternative the review raised is the mitigation the ruling names, not a shape to wait for. The task still names the decision explicitly, so the reader sees why the condition cannot be narrowed to a profile prefix yet.

**Verified today:** a workflow's job service account, its per-slug bucket grant and its secret grants are `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/workflow/sa.tf:5,24-33,36-43`; the job is `.../workflow/job.tf:15` (deletion protection) with the launcher's `job_runner` grant at `:94-102`; the launcher role's permissions are `.../modules/installation/iam.tf:26-31` (`run.jobs.run`, `run.jobs.runWithOverrides`); the history job's whole-bucket read is `.../modules/installation/history.tf:163-169`; the bucket prefixes and the condition builder are `internal/backend/gcp/names.go:374` (`JobBucketPrefixes`) and `:383` (`BucketCondition`), with the launcher's own condition at `:424` (`LauncherBucketCondition`).

1. The new module creates, per (profile, provider, account) — the provider being the one the profile pins (Task 3.1 step 2) — **one Cloud Run job** (the base image, the profile's cpu/memory/timeout, the profile's secret mounts, deletion protection as today) and **one job service account** with the bucket grant, the secret grants for the profile's mounts and (under Vertex) the same optional grant as today. The job's env carries what phase 1 left in env and nothing per-repository: `FUGARO_RUN` (an override at launch), the timeout, `FUGARO_RTDB_URL`, `FUGARO_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, `FUGARO_PROJECT`, `FUGARO_BUCKET`, **`FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT` and `FUGARO_REGION`** (B5's companion item — and they are not optional, because Task 3.3's whole identity rests on them: `backend.ExecutionFromEnv` reads `FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT`, `FUGARO_REGION` and `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` and **errors** when the first two are unset (`internal/backend/backend.go:92-104`, "on Cloud Run the job must set FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT and FUGARO_REGION"), and the provisioning rollback path already tells an owner that a job lacking `FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT` was set up before M9a (`internal/cli/exec.go:251-252`). `FUGARO_PROJECT` is the Fugaro project's *name* and `FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT` is the GCP project **ID** — two different values, and the shared job needs both: the first keys the config, the second is where the execution lives, which is what `fugaro logs`/`ls`/`cancel` resolve against. A project job that dropped either would fail Task 3.3's `jobIdentity` at its first line, on every run. **The provider is not an env var** (finding 4; the earlier draft put it there and that was wrong — `$FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER` is exactly the launcher-overridable source 12a item 2 forbids, `internal/cli/exec.go:69`, and a launcher can override any env var through `run.jobs.runWithOverrides`, `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/installation/iam.tf:30`, `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:108-112`). It is **encoded in the job's own name**, which Cloud Run sets and no launcher writes: `gcp.ProjectJobName(profile, provider, account)` → a stable, readable, hashed name of the shape `fugaro-p-<profile>-<provider>-<account>-<hash>`, built with `derive` like `JobName` (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:130`) so the 63-character limit and the collision behaviour are the same. **There is no `ParseProjectJobName`, and the earlier draft's "the parse is the inverse" was not implementable** (R2-5): `derive` truncates the readable part (`:101-112`), profile names hold hyphens, an api-key job's account segment is empty, and `fugaro-p-` is also the legacy stem of an owner named `p` (`stem`, `:122-124`) — so the name is written, never read back. The runner holds the (profile, provider, account) set the policy defines, **computes every name and matches** `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` exactly (Task 3.3 step 1); the tests here assert the name function's own three properties: it is **stable** (same triple ⇒ same name), it is **distinct** over a table of triples that a shape test would confuse (a hyphenated profile, an empty account, a long profile, a triple whose readable part is truncated), and a legacy `fugaro-<slug>-<wf>` name is **never equal to** any computed project name for the fixture policy. The name's length limit (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:130`'s `derive` hashing) is what the hash segment is for.
2. **The bucket condition loses the slug**: one condition per prefix (`runs/`, `cache/`, `locks/`), built by a new `ProjectBucketCondition(bucket, prefixes)` beside `BucketCondition` (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:383`) so the old per-slug builder stays for the per-repository path until Task 4.4 drops it. **SECURITY.md and `docs/design/bucket-iam.md` state the regression in the words D4 chose**: it is a loss of a documented 0.7.0 property, a run could poison another repository's cache (cross-repository code execution on its next run), forge or steal a lock, or rewrite a queued `task.json`, and **repositories that must stay apart use different profiles** (a profile is a job, so a profile boundary is a job-service-account boundary). **Two things the D4 paragraph must not omit because they are not in D4's accepted list** (finding 6): the same prefix exposes **another queued run's budget token** (`runs/<slug>/<run>/budget-token`, `internal/budget/token/object.go:15`) — which is an RTDB identity, not a cache entry — and it lets a run **rewrite another run's `launch.json`/`result.json`**, which is the reason `CheckRunExecution` exists at all (`internal/backend/gcp/run.go:88-91`, Task 3.4). The first was an owner decision, **D13, ruled on 2026-10-15 (R1): accepted, documented in SECURITY.md beside D4, with Task 3b.1 designing the channel that replaces it and separate profiles as the interim boundary** — this step therefore states it as an accepted regression, not as provisional text, and there is no gate. The second is handled by Task 3.4 and stated in SECURITY.md here.
3. Launcher and operator grants move from per (repository, workflow) to per project job (`modules/project/iam.tf`), with the same `job_runner` role and the same `run.jobs.runWithOverrides` permission — nothing widens: every launcher already holds every job (design §4.1's table, "Nothing: every launcher already holds every job").
4. The history job and its sweep (`.../modules/installation/history.tf:174-253`) are unchanged (it already reads the whole bucket), and the per-project base-image check is unchanged: clone repositories need no check (`internal/infra/spec.go:846`), the inert per-repository registry stays until Task 4.2.
5. **Golden plans** (`internal/infra/tf/testdata/golden/`): regenerate with `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh` (it runs `terraform show -json` offline with a fake token and a dead proxy, and derives the `*-adopt` variants with `jq`) and add the new root's plan. **The test that underpins the whole phase is a golden test asserting the project root's resource set**: one job and one SA per (profile, provider, account), three bucket conditions, no per-slug condition. A reviewer reads the golden diff, not the HCL.
6. **The size claim the design makes** (design §13: the current model exceeds the default service-account quota at about 60 repositories, the project-job model does not) gets a test: the golden plan's SA count is asserted to be a function of the profiles and accounts, not of the repository count.

**Command:** the repository's Terraform checks through `fugaro verify test` (the golden-plan test is a Go test: `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/infra/... -count=1`), plus `terraform validate`/`plan` for the new module **offline** (no apply, no credentials — the generator's own `env -i` pattern). **Depends on:** 3.0 (a probe failure can re-scope this), 3.1, 1.6. **Commit:** "tf: one project job and job service account per (profile, provider, account)".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16), and this is the security-sensitive task that needed one most.** Write the golden test first: assert the project root's resource set — **one job and one job service account per (profile, provider, account), three bucket conditions, no per-slug condition** — and run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/infra/... -count=1` **before** `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh` regenerates anything: `Expected: FAIL` (the golden has today's per-repository resources). Regenerate the plan, re-run: `Expected: PASS`. The second half is the IAM assertion the phase turns on: a test that the job SA's bucket grant is the **widened** one and that no condition names a single slug — `Expected: FAIL` until the module changes. Both are behaviour assertions over generated HCL, not `terraform plan` snapshots a reviewer has to eyeball.

### Task 3.3: the runner's binding, the remote and the admission — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/runner/{projectjob.go (new),runner.go,provision.go,project.go}` (+ tests), `internal/backend/backend.go` (the accessor the runner uses), `internal/runner/{lockcache.go,budget_session.go}` (the ordering comment).

**Verified today:** the runner clones from `r.d.Remote` (`internal/runner/runner.go:789-794`; `internal/runner/provision.go:30-44` is `RemoteEnv = "FUGARO_REMOTE"`, set at `internal/infra/spec.go:669` only for `checkout: clone`); the slug arrives in `FUGARO_RUN` (`internal/cli/exec.go:157`,`:168`); `task.json` carries repository, ref, task, workflow, account and layer but **no provider** (`internal/task/task.go:45-79`); the token is minted for `spec.Repo` (`internal/runner/runner.go:586`); `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is read by `backend.ExecutionFromEnv` (`internal/backend/backend.go:96-104`), wired at `internal/cli/exec.go:134` — **the design's §12a item 1 is wrong that the runner reads it directly today**; this task makes the derivation real.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: 3.1's profiles, 1.1's `MatchRepo`, `task.Slug` (`internal/task/task.go:179`), `gcp.ProjectJobName` and `gcp.JobName` (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:130`). Produces: `func (r *run) jobIdentity() (profile, provider, account string, err error)` — the **triple**, not the pair (R2-11) — and the extended `admissionCheck` of Task 1.5.

1. **The identity is computed and matched, never parsed** (R2-5 — the earlier draft's `gcp.ParseProjectJobName`, "the inverse" of the name, cannot be implemented and is gone). A name like `fugaro-p-<profile>-<provider>-<account>-<hash>` is not invertible: `derive` truncates the readable part to Cloud Run's 63-character limit (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:101-112`), which can cut the account or the hash off; profile names contain hyphens (the plan's own example is `standard-bitbucket`); the account segment is empty for an api-key job; and `fugaro-p-` is *also* the legacy stem of any repository whose owner is `p` (`stem` = `fugaro-<owner>-<name>-<wf>`, `names.go:122-124`), so a shape test would misclassify a legacy job as a project job and halt it. `jobIdentity` therefore **computes the candidate names and matches `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` against them exactly** (12a item 1, item 2, finding 4 — the job name is still the strongest identifier available: Cloud Run sets it, a launcher cannot write it, and it is what the execution's own `job` field reports, `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:420-430`). Three outcomes, in this order:
   - **A project job.** `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` equals `gcp.ProjectJobName(profile, provider, account)` for some (profile, provider, account) in the **set the published policy defines** — every profile's own provider with its default account, plus the non-default accounts of phase 5. The match *is* the identity: the triple the name was computed from is what the run adopts, so the provider is the one the profile pins (Task 3.1 step 2) and the account is the one whose service account the job runs as. The profile must exist in the policy (**halt** with a message naming the job and the policy if it does not — a job created by an older module run against a newer policy would otherwise run with no admission at all).
   - **A legacy job.** No project job matched, **and** `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` equals `gcp.JobName(slug, variant)` recomputed from the slug the run has bound (Task 1.5) and the workflow in the run's own `task.json` — **`variant` is `gcp.WorkflowVariant(spec.Workflow, spec.OAuthToken)`, never the bare `spec.Workflow`** (B2), and an empty `spec.Workflow` is refused before the variant is computed rather than reaching `WorkflowVariant`, which **panics** on a name outside `config.WorkflowNameRE` (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:300-302`). This is not a hypothetical: the deployed job of a named OAuth run is `JobName(slug, WorkflowVariant(wf, token))` — `internal/infra/spec.go:834` is where the deploy calls it — so recomputing with the plain workflow would **fail to match the repository's own job** and halt it. `internal/cli/cloud.go:271-300` is the shipped precedent and says so in its own comment: comparing against the plain workflow "would refuse every one of its own executions" and did break `--run-id` repeats, `ls`, `logs`, `diagnose` and follow-up for every run that used `--token` or `agent.oauth_token`. The same helper shape is reused here (`jobWorkflow`'s `recover()`-guarded wrapper, `:293-304`), because `task.Spec.Workflow` is never shape-checked: `config.WorkflowNameRE` constrains `fugaro.yaml`'s keys, not what reaches the spec. ⇒ **the phase-1 path, unchanged**: the slug binding and `allow_repos` (Task 1.5), **no profile admission, no halt**, whether or not a policy is published. This is the correction that matters most: phase 3's runner goes out to every installation at once, while repositories migrate one at a time (phase 4) and baked workflows stay per-repository until then (D3). A runner that halted "a job name that maps to no profile" would halt every per-repository job on every installation that has published a policy — including `belong` — and would make phase 3 **unmergeable**, contradicting nothing less than the audit's own claim that per-repository jobs keep working. A legacy job keeps working *because the recomputed name matches*, and `doctor` (Task 1.6 step 4) is what tells the owner which jobs are still legacy.
   - **Neither ⇒ halt.** A name that matches no computed project job and is not this run's legacy `JobName` is a job the runner cannot place: it may be another installation's, a stale name from a module the policy no longer describes, or a hand-created job — none of which the run may adopt an identity from. The message names `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, the bound slug and the workflow, and the fix is `fugaro doctor`/`fugaro init --firebase` with the current version. **The whole step is scoped to runs that have a Cloud Run execution** (R2-9): it runs only when `backend.ExecutionFromEnv` returns a non-empty id, so a `docker run`, a local invocation and `fugaro exec --provider fake` (the e2e path, `internal/cli/exec.go:134`) never reach it — there is no job identity to check, and that is why the `fake` provider is not refused (Task 1.5 step 2). **An empty `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` on Cloud Run does not land here either** (round-6 review: the earlier draft said it did, "which is correct" — it is not): `ExecutionFromEnv` already refuses it at `internal/backend/backend.go:100-102` ("on Cloud Run the job must set `FUGARO_GCP_PROJECT` and `FUGARO_REGION`…"), before `jobIdentity` is called, so "neither" means a **non-empty** name that matches nothing.
   The set is computed **once**, from one policy read (memoized, Task 1.3 step 5), and its size is the plan's own cost note: a project with N profiles and M accounts computes N×M names and compares strings — no regex, no prefix test, no ambiguity between `p` the owner and `p-` the project marker.
2. **The slug is bound, and the provider comes from the job, not the env** (finding 4). On a project job the provider is the one the matched candidate carries — that is, the provider the profile pins (step 1) — `FUGARO_GIT_PROVIDER` is never consulted, and a `--provider` flag that disagrees is refused, because both are launcher-writable. On a legacy job, phase 1's derivation stands (Task 1.5 step 2: the provider kind whose `task.Slug` of the repository equals the slug). Then `slug == task.Slug(provider, spec.Repo)`, and the check runs **before the call to `openProvider` at `internal/runner/runner.go:833-849`** — not "before `:586`", which is that function's definition — and before the budget session takes a lease (`internal/runner/budget_session.go:180`). The phase-1 ordering check (Task 1.5 step 1) already put this before the token; this task extends it to the profile case and keeps the same place.
3. **The remote comes from data, not from the job env**: the clone URL is computed from `task.json`'s repository, the job's provider and the policy (a custom clone URL lives in the policy, design §4.1(1)), reusing `internal/infra/spec.go:454`'s `repoURL` logic moved to a shared helper so the deploy-time and run-time answers cannot differ. `FUGARO_REMOTE` stops being read on a project job; `provision.go:30-44`'s constant stays for the per-repository path until Task 4.4.
4. **Admission** (design §4.1), **on project jobs**: the runner refuses a repository the profile's `allow_repos` does not admit, for the (profile, provider, account) it is actually running on — so the repository's `fugaro.yaml` selection of a profile cannot widen its own access, and the launch check stays advisory (§4.2). One function, one message, shared with the CLI's advisory twin (`internal/cli/run.go`, Task 1.5 step 4). A repository whose provider the profile does not serve is refused here with a message naming both (Task 3.1 step 2).
5. `task.json`'s repository is *not* trusted to name the slug: it is the input the binding compares, and the comparison is what makes the bucket condition's absence survivable.
6. **The GitHub App becomes the project's, not the repository's — and its key never becomes part of the policy node** (design §4.1(3), §4.5): the project names one App, and the runner mints a token per repository from it, so a project is tied to one App and a project with several Apps cannot share jobs (SECURITY.md says so; `SECURITY.md:48` already tells users to use one App per repository if that matters). **The private key stays a Secret Manager secret**: `github-app-key` (`internal/config/config.go:387`) mounted through `internal/infra/spec.go:555`, and on a shared job it is granted to the project job service accounts by Task 3.2's module exactly as the per-repository path grants it today. **It is never a field of `config/policy/shared`**: every run token can read that node (P2) and 12a item 7 is precisely about not putting secret-adjacent data where a launcher's IAM read reaches it — a key in the policy would hand a prompt-injected agent the App's credentials for every enrolled repository. What the policy carries is the App's **ID and the secret's name**, the rule Task 1.6 step 2 already applies to provider keys ("the secret's name, never a value"); the ID is not secret-adjacent, and the runner reads it from the policy rather than from an env var, for the same reason Task 3.2 step 1 keeps the provider out of the env (finding 4) — an overridden App ID is not a privilege escalation, but it is a confusing failure the policy can simply prevent. The per-repository path keeps `github-app-key` as it is until Task 4.4, and `TestPolicyCarriesNoSecretValue` (Task 1.6) covers the App key's bytes like any other secret.

**Tests**, all written against the computed-and-matched rule (R2-5): `TestJobIdentityMatchesAComputedProjectJobName` (the triple is the one `ProjectJobName` was called with — **the round-trip the earlier draft tried to get by parsing**, now asserted by computing both sides), **`TestJobIdentityIsNotAParser`** (a table of names a shape test would misread: a two-hyphen profile `standard-bitbucket`, an api-key job with an empty account segment, a name truncated at the 63-character limit, and a **legacy job of a repository whose owner is `p`** — `fugaro-p-…` as `stem` builds it (`names.go:122-124`) — must **not** be taken for a project job; the same four names as genuine project jobs **must** match), `TestJobIdentityRefusesAnUnknownProjectJob` (a computed shape naming a profile the policy does not define ⇒ halt), `TestLegacyJobNameTakesThePhaseOnePath` (the recomputed `gcp.JobName(slug, gcp.WorkflowVariant(wf, ""))` with a policy published: binding and `allow_repos` only, **no halt** — the finding-5 regression test), `TestLegacyJobNameOfANamedOAuthRunMatches` (B2's regression test, and the analogue of the shipped `cloud.go` bug: `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is `JobName(slug, WorkflowVariant(wf, "acme-personal"))` and the run's `task.json` carries `workflow: wf`, `oauth_token: acme-personal` ⇒ the legacy path, **no halt**; a run that halts here fails with "a named OAuth run failed to match its own job"), `TestLegacyJobNameOfAnEmptyWorkflowIsRefused` (an empty `spec.Workflow` is refused with the "names no workflow" message, never passed to `WorkflowVariant`), `TestLegacyJobNameOfAnUnshapeableWorkflowIsRefused` (a workflow outside `config.WorkflowNameRE` is refused by the `recover()` guard rather than panicking the run), `TestJobIdentityRefusesANameItCannotPlace` (a **non-empty** `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` that is neither a computed project job nor this run's legacy name), `TestJobIdentityIsSkippedOutsideCloudRun` (no execution — `backend.ExecutionFromEnv` returns `""` — so the step does not run and a `--provider fake` run is not refused, R2-9), `TestExecutionFromEnvRefusesAnEmptyJobName` (on Cloud Run an empty `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is refused at `internal/backend/backend.go:100-102`, never reaching `jobIdentity`), `TestProjectJobOfAnotherProviderIsRefused`, `TestRunnerRefusesSlugNotInProfileAllowList`, `TestRunnerChecksBeforeTheTokenAndTheLease` (extended; asserts the refusal precedes the call at `internal/runner/runner.go:838`/`:848` and that no credential request and no reserve write happened), `TestRemoteFromTaskAndPolicy` (including a custom clone URL), `TestProjectJobIgnoresFUGARORemote`, `TestProjectJobIgnoresTheProviderEnvVar`. Each refusal test asserts the *absence* of a token request and a reserve write in the fake, which is the mutation that matters. **The tests are written first and fail**: before the change, `jobIdentity` does not exist, `gcp.ProjectJobName` does not exist, and `TestLegacyJobNameTakesThePhaseOnePath` cannot compile — so the red state is real rather than asserted.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/runner/... ./internal/backend/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 3.1, 3.2 (the job names it **computes and matches** — R2-5, never parses), 3.0 step 3. **Commit:** "runner: bind the slug to the job's own repository and admit the profile's repositories".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestJobIdentityMatchesAComputedProjectJobName`, `TestJobIdentityIsNotAParser`, `TestJobIdentityRefusesAnUnknownProjectJob` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 3.4: every job-naming caller

**Files:** `internal/backend/gcp/{run.go,names.go,logs.go}`, `internal/cli/{run.go,runs.go,logs.go,cancel.go}`, `internal/gcpfake/` (+ tests).

**Verified today:** `CheckRunExecution` is `internal/backend/gcp/run.go:92-103` and its **CLI caller is `internal/cli/cloud.go:285`** (finding 20; the earlier draft pointed at `internal/cli/run.go:89-102`, which is `launchHooks` and a different function), `Launch` builds the request body at `:106-115`, the job path `:30-45`, execution parsing `:47-80`, and the execution's own env is read at `:420-430`; the CLI's `max_parallel` counts `fugaro-` jobs at `internal/cli/run.go:1084-1112`; the job names are `internal/backend/gcp/names.go:130` (`JobName`).

1. `Launch` launches the project job for the (profile, provider, account) the workflow selects, and sets `FUGARO_RUN` as it does today (the override, `:110`).
2. **`CheckRunExecution` compares the execution's own `FUGARO_RUN`** (already read from the execution, `:420-430`) with the run it belongs to, and with the repository the caller asked for — "which must compare the execution's own `FUGARO_RUN`, not the job's name" (design §4.1). This is what keeps `logs`, `cancel` and `ls` honest now that several repositories share a job.
3. `fugaro runs ls`/`logs`/`cancel`: selection stays by execution name and by the `FUGARO_RUN` the execution reports, and `max_parallel` (`internal/cli/run.go:1084-1112`) keeps counting `fugaro-` jobs at the same horizon — but its per-job assumption is restated: with shared jobs, one job's executions are several repositories', so the count is by execution, not by job.
4. `internal/gcpfake`: the fake learns project jobs (a name → (profile, provider, account) mapping, and the override merge), because every runner and CLI test in this phase runs against it.
5. `docs/`: the `logs`/`ls` user-facing notes that named a per-repository job.
6. **`logs` and `diagnose` keep refusing a run whose Cloud Run execution no longer exists (owner ruling 2026-10-17).** The refusal is `checkExecution` (`internal/cli/cloud.go`) over `gcp.CheckRunExecution`, and it stays as it is on project jobs: **no bucket-only fallback is added**, and the message is unchanged. `TestLogsAndDiagnoseKeepRefusingAGoneExecution` pins it.

**Tests:** `TestListAndLogsAcrossRepositorySharedJobs`, `TestCancelRefusesAnotherRunsExecution`, `TestMaxParallelCountsExecutionsNotJobs`, and the existing golden-adjacent CLI tests updated to project job names.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... ./internal/backend/... ./internal/gcpfake/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 3.2, 3.3. **Commit:** "cli and backend: every job-naming caller on project jobs".

Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 3.4a: a Claude OAuth launch onto a shared project job is refused up front

**Added 2026-10-17 at the owner's ruling** (item 2 of "Owner rulings of 2026-10-17 on phase 3"). **Why:** until phase 5 gives a shared account its own job per (profile, provider, account) (Task 5.2), a shared project job runs under the profile's own credentials, and a launch that names a Claude OAuth account (`fugaro run --token`, or the checkout's `agent.oauth_token`, resolved at `internal/cli/run.go`) would be started under a variant the job cannot honour. It is refused **before the execution is created**, with a message that names the account, the profile and the fix, instead of failing inside the run.

**Files:** `internal/cli/run.go` (the refusal, in the pre-launch path beside Task 1.5 step 4's admission check), `internal/cli/run_oauth_shared_test.go` (new).

1. In the launch path, when the workflow selects a **project job** (Task 3.4 step 1) and the resolved Claude account is a named OAuth token (the variant `gcp.WorkflowVariant(spec.Workflow, spec.OAuthToken)` is non-default), refuse with: the account name, the profile, and "a Claude OAuth account is not supported on a shared project job until phase 5; use an API-key workflow, or keep this repository on its own job". **No execution is created, no `task.json` is written and no budget token is minted.**
2. **A legacy (per-repository) job is untouched**: the same `--token` on a repository that still has its own job launches exactly as today. The refusal is scoped to project jobs.
3. The message is one function with one text, so Task 5.2 can lift the refusal in one place when accounts get their own job.

**Tests:** `TestOAuthLaunchOntoASharedProjectJobIsRefusedBeforeLaunch` (the refusal, and that the fake backend saw no `Launch`), `TestOAuthLaunchOnALegacyJobIsUnchanged`, `TestOAuthRefusalNamesAccountProfileAndFix`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 3.4. **Commit:** "run: refuse a Claude OAuth launch onto a shared project job before it starts".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL`.** Write the three tests first with the refusal unimplemented: `TestOAuthLaunchOntoASharedProjectJobIsRefusedBeforeLaunch` fails because the fake backend saw a launch. **Not approved by this ruling beyond what the owner already approved**; PR group L.

### Task 3.6: `cloneShipped` flipped, and the docs that gate on it

**Files:** `internal/cli/recipes_skew.go`, `internal/cli/{docs_checkout_test.go,image_refresh_steps_test.go,init_repo_clone_test.go}`, `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/git-providers.md`.

**Verified today:** `cloneSince = "0.7.5"` at `internal/cli/recipes_skew.go:44` — **verified on `main` at `2b6f55b`, not on the drift table's `fd86542` baseline, where the same line still reads `0.7.1`** (the rename is #370, merged on `main` with the 0.7.5 release, R4) — and `cloneShipped = false` at `:70`, with three tests pinning the docs/gate agreement (`internal/cli/docs_checkout_test.go:123-132`, `image_refresh_steps_test.go:662`, `init_repo_clone_test.go:19`).

### Task 3.7: Check 53 — OWNER-RUN

Append `## Check 53: a shared project job, end to end (installation fugaro and the sandbox only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row — **the design's phase 3 live check (§11), as the owner's ruling of 2026-10-15 amends it (Task 3.0 step 2)**: the design says *"Live check on the Bitbucket sandbox, plus a GitHub repository for the GitHub-specific parts (one has to be created; `dimipaun/fugarosandbox` is Bitbucket)"*, and the ruling removes its Bitbucket half — Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path, so there is no shared Bitbucket job to check and **the check runs the GitHub path** (the repository step 3 creates). The design's §11 sentence is corrected by the plan's D5 amendment, applied with the others by Task 0.5. Steps, guarded with **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1):

1. Setup and guard; record the restore (the sandbox's workflow/job names, its service account's bucket condition, its launcher grants, the workflow's `profile`, and whether `cloneShipped` is true in the installed binary).
2. **The Bitbucket half is not run, and the step says why (B6, restated by the ruling).** The design's B6 step asked the owner to confirm, against Bitbucket's own UI, that the project holding `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` holds no production repository — because the *shared* job the design imagined would have been handed a project- or workspace-scoped Bitbucket token (Task 3.0's probe, D5), whose reach is every repository that project holds, `belong`'s `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb` included. **That credential does not exist: the owner ruled on 2026-10-15 that a Bitbucket credential cannot be scoped (Task 3.0 step 2), so Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path and no shared job holds a Bitbucket token.** The question B6 asked therefore has no object, and the step is a **record, not a check**: it states the ruling, that the Bitbucket sandbox keeps its own per-repository job and its own `bitbucket-token`, and that **nothing in this check touches Bitbucket** — so there is no project to confirm and no token whose reach could reach production. **The rule the step existed for is unchanged, and it is why this check still says "never belong" in its own title**: no step here creates, reads or reaches a Bitbucket repository or credential, and no agent touches `belong`. **FACT:** the ruling's date, that Bitbucket is not exercised by this check, and **the GitHub half's own version of the sentence** — step 3's scratch repository must be one the App is installed on and that no production workflow uses — which is the half that still runs.
3. **The check's repository is a GitHub one that does not exist yet** (design §4.4): the owner creates a GitHub sandbox repository under the App, adds it to `allow_repos`, and **everything below runs on it — it is the check's only repository, because step 2's ruling took the Bitbucket half away**. If no GitHub repository is created, **the check does not run**: without it there is neither a shared job to exercise nor a Bitbucket half to fall back on, and the owner is told before phase 3 starts (the GitHub-specific claims — the App token minted per repository, the profile admission, the job's binding — would all stay open, which is the whole check).
4. **The project root applies** (OWNER-RUN Terraform): `fugaro init`'s installation root with the new `modules/project`, on installation `fugaro`. **FACT:** the resource counts (jobs, service accounts, bucket conditions) and that the per-repository root is **not** applied.
5. **A clone workflow runs on the shared job**: `fugaro run` on the sandbox, `checkout: clone`. **FACT:** the job name, the execution's own `FUGARO_RUN`, the clone URL the runner used and where it came from, the profile it ran on, and the run's outcome.
6. **The binding refuses a mismatch**: launch with a `FUGARO_RUN` override naming another slug, and (a second run) with a repository the profile's `allow_repos` does not admit. **FACT:** both refusals verbatim, and that no token was minted and no lease taken (the absence of the credential and reserve lines). **Then the identity the binding rests on:** a project job's run takes its profile, provider and account from `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, which Cloud Run sets — so the check verifies, as the launcher and with the same `run.jobs.runWithOverrides` call `fugaro run` makes, that a container override setting `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` to another job's name (another profile's project job) is either rejected by the API or ignored by the container (the execution's own environment still reports its own job). **FACT:** the API's answer verbatim, or the `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` the execution's container printed; if the override reaches the container, **stop**: the binding's identity is writable by a launcher, and the owner is told before anything of phase 3 ships.
7. **The regression, measured**: from the sandbox job's service account, `gcloud storage cat` of **another** slug's `cache/` object succeeds (the property D4 accepted). **FACT:** the object read, and one sentence confirming SECURITY.md's wording matches it. This step is deliberately written as a test of the documented regression rather than a test that it does not happen.
8. **`ls`, `logs` and `cancel` on a shared job**: **two enrolled GitHub repositories the check names — the scratch repository step 3 creates and a second GitHub scratch repository the owner creates for this step, both non-production, neither in `belong` and none under `edgeappinc`** (R2-6; the second is created the way Task 2.4 step 3's scope repository is, and deleted in the Restore) — run on the same job, then `fugaro runs ls`, `fugaro logs <run>` and a `fugaro cancel` of the wrong execution. **FACT:** each command's selection, and that cancel refuses an execution that is not the run it was asked for.
9. Restore: the second GitHub scratch repository deleted (the first is kept or deleted as the owner chooses — the plan's later checks use it), **the Bitbucket sandbox untouched: it was never on a shared job and its own per-repository root is still applied**, the launcher grants and bucket conditions as recorded, one ordinary sandbox task.

**Paste back:** every FACT line, never a token or key. **Depends on:** 3.0 to 3.6.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck53`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 53` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck53`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

## Phase 4 — enrollment and migration (six tasks; release **0.9.0**, R6)

The design's §4.3, §4.4 and D3. **This phase is where the owner's own installations move**, and where the two things the design says the plan must add on top of #344 land: the enrollment commands and **"retire a repository root"** (design §4.4: config-transitions' engine covers a changed `fugaro.yaml`, not the retirement of a whole repository root with `prevent_destroy` secret containers and a registry — its plan Tasks 1 to 5 do not cover full offboarding; **D8 is superseded (R2) — the full config-transitions plan ships on `main` in 0.7.5, and #365 is its core, so phase 4 builds on the whole engine rather than on a core subset**).

### Task 4.1: `project sync` and `project repos` — enrollment as data

**Files:** `internal/cli/project_enroll.go` (new) + tests, `internal/cli/project.go` (the existing checkout/project helpers it sits beside, `checkoutProject` at `:30`), `internal/infra/discover*.go` (the App's installed repositories), `docs/project-layer.md`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: 1.6's `newProjectCmd()` and the published policy, the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:374`), Firestore's `spendDaily` (the durable sources the design names, §4.3), `internal/gitprov/github`'s App authentication. Produces: `sync`, the fuller `repos ls`, and the `repos add`/`repos rm` pair 1.6 already shipped.

1. `project repos`: lists **enrolled** (the allow-list, from the policy) against **seen** (the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes and Firestore `spendDaily`), each with its canonical name and provider. **No registration record is written** (design §4.3): the set of repositories that have run is derivable, and the RTDB ledger is pruned after about 8 days, so the bucket and Firestore are the sources.
2. `project sync`: lists the GitHub App's installed repositories (the App API, with the project's App credentials) and offers the ones the allow-list does not name, **one confirmed batch**, writing the allow-list through the CAS path 1.6 uses. It never removes anything.
3. `project repos add <provider>/<owner>/<name>` is a one-line data write (shipped in 1.6 for the interim path of 12a item 3) and `project repos rm` its inverse; both print the resulting allow-list.
4. The interim path is closed: after this task a new repository needs **no Terraform** and no `init --repo` for a clone workflow, and `docs/project-layer.md` says the three things that tie a repository to a project (its `project:`, the allow-list, the App's installation) in one paragraph (design §4.3).

**Tests:** `TestProjectReposListsEnrolledAndSeen` (the seen set from a bucket fake and a Firestore fake), `TestProjectSyncAddsOnlyTheMissingOnesAndConfirmsOnce`, `TestProjectRemoveRepoStopsAdmittingTheRepo` (the runner refuses on the next run, via the 1.5/3.3 check).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... ./internal/infra/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 1.6, 3.3. **Commit:** "project: enrollment as data, and the repositories the installation has seen".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestProjectReposListsEnrolledAndSeen`, `TestProjectSyncAddsOnlyTheMissingOnesAndConfirmsOnce`, `TestProjectRemoveRepoStopsAdmittingTheRepo` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 4.2: retire a repository root

**Files:** `internal/cli/init_repo_stage.go`, `internal/cli/init.go` (+ tests), `internal/infra/forget.go`, `internal/infra/{repo.go,workdir.go}`, the config-transitions retirement engine (`internal/infra/retire*.go`, **the whole engine**, not a core subset — D8 is superseded and its plan ships on `main` in 0.7.5, R2), `docs/design/config-transitions.md` (a pointer), `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/design/bucket-iam.md`.

**Verified today:** the repository root's state is `fugaro/repos/<slug>` (`internal/infra/workdir.go:19-23`, `internal/infra/repo.go:38-40`); what it owns is a secret container per logical secret with `prevent_destroy` and two operator grants (`deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/repo/secrets.tf:4-42`), an Artifact Registry repository (`.../repo/registry.tf:10-72`), a build service account (`.../repo/build.tf:5-93`), optionally a check job and scheduler (`.../repo/check.tf:16-142`), and per workflow variant a job service account, the Cloud Run job and its grants (`.../modules/workflow/{sa.tf,job.tf}`).

1. A new command — **`fugaro init --repo --offboard`, never `--retire`** (B4; finding 18 and line 18 of the release table already settled this, and this step had drifted from them): `--retire` is the config-transitions plan's own flag for its own Task 5, and #365 defines it, so reusing the spelling here would put two different meanings on one flag — a repository's root retired versus a repository's per-repository Terraform retired. The action is offboarding, and the flag says so. It **calls** #365's `Retirements`/`RetireSet`/`Orphans` helpers rather than reimplementing them (finding 18). It retires a repository **root**: it computes the retirement set from the state and the labels Fugaro writes (`gcp.LabelManaged`, `gcp.LabelRepo`), removes the module from the root's state, then deletes the labelled jobs, service accounts, IAM bindings and registries **through the API, not through a `destroy`** — because `prevent_destroy` on the secret containers means a destroy cannot run, and the design says plainly that **secret values cannot be carried over**: no person can read them (`.../repo/secrets.tf:31-42`), so a project-scope secret is re-entered (Task 4.5 step 3).
2. **A safety property the plan adds**: the command refuses to retire a repository whose slug another repository's resources still reference (another repository's still-live job, a launcher grant, an operator grant), and it **prints** the allow-list entry the repository currently holds without touching it — **retiring a root never un-enrolls the repository by itself**, because retiring a root and removing a repository from the project are different acts (B4). The step previously said both things at once: that the command never un-enrolls, and that "the ordering it enforces" is *un-enroll → retire the root*. The command enforces no such ordering; it performs the retirement and nothing else. The two orderings it is used in, both the **operator's** and both spelled out in the output the command prints:
   - **Migrating a repository onto a project job** (design §4.4's "re-run init under the new model"): deploy the project jobs → **enroll** the repository (`project repos add`) and switch its launches → verify a run on the new job → **then** retire its root. Enrollment comes **before** the retirement here, and it must: the project job admits only repositories on the allow-list (Task 3.3 step 4), so retiring the root of a repository that is not yet enrolled would leave it unable to run at all.
   - **Removing a repository from the project**: **un-enroll** it (`project repos rm`, a separate and explicit act, Task 1.6) → retire its root → its runs stop. This is the only order in which un-enrollment precedes retirement, and the retirement command does not perform it.
   Both are printed by the command as the two lines of the sequence it is part of, with the row that applies to this invocation marked, so an operator who runs it mid-migration sees that the enrollment already happened (or is still missing) rather than inferring it.
3. `internal/infra/forget.go`'s existing forget path is reused for the state half rather than duplicated; the labels half is the new code.
4. `docs/checkout.md:81`'s inert per-repository registry paragraph is replaced by "a clone repository has no root at all" once 4.3 lands.
5. **A test that no retirement deletes a resource another repository needs**: a golden-plan test over the retirement set, plus `TestOffboardRefusesAStillLiveRepositoryJob` and `TestForgetKeepsTheStateOfUnrelatedRepos`, and `TestOffboardNeverTouchesTheAllowList` (B4: `--offboard` on a repository that is enrolled leaves `config/policy/shared`'s `allow_repos` byte-identical, and its output names the entry as printed-not-removed).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... ./internal/infra/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** **the full config-transitions plan, which ships on `main` in 0.7.5 (#365 is its core, already in flight — D8 is superseded, R2)**, and 3.2. **Commit:** "init --repo --offboard: retire a repository's whole root, state removal then labelled deletes".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestOffboardRefusesAStillLiveRepositoryJob`, `TestForgetKeepsTheStateOfUnrelatedRepos` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 4.3: `init --repo` retired for clone repositories

**Files:** `internal/cli/{init_repo_target.go,init.go,init_repo_stage.go}` (+ tests), `internal/infra/spec.go`, `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/project-layer.md`.

1. For a `checkout: clone` repository, `init --repo` becomes: the local `repos:` entry (unchanged) **plus** an allow-list line — it applies **no Terraform** and asks **no confirmation** beyond the one write, which is the spec's "adding a repo must be lightweight: no Terraform, no confirmations" (spec §4.2). Where it used to create a registry, a build service account, secret containers and a job, it now does nothing of the kind.
2. **This is where the design supersedes #343's repository-root grant wiring and the `ExtraSecrets` union** (design §10): with no repository root for a clone repository, a project secret's grant is an installation-root grant, and the union the repository root computed is no longer needed on this path. #343's machinery stays for the per-repository path (baked workflows, until 4.4) — the code is not deleted, the path is.
3. `fugaro init --repo` on a **baked** workflow keeps today's behaviour until 4.4 (Task 4.4's ordering), and prints one line saying so.
4. `docs/checkout.md`: a clone repository's onboarding is now "commit `project:` and `checkout: clone`, run `fugaro init --repo` once, run `fugaro project repos add`" — with the note that only the App's installation remains as a provider-side step.
