### Task 4.4: baked workflows, dropped after phase 4 (D3)

**Files:** `internal/cli/{init_repo_stage.go,init.go}` (+ tests), `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/base-image.md`, `SECURITY.md` (a pointer), `plugin/skills/setup/**`.

**The design's ruling:** "phases 3 and 4 support clone workflows only; baked workflows stay on the existing per-repository path, unchanged, until phase 4 ends, and are then **dropped** … or **re-implemented as per-repository jobs created through the Cloud Run API**, adopted by labels, with no Terraform (the `image refresh` precedent)" — owner decision **D3: dropped after phase 4**.

1. `fugaro init --repo` refuses a baked workflow **for new onboarding only, with the way out in the message** (finding 17): `checkout: clone` (measured at a 10.5 s median cold start, Check 41, design §4.4) or a recipe, since "the base image carries every runtime". **The refusal is gated on new onboarding, and the gate is not cosmetic**: `belong` — the owner's production installation, which holds `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb` — runs **baked** workflows (design §4.4), and an unconditional refusal would break the owner's production `init --repo` the moment they upgrade, on the one installation no agent may touch and whose migration is the owner's own work after the model is proven on `fugaro` (D2, D3). So: a repository being onboarded **for the first time** is refused and told to move to clone; a repository that already has jobs and is being re-initialised (the upgrade path, which is exactly `belong`'s and every other existing repository's) gets a **warning** naming the migration — "this workflow is baked; baked workflows are dropped after phase 4" — and proceeds. The gate is the same fact `init --repo` already computes when it decides whether it is creating or updating a repository, and the task's test asserts both sides. The owner migrates `belong` **after** `fugaro` is proven, by their own hand (Task 4.5's rule, extended): nothing in this plan performs that migration, and the warning is what tells them it is due.
2. `fugaro doctor`: a `baked-workflows` check that lists any workflow still baked in a repository the installation knows, as a `warning` naming the migration, so an owner finds them before a run fails.
3. The per-repository code path stays in the tree but is unreachable for new deployments; **the API-created-job alternative is explicitly deferred, not built** — if an owner needs a baked workflow after this phase, that is a new design (§4.4's second option), and the plan says so rather than leaving it implied.
4. `docs/base-image.md` and `docs/checkout.md`: what "baked" was, why it is gone, and what replaces it.

**Tests:** `TestInitRepoRefusesABakedWorkflow` (message names `checkout: clone` and the base image), `TestDoctorListsBakedWorkflows`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 4.2, 4.3. **Commit:** "baked workflows are dropped; clone and recipes are the way".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestInitRepoRefusesABakedWorkflow`, `TestDoctorListsBakedWorkflows` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 4.5: the owner's migration of installation `fugaro` — OWNER-RUN, Check 54

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` (and, if the migration exposes a gap, a follow-up task in the same PR — never a silently-skipped step).

Installation `fugaro` holds `dimipaun/fugaro` (GitHub, baked, with a check job), `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` (Bitbucket, baked, no check job) and `dimipaun/fugarosandbox` (Bitbucket, listed in the local config with no workdir on this machine: stale or onboarded elsewhere, UNVERIFIED — design §4.4). **No agent touches `belong`** (which holds `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb`): moving it is the owner's own work after the model is proven on `fugaro`.

Append `## Check 54: migrate installation fugaro to project jobs (installation fugaro, sandbox first, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row:

1. Setup and guard — **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1), and this check's own name list is a `never belong` list: every repository it names is the sandbox or a scratch repository the owner creates for it. Record **everything** to restore: the local config file, each repository's `fugaro.yaml`, the caps, the policy, the secrets' names and scopes (**never their values**), each job's env, and the project jobs' names.
2. **Sandbox first**: `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` converted to `checkout: clone` on a branch, `fugaro init --repo` run once, added to the allow-list, run once on a project job. **FACT:** the job it ran on, its profile, and the outcome.
3. **Secrets are re-entered, not copied** (design §4.4): the owner types the project-scope values (nothing can read the old ones). **FACT:** the names and scopes only, that `fugaro secrets ls` shows a version, and that no value appears in any log, plan or state print — never a value.
4. `dimipaun/fugarosandbox` (`UNVERIFIED`: stale or onboarded elsewhere): the owner decides from `fugaro project repos` — if it never ran, `project repos rm` and retire its root if one exists; if it is live elsewhere, it stays untouched and the FACT records the decision.
5. `dimipaun/fugarosandbox`'s sibling, `dimipaun/fugaro`: `dimipaun/fugaro` is **baked**, so it is the phase's proof that D3's refusal is survivable: convert its workflow to `checkout: clone` (or a recipe), run it once on a project job. **FACT:** the workflow's before/after `checkout`, the run's outcome, and the cold-start time against Check 41's 10.5 s median. If a workflow genuinely cannot be converted, **stop and report** — that is the signal for §4.4's deferred API path, and it is the owner's decision, not the agent's.
6. **Retire the roots** (OWNER-RUN Terraform, one at a time, sandbox first): `fugaro init --repo --offboard`, then confirm no labelled job, service account, registry or secret container remains and that `gcp`'s service-account count dropped. **FACT:** the resource counts before and after, and that the runs bucket's objects were left alone.
7. Restore: put back whatever was changed, run one ordinary task on the sandbox, and confirm `fugaro project repos`, `fugaro doctor` and `fugaro runs ls` agree with what is deployed. **`belong` is not touched at any step** — the check's last FACT line says so explicitly.

**Paste back:** every FACT line, never a secret value. **Depends on:** 4.1 to 4.4, and 3.7 (the shared job proven first).

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck54`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 54` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck54`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

Update the roster, re-run: `Expected: PASS`. A sweep that produces no failing test either changed no text or changed text no test reads; both are worth knowing before the commit, not after.

## Phase 5 — the OAuth pool (five tasks; release **0.9.0**, R6)

The design's §7. **What changes**: a Claude OAuth token stops being a repository's secret and becomes **a person's**, referenced by the project. Residual risks the design states and this phase's docs repeat: a project job that mounts several people's tokens widens the `/proc` and metadata-server exposure of `mixed-credentials.md` §6 to every repository sharing that job, and **OAuth spend is notional and no per-person budget exists** (`internal/runstore/cost.go:8`, `:48-50` and `:62-65` — the `BasisSubscription` constant's own "oauth: notional" comment, `NewCost`'s doc saying a subscription's model figure is notional and left out of the total, and `newCost` excluding it; **round-6 correction**: the earlier draft cited `:62-71`, which is `newCost`'s whole body and not where the notional rule is stated). **#363 is held until this phase** (D9) and is rebased in Task 5.4.

### Task 5.1: person-owned secrets and their references — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/projectpolicy/policy.go` (+ tests, the `accounts` records), `internal/cli/accounts.go` (+ `accounts_test.go`), `internal/infra/{secretmounts_test.go,readiness.go}`, `internal/backend/gcp/names.go` (the per-person secret ID), `SECURITY.md`.

**Verified today:** tokens are per (repository, account) — `internal/cli/accounts.go:60-94` lists them, `config.ClaudeAccountSecret` derives the ID (`internal/config/claude_accounts.go:80`), and `internal/infra/spec.go:589` mounts the (workflow, account) variant's secret; `claude-oauth-token-*` is refused project scope by `claudeOAuthAccountAllowed`/`claudeOAuthAccountRefusal` (`internal/cli/secrets.go:174-204`) and is **not** swept in by D2. A secret's ID is a flat, project-wide namespace and a `secret_id` IAM grant works identically whichever root created it (`docs/design/project-secrets.md:18`), which is what makes this task Go and IAM rather than a new resource type.

1. **Storage**: one Secret Manager secret per **(person, account)** in the project's GCP project, with a new ID helper beside `gcp.SecretID`/`ProjectSecretID` (`internal/backend/gcp/names.go:143-158`) — flat IDs and per-secret grants work regardless of which root owns them (`docs/design/project-secrets.md:18`). **The ID is opaque (finding 26), and this is a security requirement, not a style choice**: the ID **is** published — it travels in the run-readable reference record in step 4 — so an ID derived from the person's address (even sanitized, as `dimi-billmybank-com` or `dimi-at-example-com`) publishes the address to every run token on the installation. The helper is therefore something like `gcp.PersonSecretID(fugaroProject, account, ref)` where `ref` is a random or hashed opaque reference minted when the secret is created (`derive` truncation applies, `internal/backend/gcp/names.go:101-112`), and the person's identity never enters the ID. `account` stays in the ID — it is the job's own field, not a person's — so an operator can still see which account a secret belongs to.
2. **Who creates it**: **the person** (12a item 11), with `fugaro accounts add <account>` — **a new subcommand this task adds** (`internal/cli/accounts.go` has `share` and the listing today, not `add`; finding 21) — storing their own token at person scope; the operator accepts the reference. The owner of the secret is granted `secretVersionAdder` and `viewer` on that secret only, **never `setIamPolicy`** (design §7).
3. **The grant is an operator step, and the plan says why**: granting the project's job service account `secretAccessor` on a person's secret needs `setIamPolicy`, so it happens when the reference is accepted — through the installation root (Task 5.2), not by the person.
4. **Reference record** in `config/policy/shared`: `{account, secret ID, allowed profiles}` — **no value and no identity**; the owner's identity lives in the admin-only `config/admin` node and in the export (design §5(2), §7). `fugaro accounts share <name>` proposes it after the owner confirms; an operator accepts; the owner can retire it.
5. `fugaro accounts ls` (and `--json`) shows, per account: the person, the reference's state, which profiles mount it, and — for the owner — whether their token is used by anyone else. **The run-readable node never carries an email**, and the test is stronger than an `@` scan (finding 26): `TestShareRecordCarriesNoIdentity` scans the published node for `@` **and** for every known address's sanitized forms — the local part with non-alphanumerics collapsed to `-`, lowercased, and the same with the domain appended — so `dimi-billmybank-com`, `dimibillmybankcom` and the dotted variants fail the test rather than passing an `@` scan. The reference record's only person-adjacent field is the **opaque ID** of step 1, which this same test asserts is not derived from the address (it checks that the published ID is not one of the sanitized forms, and that it does not contain the local part as a substring).
6. SECURITY.md: the per-person model, the operator's grant step, the widening risk in the design's own words, and the notional-spend caveat.

**Tests:** `TestPersonSecretIDIsFlatAndPerAccount`; `TestShareRecordCarriesNoIdentity` (`@` scan of the published node); `TestOwnerCanRetireAReference`; `TestPersonCannotSetIamPolicy` (the CLI never calls it from a person's flow); `TestAccountsShareProposesBeforeWriting`.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... ./internal/projectpolicy/... ./internal/backend/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 3.2 (a job per (profile, provider, account)), 1.6. **Commit:** "accounts: a person's OAuth token, referenced by the project".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestPersonSecretIDIsFlatAndPerAccount`, `TestShareRecordCarriesNoIdentity`, `TestOwnerCanRetireAReference` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

1. `fugaro run` retries, on a usage limit, with the **next account**: the launcher's own accounts first, then the shared ones, with the tried-list the existing design names, and it records the switch in the run's record so `fugaro runs ls`/`report` show it.

4. `agent.oauth_token` stays as a preference (design §7, "No repository level"): it can name an account, and the per-repository copies plus `secrets set claude-oauth-token-* --repo` go away with the per-repository path.

## Phase 6 — the backing Git repository (five tasks; release **0.9.0**, R6)

The design's §8. **`fugaro project export` writes a record, not a unit of execution**: the backing repository is never enrolled and never runs. What it lets one reconstruct is the project's configuration and policy and an account of spend over time — **not** the project end to end: secret values must be re-entered, and Firestore history, run records, the GitHub App and the Firebase project itself are not in it (design §8, stated in the doc and in `doctor`).

**Revision 2026-10-15 — the location is a folder, not necessarily a repository.** The earlier revision of this phase had the project name a **backing repository** and nothing else; the owner's requirement is that a project may keep its record **in a folder of a repository it already has**: *"we may not necessarily want a repository dedicated to that information. Maybe somebody has a mono repo and would like to save that information under a folder in that repository, called maybe fugaro-project."* (design §8, D14). So the location is **three settings, not one** — `repo`, `path` (default `fugaro-project/`; the **repository root** is the dedicated-repository case) and `branch` (default `fugaro-project`) — carried as one **`backing` object** in the policy, and **D14 — the owner's own decision about the branch and about a PR mode — was answered on 2026-10-15**: a **dedicated branch by default** (`fugaro-project`, writing only under `fugaro-project/`), **never the monorepo's `main` by default**, and the PR mode stays a documented opt-in **not built in the first slice**. Four things this revision changes in the tasks below, each one written where it is implemented rather than left as a paragraph here:

1. **Only `path` is written and only `path` is staged** (Task 6.2 step 3), with the test the owner asked for: an export that changes, stages or reformats a file **outside** the folder fails it (`TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath`) — and the tree beside the folder is, in the monorepo case, another team's product.
2. **Concurrency is fetch → commit on the fetched tip → plain push, and never a force** (Task 6.2 step 2): a branch that moved is rebased onto when it can be, and **fails loudly** naming the paths when it cannot; `gitops.Repo.Push` is deliberately **not** reused (it is forced under a lease, and it refuses branches outside `fugaro/`).
3. **The commit is quiet** (Task 6.2 step 4): a `[skip ci]` marker, the fixed `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>` identity (`internal/gitops/gitops.go:62-78`) so the operator's own name and address are not committed, and a sorted, stable tree so the folder's diffs stay the size of the policy change.
4. **Access is stated, not implied** (Task 6.3 step 1): in a monorepo everyone who can read the repository can read the policy, the caps and the spend history — which is why **people are omitted by default** (P10) and secrets are excluded; `--with-people` is the operator's explicit choice.
5. **The fix round adds the three things the four above assumed** (2026-10-15, P11's second half): the **detector** that makes "somebody changed a file under `path`" observable (§8's marker, with `--adopt` and `--force-regenerate` as the only overrides), the **grammar** of `path` in one place with a test per case, and the **credential** — the operator's own local git credentials, never the runner's git secret.

Its own check is **Check 61** (Task 6.5), separate from Check 56: "the folder is fenced off from the rest of the tree, a moved branch is followed without a force, and an edit inside the folder is refused by name" is a different assertion from "the export holds no secret value", and it is the folder case's own. The check runs against a scratch repository **the owner owns** — a local `file://` bare repository by default — and never one in the `edgeappinc` workspace the sandbox lives in.

### Task 6.1: `fugaro project export` — SECURITY-SENSITIVE, independent opus review

**Files:** `internal/cli/project_export.go` (new) + tests, `internal/cli/project.go`, `internal/report/report.go` (read-only reuse), `internal/watch/json.go` (a reader), `internal/budget/model.go` (the paths it reads), `docs/project-layer.md`.

**Verified today:** `report.Build` is `internal/report/report.go:159` and is called from `internal/cli/report.go:170,393`; the `watch` JSON document is `internal/watch/json.go:148` (`BuildJSON`); the RTDB cap and spend paths are `internal/budget/model.go:51-80` (`PathCapsRepo`, `PathSpendGlobal`, `PathSpendRepo`, `PathSpendRun`); the shared bucket config is read through `internal/blobx`/`internal/cli/sharedcfg.go`.

**Interfaces.** Consumes: the policy (1.1), the caps' paths, the bucket's `fugaro/` objects, Secret Manager's metadata list, `report.Build`, the local operator config. Produces: a directory tree and a `--push`-able commit.

```go
// Layout (design §8). Every path below is relative to the backing location's
// path: in a monorepo this block IS the fugaro-project/ folder, and in a
// dedicated repository it is the repository root (the same tree, one setting
// apart). Nothing here holds a secret value (P10).
README.md        what this folder is, who writes it, what it deliberately does not hold (design §8)
policy/          config/* as JSON: caps (global, defaults, per-repo), mode, limits, kill switches with when and why
policy/shared.json  the policy object, admin nodes included only under --with-admin
config/          the bucket's shared config, project layer and recipes, verbatim bytes
config/operator.yaml  the operator's local config without identity and endpoints; secrets by NAME only
inventory/       secrets metadata (names, scopes, versions' create times), enrolled repositories, project jobs
spend/           Firestore spendDaily (final days only, so history is never rewritten) and per-run CSVs from result.json
```

1. **Final days only** for `spendDaily` (design §8): the current day is still being written, so an export never rewrites a number that later changes.
2. **People are omitted by default** (P10): no `byPerson` spend, no kill-switch author. `--with-people` includes them and its help text says they are committed to a Git repository.
3. **No value, ever**: the secret inventory reads metadata only (`secretmanager.secrets.list`/`versions.list` — the launchers already hold it, `deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/installation/iam.tf:15-19`), and `TestExportContainsNoSecretValue` scans every byte of the output for every value the fake Secret Manager holds plus the run's own token.
4. `--json` prints the tree's manifest, so a caller can diff two exports without reading files; the human output prints per-file counts and the export's own `checked_at`.
5. Idempotent and deterministic: same input → byte-identical output, including ordering (sorted keys and **a sorted, stable file list** — same names, same order, no timestamped filenames), so a diff shows a policy change and not a map iteration or a renamed CSV. This is what keeps a **monorepo's** diffs small: the folder is reviewed by people who do not work on Fugaro, and a reordering of the same content is noise they have to read (design §8).
6. **The tree is a folder's content** (design §8, D14): every path above is relative to the backing location's `path`, and the export never writes outside it — not in the local `--out` directory tree, and not in the repository when Task 6.2 pushes it. In a dedicated repository `path` is the root and the two are the same statement.

**Tests:** `TestExportContainsNoSecretValue`; `TestExportOmitsPeopleByDefault` and `TestExportWithPeopleIncludesThem`; `TestExportDeterministic` (two runs, byte compare, **including the file list and its order**); `TestExportSpendSkipsTheCurrentDay`; `TestExportOperatorYamlHasNoIdentityOrEndpoints`; `TestExportWritesOnlyUnderItsOutputDirectory` (every file the run creates is under `--out`, which is the local half of Task 6.2's `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath`).

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... -count=1`. **Depends on:** 1.6, 3.5 (the inventory's job list). **Commit:** "project export: the project's record, secrets excluded".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestExportContainsNoSecretValue`, `TestExportOmitsPeopleByDefault`, `TestExportWithPeopleIncludesThem` first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 6.2: `--push`, the location's three settings, and the cadence the design defers

**Files:** `internal/cli/project_export.go` (+ tests), `internal/projectpolicy/policy.go` (+ `policy_test.go`, `read_test.go`), `docs/project-layer.md`.

1. **The location is one `backing` object in the policy, written by the operator — and it replaces the single `backing_repo` field the earlier revision of this task named**, which cannot express a folder (the owner's requirement of 2026-10-15, design §8/D14):

```json
"backing": {"repo": "github/acme/platform", "path": "fugaro-project/", "branch": "fugaro-project"}
```

   `repo` is canonical `provider/owner/name` — the same spelling `allow_repos` and `task.Slug` use, and the same value the runner's slug binding compares — **or an absolute `file://` URL** (design §8's table), the one non-provider form, which exists for a rehearsal or a live check with no host, no account and no workspace to write in (Check 61 uses it) and which needs no credential and is not shareable, as the export's own output says. `path` defaults to **`fugaro-project/`** (the owner's own name for the folder) and the **repository root** — the dedicated-repository case — is `""` or `"."`; `branch` defaults to **`fugaro-project`**, never the monorepo's `main` unless the owner writes it. Validation lives beside `validRouting` (`internal/projectpolicy/policy.go:445`) and refuses a `repo` that is not canonical, a `path` that is not one of design §8's **two accepted shapes**, and a `branch` that is not a valid ref name, is `HEAD`, or is **empty-set-to-the-repository's-default-branch** (the default is written out, never inherited from the checkout's own `HEAD`).

   **`path`'s grammar is design §8's and this task does not restate it — it implements it, in one function, and tests every line of its table.** The earlier revision of this task said "refuses a `path` that is absolute, that escapes the root (`..`), or that does not end in `/`" **in the same paragraph that allowed `""` and `"."` as the repository root**, which is a contradiction: a bare `""` does not end in `/`, and a rule a reader has to reconcile with its own exception is a rule that will be implemented two ways. §8 now states the grammar once (root as `""`/`"."`, or relative + `/`-separated + normalised + trailing slash + no `.git` segment in any case), and this task's `TestBackingPathGrammar` carries **one row per accepted and refused spelling in §8's table** — including the ones the old sentence got wrong (`""` and `"."` accepted; `fugaro-project` without the slash, `./fugaro-project/`, `a/../fugaro-project/`, `fugaro-project//` and `fugaro-project/.GIT/` refused) — so the grammar has one implementation and its refusals have one test. **The filesystem half is three more tests, because the string half cannot see the tree** (design §8): `TestExportPushRefusesASymlinkEscape` (the fetched folder carries a symlink where the export expects a directory, or one pointing out of the working tree: the export refuses rather than writing through it), `TestExportPushRefusesAGitDirectoryUnderPath` (the fetched `path` resolves to a directory named `.git` in any case) and `TestExportPushRefusesACaseVariantCollision` (two entries under `path` that differ only in case, which cannot both be checked out on macOS). **The version-skew direction is stated where it lands:** `backing` is a new **top-level** key, so it joins `knownTopLevelKeys` (`internal/projectpolicy/policy.go:118`) in the same commit; the runner **tolerates and warns** on a key it does not know (`internal/runner/projectpolicy.go:111-116`) while the **CLI refuses** it (`internal/cli/launch_budget.go:116-121`, `internal/cli/project_cmd.go:282-286`), and the policy's `version` does **not** move — nothing here changes what a run is held to, and bumping `Version` would halt every runner image older than this release over a setting only the operator's CLI reads. The consequence for the docs: publishing a `backing` object needs a CLI at least as new as the release that ships it, and the CLI's own refusal already says so.
2. **The push fetches, commits on the fetched tip, and pushes plainly — never a force** (design §8, the owner's concurrency requirement). The sequence is: clone the repository into a scratch dir (`noHooks`, `internal/gitops/gitops.go:179`, so the cloned repository's own hooks never run) → fetch `branch` → **create the export's single commit on that branch's current tip** (the export keeps no history of its own, so "rebase onto the branch" and "create the commit" are one operation) → `git push` with **no `--force`, no `--force-with-lease` and no `+` refspec**. A branch that moved between the fetch and the push makes the push be *rejected*, and the export reports the divergence and stops; a change **inside `path`** by someone else makes the commit impossible, and the export **fails loudly naming the paths** rather than overwriting them. **`gitops.Repo.Push` is deliberately not reused**: it is forced **under a lease** because a run's own branch may be amended and rebased (`internal/gitops/gitops.go:594-598`), and it refuses every branch outside `fugaro/` (`checkRunBranch`, `:664-672`) — the second is also why the default branch is not inside the runner's own prefix, and reusing `PushExisting` would reintroduce exactly the force the owner ruled out. **The credential is the operator's own local git credentials, never the runner's git secret.** An earlier revision of this step said "the same path the runner's git secret uses", which the design's §8 contradicted in the same document (its cadence paragraph already had the manual export needing "the operator's local file and git credentials") — and only one of the two can be true, because the runner's git secret is minted for a run and **no person can read it**: a manual export could not use it even if the design wanted it to. So the export authenticates the way a `git push` typed in the operator's own shell does — the SSH agent, the credential helper, or `gh auth` for the host the `repo` setting names — and it reads no policy, secret or environment variable that holds the runner's credential. The scheduled half (step 8) is the one that will need a machine credential, and the plan keeps that distinction where the two meet. **The push target's provider is the one `repo` names** (`provider/owner/name`; `docs/git-providers.md` lists them), and nothing infers it from a URL.
   **The branch is bootstrapped from the default branch's tip, never orphan** (design §8): when `branch` does not exist on the remote, the export creates it at the **remote's** default branch tip — read with `git ls-remote --symref <url> HEAD`, never from the operator's checkout, whose `HEAD` is a different repository's — and puts the export's single commit on top. **And the clone is shallow, blobless and sparse**: `--depth 1`, `--filter=blob:none` (the mechanism the runner already uses, `gitops.CloneBlobless`) and `git sparse-checkout set <path>`, so a monorepo costs one folder and not its history; the tree hashes the detector compares are read from the fetched commit (`git ls-tree <tip>:<path> | sed -e '/[[:space:]]\.fugaro-export\.json$/d' | git mktree` — design §8's recipe, and **not** `git rev-parse <tip>:<path>`, which names the committed folder *with* the marker in it and so could never equal a marker value that excludes it), so the comparison needs no content and the push carries only the folder's own objects.
3. **Only `path` is written and only `path` is staged — the task test the owner asked for.** The commit stages `path` and nothing else: never `git add -A`, never `git add .`, never a `git clean`, never a reformat or a re-sort of a file outside the folder. `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath` is the test that fails if an export changes any file outside `path`: it exports into a scratch repository (`internal/testutil.NewRemote`, `internal/testutil/git.go:98`) whose tree **already carries unrelated files** — a root `README.md`, `src/app.go`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — and asserts (a) every one of them is byte-identical after the run, (b) `git status --porcelain` outside `path` is empty (nothing stages what it must not), and (c) the commit's own diff (`git show --name-only`) names only paths under `path/`. In the dedicated-repository case, where `path` is the root, the same test passes vacuously and says so rather than pretending to cover it.
4. **The conflict rule gets a detector, because without one the rule cannot be tested — and this is the fix round's most important change** (design §8). The earlier revision asserted that "a change **inside `path`** by someone else makes the commit impossible, and the export fails loudly naming the paths", with **no mechanism that could know**: the export regenerates the whole folder and keeps no history of its own, so a folder the last export wrote and a folder a person edited are the same thing to a fetch, and the test listed for it (`TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath`) had nothing to observe. The detector is the export's own record, written **inside the folder it owns**: a **marker file** `<path>.fugaro-export.json` (`.fugaro-export.json` in the repository-root case) holding the export's `checked_at` and the **git tree hash of everything the export wrote under `path`, the marker itself excluded** (a file cannot hash itself, and the marker is the one path under `path` the export rewrites unconditionally). The sequence is then: fetch `branch` → read `path` from the fetched tip → compute the same tree hash from the fetched commit → compare with the marker's. **The computation is design §8's one recipe, written there once because the obvious one is wrong:** `git rev-parse <tip>:<path>` names the folder's tree *as committed*, the marker included, so it could never equal a value that excludes the marker — every export would refuse forever, and the refusal would read as somebody else's edit. It is `git ls-tree <tip>:<path> | sed -e '/[[:space:]]\.fugaro-export\.json$/d' | git mktree`: the folder's entries with the marker's line dropped, built into a tree object by `git mktree` (which normalises entry order, so the hash is the entries'). **The export's own side is the same object built the other way** — it writes the folder, stages it, and takes `git rev-parse "$(git write-tree):<path>"` **before the marker is in the index**, writes the marker with that value, and stages it — so the two hashes are comparable by construction. **`TestExportAcceptsAFolderItWroteItself` pins that the two sides agree**: a second export into an unchanged folder is accepted rather than refused, which is the property the wrong recipe breaks (with `git rev-parse <tip>:<path>` every run refuses, and a test that only compared a marker with itself would still pass). **Equal** means nobody has written under `path` since the last export: replace the folder, rewrite the marker, commit. **Different, or a non-empty `path` with no marker at all**, means somebody else's content is there: **refuse, name the paths that would be overwritten, print both hashes, and stop without committing and without pushing.** `TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath` is therefore a real test of this step (the second writer's edit is made **inside** `path`, the export refuses, the branch tip is unchanged and the edit is still there), joined by `TestExportAcceptsAFolderItWroteItself` (nothing changed: the marker's value and the fetched folder's agree, so the export proceeds — the test that fails if the marker-excluded recipe is replaced by one that includes the marker, which refuses every time) and `TestExportRefusesWhenTheMarkerIsMissingOnANonEmptyFolder` (a folder the export did not write, and no marker) and `TestExportDetectsACommittedEditUnderPath` (the out-of-band edit is **committed** to `branch`, so "diff against the parent commit" would have called it clean while the marker does not — the case that shows the detector is about the folder's content and not about who committed last).
5. **The two overrides are `--adopt` and `--force-regenerate`, and they answer two different questions** (design §8). `--adopt` — *"whose folder is this?"* — replaces a folder the export did not write (a hand-made one, or the first export into a repository that already has a `path`) and records a fresh marker; `--force-regenerate` — *"may I discard this edit?"* — replaces a folder whose marker exists and whose tree has changed since, and names in its own output the paths it replaced. Neither is a synonym for the other, **neither is available to a scheduled or agent-run export**, and there is no third way past the refusal (no `--yes`, no prompt, no quiet adoption of a marker-less folder): `TestExportAdoptsAFolderItDidNotWrite` and `TestExportForceRegenerateOverwritesAnEditedFolder` pin the two, and `TestExportWithoutBackingRefusesPush`'s sibling for the scheduled path asserts the flags are refused there. *Veto alternative:* one `--force` for both (it makes "take over this folder once" and "discard my colleague's edit every cadence" the same keystroke, and the second is the one a scheduled export must never hold).
6. **The commit is quiet** (design §8): the subject names the export's `checked_at` and carries the **skip-CI marker** `[skip ci]` (the marker GitHub Actions, GitLab and Bitbucket Pipelines all honour) — a monorepo builds on every push, and an export must not start one; the author and committer are the **fixed identity** `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>` through `gitops.IdentityEnv()` (`internal/gitops/gitops.go:62-78`), so the operator's own name and address are never written into a repository other people read — the same principle as P10's omission of `byPerson` spend; and the tree is the sorted, stable one Task 6.1 step 5 builds, so the folder's diff is the policy change.
7. **The PR mode is answered — D14, ruled 2026-10-15 — and it is not this task's default** (design §8/D14): if the owner later wants it, the export would push the export's own branch and open a pull request on the repository's host instead of committing to the branch the policy names. **The ruling, in the design's §8 wording, used here, in D14 and in P11 without variation: *a dedicated branch by default — the branch `fugaro-project`, writing only under the `fugaro-project/` folder — never the monorepo's `main` by default; and the PR mode is a documented opt-in, not built in the first slice.*** The earlier revision of this step called the PR mode "an explicit non-feature" while §8 called it "the documented opt-in" and D14 called it "the documented opt-in" too — three descriptions of one thing, which is how a documented opt-in ends up read as a rejected feature (or the reverse) by whichever reviewer reads one of the three. So: this task ships the recommended default — a dedicated branch, pushed directly — and `docs/project-layer.md` documents the PR mode as an **opt-in the CLI does not have yet**, with its reason (it needs a credential that can open a pull request on the host, and an export that waits on a person every cadence stops being an export, D7's deferred half).
8. **The scheduled export and the `init`/`budget set` hooks are deferred** (D7: "manual first, scheduled later"). The design's later shape is recorded where the code will be: a history-job entry (`deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/installation/history.tf:174-253`, whose service account already has RTDB admin) needs a deploy key or App token as a project secret, and whether the existing App can push to the backing repository is **UNVERIFIED** (design §8) — a probe for that phase's own check, not this one.
9. `docs/project-layer.md`: the manual cadence; **what a folder in a monorepo is** and what to put in it (this task's README.md, and that everything beside it is untouched); **who can read it** — everyone who can read the repository can read the policy, the caps and the spend history, which is why people are omitted by default and secrets never written (P10, design §8); and the sentence that the export does not make the project reconstructable end to end.

**Tests:** `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath` (**the one the 2026-10-15 revision adds**, step 3); `TestExportPushRebasesOntoAMovedBranch` (a second writer commits to `branch` under the export's feet, **outside** the folder: the export fetches, commits on the new tip, pushes, and the out-of-band commit is still an ancestor); `TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath` (an out-of-band change **inside** `path`, detected by the marker: the export refuses, names the paths, exits non-zero and leaves the branch exactly as it found it); `TestExportDetectsACommittedEditUnderPath` (the same edit **committed** to `branch`, which a parent-commit diff would call clean); `TestExportRefusesWhenTheMarkerIsMissingOnANonEmptyFolder`; `TestExportAcceptsAFolderItWroteItself` (the marker-excluded tree recipe agrees on both sides, step 4); `TestExportAdoptsAFolderItDidNotWrite` (step 5's `--adopt`); `TestExportForceRegenerateOverwritesAnEditedFolder` (step 5's other override, and that the scheduled path refuses both flags); `TestExportBootstrapsTheBranchFromTheDefaultBranchsTip` (the branch does not exist: it is created at the remote's default tip, `git merge-base --is-ancestor` holds, and no orphan branch is made); `TestExportPushNeverForces` (**the named seam: `recordGit` in `internal/cli/project_export_test.go`, a test helper that puts a recording `git` shim first on `PATH` for the run — the shim appends its own `argv`, one invocation per line, to `$FUGARO_TEST_GIT_ARGV` and then execs the real `git` — and the test asserts on the recorded lines** that no invocation carries `--force`, `--force-with-lease` or a `+` refspec, and that the last one is a plain `push` of `branch`; `TestExportPushClonesShallowBloblessAndSparse` and `TestExportPushUsesTheOperatorsLocalCredentials` read the same recording rather than a second fake, so "the recorded argv" means one thing in this task's tests and each of the three asserts a different property of it); `TestExportPushUsesTheConfiguredLocation` (`repo`, `path` and `branch` all read from the policy; the default branch is `fugaro-project` and never the checkout's `main`); `TestExportPushClonesShallowBloblessAndSparse` (`recordGit`'s recording carries `--depth 1`, `--filter=blob:none` and a sparse checkout limited to `path`, so a monorepo's history is not fetched); `TestExportPushUsesTheOperatorsLocalCredentials` (the export reads no runner git secret: no Secret Manager read and no policy or environment value supplying one appears in the environment `recordGit` recorded for the run); `TestBackingPathGrammar` (**one row per line of design §8's table**, accepted and refused); `TestExportPushRefusesASymlinkEscape`, `TestExportPushRefusesAGitDirectoryUnderPath`, `TestExportPushRefusesACaseVariantCollision` (the grammar's filesystem half); `TestPolicyWithoutBackingStillDecodes` (the key is optional, and `Version` is unmoved); `TestExportWithoutBackingRefusesPush`. **The earlier revision's `TestBackingPathRefusesEscapingAndUnanchoredPaths` is replaced by `TestBackingPathGrammar`**: "escaping and unanchored" named two of §8's seven rules and left the other five to a reader's imagination, which is the shape that produced the contradiction this fix round removes.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... -count=1` (and `./internal/projectpolicy/...` for the validation rows). **Depends on:** 6.1. **Commit:** "project export --push: the folder, the branch, and nothing outside it".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath`, `TestExportPushUsesTheConfiguredLocation`, `TestBackingPathGrammar` and the detector's three tests (`TestExportPushFailsLoudlyOnAConflictingChangeUnderPath`, `TestExportDetectsACommittedEditUnderPath`, `TestExportRefusesWhenTheMarkerIsMissingOnANonEmptyFolder`) first, with the change unimplemented: the symbols this task adds get their signatures and empty bodies in the same commit, so the first run **compiles and fails on behaviour**, not on an undefined name. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 6.3: the docs, the skills and `doctor`

**Files:** `docs/project-layer.md`, `docs/multi-model.md`, `plugin/skills/setup/**`, `internal/cli/doctor.go` (+ tests), `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go` (new: the checklist guard test), `SECURITY.md`.

1. The user-facing story: what a backing location is for (history and audit; survival of the Firebase project), what it is not (a unit of execution, a backup of secrets), and how to read it — written for **both** shapes the owner named, a dedicated repository and **a folder inside a repository the owner already has** (design §8, D14): the `fugaro-project/` folder, its README, that everything beside it is untouched by an export, and **who can read it** — anyone who can read that repository can read the policy, the caps and the spend history, which is why people are omitted by default (P10) and no secret value is ever written. A monorepo reader who finds the folder with no context is the reader this section is for.
2. `doctor`: a `backing` check (info/warning) — configured or not, **which location** it names (`repo@branch:path`, so the folder case is visible and not assumed), whether the last export is stale against the policy's own `checked_at` (a `warning` past 30 days, the same threshold family the price catalog's P7 uses), that the branch it names is not the repository's default branch unless the owner wrote it there (a `warning`, D14's ruling, never a refusal), and that a divergence on `--push` is reported rather than forced.
3. The setup skill: the backing location's three questions (`repo`, `path`, `branch`) with their defaults, the folder's own case as the recommended one, and the export command.
4. SECURITY.md: the export's security posture in one paragraph — metadata only, no values, people's addresses are a deliberate opt-in.
5. **`TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (Review Focus 13, `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, in the `docs_*_test.go` convention Task 1.6 and 2.3 use): a docs test over `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` asserting that **every** `## Check 48`-onward section carries **Check 43's three hard stops and its project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`** (R2-6, round-1 finding 13): that each section (a) asserts `P` equals `fugaro-dev` with the other variables non-empty, (b) calls `fugaro doctor --project fugaro` and greps its `project:` line, (c) greps the checkout's origin against `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`, and (d) uses `--project fugaro` on every `fugaro` command that takes the flag and `--project "${P:?}"` on every `gcloud` command — the last two asserted by scanning the section's own lines for `fugaro`/`gcloud` commands that lack the flag, so a hand edit that drops one fails the test. It also asserts the three names the plan fixes: **no section may mention `edgeappinc/edgeserver`, `edgeappinc/edgeweb` or a `edgeappinc` workspace token** except in a "never" sentence. It lands here, in the last phase, because checks 48 to 56 do not all exist before it — and it is the test that fails if a later hand edit weakens a guard. **It is the backstop, not the red step of the ten tasks that write those checks — one per check, 48 to 56 and 61**: each of Tasks 0.4 (48), 1.7 (49), 1b.4 (50), 2.4 (51), 3.0 (52), 3.7 (53), 4.5 (54), **5.5 (55)**, 6.4 (56) and **6.5 (61)** writes its own `TestChecklistHasCheckNN` for its own section, because this test cannot exist while any of them is being written — and a red step that waits for a test in a later phase fails by not compiling, which is what finding 32 exists to prevent. The per-check tests assert the section, its row and the guard; this one asserts the **property across all of them**, which is the part no single section can.

**Command:** `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` and `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli/... -count=1`. **Commit:** "docs, skills and doctor: the backing repository".

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** Write `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded` first, and make it fail for a reason that is about behaviour and not about a missing name: the sections all exist by now (Tasks 0.4 to 6.4 wrote them, each with its own `TestChecklistHasCheckNN`; **Check 61's is Task 6.5's, written after this test — so the property this test asserts is "every `## Check 48`-onward section that exists", and it covers 61 from the moment 6.5 writes it**, which is why 6.5 carries its own red step rather than waiting for this one), so a first run of the new test can compile and fail on a real gap — assert the property, run it, watch it point at the sections whose guard is weakest, then fix those sections. Run the command above — `Expected: FAIL`. Then implement and re-run it — `Expected: PASS`. A first run that is green means the test does not exercise the change; a first run that does not compile means the sketch was skipped.

### Task 6.4: Check 56 — OWNER-RUN

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`.

Append `## Check 56: the export, and what it does not contain (installation fugaro only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row:

1. Setup and guard — **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1); record the project's caps, policy and the sandbox's state.
2. `fugaro project export --out <dir>` on installation `fugaro`. **FACT:** the file tree with per-file byte counts, and the manifest's `checked_at`.
3. **The scan that matters**: grep the whole export for a value the owner knows is in Secret Manager (the owner chooses one and greps for it **without pasting it**). **FACT:** zero matches, and the counts for the `inventory/` files that name the secret.
4. **The people check**: `grep -r '@' <dir>` (before any `--with-people`). **FACT:** the count, and which files matched (a commit message or a URL is fine; a `byPerson` key is not).
5. Determinism: export twice into two directories and `diff -r` them. **FACT:** empty diff (or the one field that legitimately differs, named).
6. `--push` to the backing location if one is configured: **FACT:** the commit's subject (with its skip-CI marker), the author and committer (the fixed `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>`, never the owner's own identity), the file count, that the divergence case was reported rather than forced (a second push attempt after an out-of-band commit), and that nothing in the pushed tree is a secret value. **Which location it names is part of the FACT** — `repo@branch:path` — because the folder case is a different tree from the dedicated-repository one.

This check is the export's **content**: no secret value, no person's address, a deterministic tree. The folder case's own assertion — that the files *beside* the folder are untouched, that a branch which moved is followed onto its new tip without a **force**, and that an edit made inside the folder is refused by name — is **Check 61** (Task 6.5), deliberately a separate number: content and containment fail for different reasons, and a single check that asserted both would not tell the owner which one broke.
7. Restore: none needed (the export writes outside the installation) — but confirm one ordinary sandbox task still runs and, if `--push` was used, that the branch the policy names (`fugaro-project`, or the owner's own) is where the owner expects, and that no other branch was touched.

**Paste back:** every FACT line, never a secret value. **Depends on:** 6.1 to 6.3.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, and its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck56`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, following the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`, which are the two that exist on `main`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 56` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL` (**`TestChecklistHasCheck56`**: the section is unguarded and the row is missing). Add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. **This task's red state is its own test, not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded`** (the review's correction, and Task 6.3 says the same from the other side): that test is written in the **last** phase, over checks 48 to 56 together, so it does not exist while any of them is being written — a red step that waits for it would not fail, it would not compile, which is the opposite of what finding 32 asks for. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

### Task 6.5: Check 61, the folder export's own fence — OWNER-RUN

**Files:** `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md`.

**Number.** **61** — the next number free past every reservation, read on 2026-10-15: 48 to 56 are this plan's own (phases 0 to 6), 57 the compute backend's (`docs/design/compute-backend.md` §7, PR #375), 58 the artifact-runs plan's (PR #393, merged to `main` as `fd06635`), 59 the desktop console's (PR #401, **merged to `main` as `411fc18`** on 2026-10-15) and 60 the budget-token channel's (PR #399, open) — see §"Check numbers this plan takes". The register's own rule applies to it unchanged: **a number is chosen at the moment of merge**, so if a later claim collides with it, the later merger renumbers itself and never this check. **Only the reservation is written now**: this task writes the row and the section when phase 6 ships, exactly as every other check in this plan does.

The check exists because the folder case has an assertion the dedicated-repository case does not: **the tree beside the folder is another team's product, and an export must leave it exactly as it found it** (design §8; the same requirement Task 6.2's `TestExportPushTouchesNothingOutsidePath` pins as a test). Append `## Check 61: the folder export, and the files beside it (installation fugaro, a scratch repository only, never belong; run by you; NOT RUN)` and its row, with these steps.

**Corrections that make this check runnable — the fix round's two, and fix round 2's four step-level ones.** **(1) The scratch repository is the owner's own, never the sandbox's.** The earlier revision said the scratch repository was "one the owner creates for this check on the sandbox's own host" — and the sandbox's host is Bitbucket, in the **`edgeappinc` workspace**, which holds `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox` *and* `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb` (`belong`). Creating a repository there is writing in somebody else's workspace, which is the thing every rule in this plan forbids in the same breath as touching `belong`. So the push target is one of two things, and **neither is in that workspace**: a **local bare repository** the check creates in a temporary directory (`git init --bare`, pushed to over a `file://` remote — no host, no account, no network, and the default because it costs nothing and writes nowhere: `backing.repo` accepts an absolute `file://` URL, design §8) or a throwaway repository the owner creates under **their own personal account**. **Check 43's guard is unchanged and is not weakened by this**: its third hard stop pins *the checkout the `fugaro` commands run from* to `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`, which is exactly right — the guard is about which installation and which checkout act, and the push target is a different object; step 1 says so in the check itself so no reader has to infer it from the guard. **(2) Every step asserts exactly one outcome.** The branch-moved step used to accept "it commits on the new tip *or* it fails loudly", which asserts nothing: a refusal and a success are not two ways of passing the same check, they are a pass and a failure of different steps. The moved branch with nothing changed inside the folder has **one** correct outcome — the export follows the branch, the out-of-band commit is still an ancestor, no force — and the refusal belongs to the step that produces it on purpose (step 8, the edit inside the folder). A step that can pass either way is replaced rather than annotated. **(3) The four things a *live* run needs and a written step had not: the push target is pinned by a guard line, the branch's own commits are what the identity check reads, the push command is recorded rather than remembered, and the policy step 10 restores is recorded in step 1.** Fix round 2, one per step below: step 1 gains `${R:?}`/`${path:?}`/`${branch:?}` guards, a hard stop that refuses a target in the `edgeappinc` workspace, and the policy recording step 10 needs; step 5's identity assertion is restricted to `<default-branch>..fugaro-project`, because the branch is bootstrapped from the default branch's tip and that tip carries the owner's own step-2 seed commit — the owner appearing there is correct and is not what the step asserts; step 6 uses design §8's marker-excluded tree recipe, which the earlier text had as `git rev-parse <tip>:<path>` — a value that includes the marker, so it could never equal the marker's own; and step 7 records the push command (`GIT_TRACE=1` into a file) and asserts *that*, because "no force" asserted from memory is not an assertion.

1. **The guard, and the push target.** Re-run **Check 43's block verbatim** (Task 0.4 step 1) — the three hard stops (`P` exactly `fugaro-dev` with the other variables non-empty; `fugaro doctor --project fugaro` grepped for its `project:` line; the checkout's origin grepped against `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`), `--project fugaro` on every `fugaro` command and `--project "${P:?}"` on every `gcloud` command — from the **sandbox checkout**, which is where the `fugaro` commands run from. Record the push target's own facts, which are about a **different** repository: `S=$(mktemp -d); git init --bare "$S/scratch.git"`, and `R="file://$S/scratch.git"` as `backing.repo` (or the owner's own personal-account repository, if they prefer a host). **The push target is pinned by its own guard line, because Check 43's three hard stops pin the checkout and not the target**, and a guard that pins one of the two objects a check writes to is a guard with a hole in it:

```sh
R="${R:?set R to the scratch repository: file:///…/scratch.git, or a repository under your own account}"
path="${path:-fugaro-project/}"; branch="${branch:-fugaro-project}"
case "$R" in *edgeappinc*|*/edgeserver*|*/edgeweb*)
  echo "refusing: $R is in the edgeappinc workspace, which this check must never write to"; exit 1;; esac
```

   **And step 1 records the policy it will restore in step 10**, which the earlier revision asked step 10 to restore without ever recording: before publishing, `fugaro project policy show --project fugaro > "$S/policy-before.txt" 2>&1` (or the line `(no policy published)` when there is none), together with whether one existed at all — a **per-job** view of the effective budget mode is **not** enough (and cannot be had from here: `fugaro project policy show` takes no job argument, its only flag is `--json`, `internal/cli/project_cmd.go:924`), since what matters is whether a policy is published *at all*, so the recording is `fugaro project policy show --project fugaro` plus its exit status. **FACT:** the resolved `$R`, the guards above as they were run, the statement that the target is not `edgeappinc/fugarosandbox`, not `belong` and not in the `edgeappinc` workspace, and the recorded policy (or the fact that there was none, which is a different restore).
2. **Seed the unrelated content the export must not touch.** In a working clone of `$R`: a root `README.md`, a small `src/app.go` and a CI file (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), committed to the repository's default branch and pushed. **FACT:** the default branch's name and sha, and the three files' paths with their sha256s.
3. **Configure the location and export once.** `fugaro project policy publish` with `backing: {"repo": "$R", "path": "fugaro-project/", "branch": "fugaro-project"}` for a **sandbox** slug (the operator's own publish, never an agent's), then `fugaro project export --out <dir> --push`. **FACT:** the branch that appeared is `fugaro-project` and **not** the repository's default branch — which is also the assertion that the bootstrap took the default branch's tip rather than creating an orphan, so the next line is `git merge-base --is-ancestor <default-branch> fugaro-project` exiting 0.
4. **The fence — the assertion this check exists for.** In a fresh clone: **FACT:** (a) `git status --porcelain` is empty; (b) `git diff --name-only <default-branch>..fugaro-project -- . ':(exclude)fugaro-project'` prints nothing; (c) `git diff --name-only <default-branch>..fugaro-project` lists only `fugaro-project/…`; (d) every sha256 from step 2 is unchanged; (e) `git show --name-only` on the export's commit names only the folder's files, so the fence holds for the commit and not merely for the branch. A file that was reformatted, re-sorted or re-committed with a different mode fails (d) and (e).
5. **The commit is quiet.** **FACT:** the commit's subject verbatim (the export's `checked_at`, the `[skip ci]` marker) and its author **and** committer — both `Fugaro <fugaro@users.noreply.invalid>`, and **not** the owner's own `git config` identity (`internal/gitops/gitops.go:62-78`). **One assertion, and it is a negative, over the branch's own commits:** `git log --format='%an <%ae> %cn <%ce>' <default-branch>..fugaro-project` contains the owner's name or address zero times. **The range is what makes it pass:** the branch is bootstrapped from the default branch's tip (step 3), and that tip carries the owner's own seed commit from step 2 — so an unrestricted `git log fugaro-project` would print the owner's identity, correctly, and fail a step that is about what *this export* wrote. The range is the export's commits and nothing else, which is the assertion the step is named for.
6. **The detector's marker is there, and the export did not write it under anybody else's name.** **FACT:** `git show fugaro-project:fugaro-project/.fugaro-export.json` — the marker exists, its `checked_at` matches the export's output, and its tree hash equals the folder's own hash computed with the marker excluded, by design §8's recipe and by nothing else: `git ls-tree fugaro-project:fugaro-project | sed -e '/[[:space:]]\.fugaro-export\.json$/d' | git mktree` — the same value the export printed. **`git rev-parse fugaro-project:fugaro-project` is *not* that value and cannot be**: it names the folder's tree as committed, which contains the marker, so a step asserting the two were equal would fail on a correct implementation and pass on none. This is the fact the next two steps depend on, and it is asserted here rather than assumed there.
7. **A branch that moved is followed, and nothing is forced.** A second writer commits to `fugaro-project` out of band — a file **outside** the folder, so nothing conflicts — and the owner runs the export again. **FACT:** the push succeeds, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <the second writer's sha> fugaro-project` exits 0 (the out-of-band commit survived), and the command that was run carried **no force** — no `--force`, no `--force-with-lease`, no `+` refspec. **The command is recorded, not remembered:** run the export with its git trace captured — `GIT_TRACE=1 fugaro project export --out <dir> --push 2> "$S/trace-7.txt"` (the export shells out to `git`, `internal/gitops/gitops.go:215`, so the trace names every invocation) — and paste the `git push` line the trace recorded, verbatim: the FACT is that line, that it carries no `--force`/`--force-with-lease` and no `+` refspec, and that it pushed `<branch>` and not the default branch. `grep -e 'force' "$S/trace-7.txt"` is the assertion; an empty result is the pass. (If the trace is unavailable on the owner's git, the fallback is a recording `git` shim earlier on `PATH` that appends `"$@"` to a file and execs the real git — the same seam Task 6.2's `TestExportPushNeverForces` reads.) **One outcome:** a refusal here is a **failure** of this step, not an acceptable variant, because nothing under the folder changed.
8. **A change inside the folder is refused — by name.** A second writer edits a file **inside** `fugaro-project/` (leaving the marker untouched, which is the point: a person editing the record does not update the marker), and the owner runs the export again. **FACT:** the command **exits non-zero**, its refusal names that path, and afterwards `git rev-parse fugaro-project` is **the sha it was before this step** — the branch did not move and the edit is still there.
9. **The override is explicit, and it takes effect.** The owner re-runs the export with **`--force-regenerate`**. **FACT:** it exits 0, the edited file is replaced by the export's own content (`git diff` between the two tips names that path), the marker's tree hash now matches the new folder (step 6's recipe, run again), and a **second** identical run without the flag succeeds — which is what shows the override left a consistent record behind rather than a marker that refuses forever. **`--adopt` is asserted the same way in one line:** on a second scratch repository whose `fugaro-project/` the export did not write (a hand-made folder with a file of the owner's own in it), the export **refuses** without `--adopt` and succeeds with it, replacing that file.
10. **Restore.** `rm -rf "$S"` and the local export directories; put the policy back the way **step 1 recorded it** — a recording, not a gesture. If `$S/policy-before.txt` holds a policy, publish again from the operator's own config, which is what step 1 read and does not carry `backing` (`fugaro project policy publish --project fugaro`), and compare with `fugaro project policy show --project fugaro`; if it recorded *(no policy published)*, remove the one step 3 published (`fugaro project policy publish --delete --yes`). Then confirm one ordinary sandbox task still runs. **FACT:** the policy read back matches step 1's recording **exactly** — the same `backing` state, which is no `backing` at all in the usual case — and the sandbox's `fugaro-dev` project has no resource this check created (nothing in it was created: the push target is local).

**Paste back:** every FACT line, never a secret value, never a token, never a file's contents.

**Depends on:** 6.1 to 6.4, and on Task 6.3's `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded` for the ordering below.

**Ordering — and this is the second half of the fix round's correction to this task.** `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded` (Task 6.3) is the backstop that asserts **every `## Check 48`-onward section** — which by the time phase 6 ships is checks **48 to 56, 57, 58, 59, 60 and 61** — carries Check 43's three hard stops and its project pinning. A task that writes the last section and never runs it is a task that leaves the property unverified for exactly the section this plan adds. So this task's verification step is **both** tests, in this order: run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` for `TestChecklistHasCheck61`, then run the same command with `-run 'TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded'`, where the sweep also takes in 57 to 60 — **sections this plan does not own.** The rule for a failure there, stated so the implementer does not "fix" someone else's check: **this task repairs only its own section (`## Check 61`); anything the sweep reports in checks 57, 58, 59 or 60 is reported in this PR's description and not edited here**, because those sections belong to the compute-backend, artifact-runs, desktop-console and budget-token plans, each of which is writing its own section in its own PR, and an edit here would be a change to another plan's check made where nobody is looking for it. If the sweep's own text does not yet cover 61 (because Task 6.3 wrote it over 48 to 56), extending that one test's range to include 61 **is** this task's edit — the property is the point, and a backstop that does not include the newest section is not a backstop.

**Red step — `Expected: FAIL` (finding 32, R2-16).** This task's artifact is the checklist section, so its red state is a docs test: write the section and its row **without** the guard block, the FACT lines or the restore step, and write **`TestChecklistHasCheck61`** in the same commit (in `internal/cli/docs_project_centric_test.go`, the `docs_*_test.go` convention and `TestChecklistHasCheck33`/`TestChecklistHasCheck40`): it asserts that `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md` carries the `## Check 61` section, its row in the check list, **and Check 43's guard block inside that section** — the three hard stops and the project pinning, not merely a `${P:?...}`. Run `GOFLAGS=-p=2 go test ./internal/cli -run 'Docs|Lint' -count=1` — `Expected: FAIL`, then add the guard block, the FACT lines and the restore step and it passes. As with every other check task here, its **first** red is its own test and not `TestChecklistChecksAreGuarded` (Task 6.3), which is written over the earlier sections and is the backstop rather than the first failure — but the sweep is **run**, not skipped (see "Ordering"), and a backstop that has never been run over the section it is meant to cover is the shape this task exists to avoid repeating. No line of this task runs live: the check is written **NOT RUN**, and an agent never performs a step of it.

## Cross-task dependency audit

The owner's first requirement is that **each phase, and where practical each task, merges alone and leaves `main` green**, with no task depending on a later one. This section is that check, done explicitly rather than assumed: every task's "Depends on" line from its own section, in one table, with the direction and the answer to "what if the dependency is not merged yet".

**Direction.** Every edge below points **backwards** — to an earlier task in the same phase, to an earlier phase, or to an already-open PR. **There is no forward edge in this plan**, and no task is written as "do this after the next one": that is the property the table exists to demonstrate.

| 0.2 | — | merges alone |
| 0.3 (D6) | — | merges alone; the deploy is 0.4 |
