The sweep it runs covers checks 57 to 60 as well — **sections this plan does not own** — and the task's rule is to repair only its own section and report the rest (the fix round added this, because a backstop nobody runs over the newest section is not a backstop) |

**The cross-phase edges that are not just ordering**, spelled out because they are the ones a reviewer will question:

1. **`#365` before 4.2, `#360` before 3.6, `#359`+`#361` before 1b, `#343` superseded by 4.3.** Four external edges, all open PRs, all backward — the plan builds on them or waits for them, and never the reverse. D9's `#363` is deliberately **after** phase 5, not before it.
2. **Phase 1 without phase 2 or 3**: an installation that publishes a policy gets enforcement, and one that does not is untouched (P3), so phase 1 merges into a working system on its own.
3. **Phase 2 without phase 3**: the default scope changes; a repository keeps its own job and mounts the project's secret on it (2.2), which is #343's supported path. Nothing needs a shared job.
4. **Phase 3 without phase 4**: shared jobs work; a repository's root still exists and is simply unused for clone workflows. Nothing is deleted until 4.2, which is exactly why 4.2 is safe.
5. **Phase 4 retires what phases 0–3 left behind, and nothing earlier depends on phase 4.** 4.2's refusal to retire a root whose resources another repository references is what makes it mergable into an installation that has not finished migrating.
6. **Phases 5 and 6 are independent of each other** (5 needs 1.6 and 3.2; 6 needs 1.6 and 3.5), so either can ship first, and neither is needed by 0–4.

**What each phase leaves on `main` when it lands**, which is the "system still works" half of the requirement: after 0, the rules and the messages are fixed and nothing else changed; after 1, an installation with no policy behaves exactly as before and one with a policy is enforced, with `fugaro run` still working in both; after 1b, routing is data and the unset case falls back to today's behaviour; after 2, `secrets set` stores project-wide and every existing path still mounts it; after 3, clone workflows run on shared jobs and per-repository jobs still exist for anyone not switched over — **enforced by Task 3.3 step 1, which routes a legacy job name down the phase-1 path instead of halting it** (finding 5; without that the phase could not merge on an installation that has published a policy); after 4, the old roots are retired one at a time, by the owner, each after its replacement is proven; after 5, OAuth accounts are people's, with the per-repository copies still readable until the repository path is gone; after 6, a record can be exported, and the installation is unaffected by the export having run.

## The design's §12a items, by owning task

| §12a item | Owning task | What carries it |
|---|---|---|
| 1. profile and account from `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, not an overridable env var | 3.3 | the derivation is explicit (the runner reads it through `backend.ExecutionFromEnv`), and the corrector was in the drift table: the runner does not read `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` directly on this branch |
| 2. no provider from an overridable env var; the job or the policy supplies it | 3.3 (binding), 1.5 (the first half) | the job's provider and the policy's canonical names |
| 3. the interim path between phases 1 and 4 | 1.6 step 1 (`fugaro project repos add`, the one-line data write shipped early for exactly this gap), 4.1 (closes it) | a new repository is unenrolled between them; the interim is one allow-list write, not a Terraform run |
| 4. seed `allow_repos` without one laptop, and no silent re-add | P4, 1.6 (the seed), 1.3 (the reader) | seeded once, from the operator's own `repos:` map and the shared config's repository list — **not** from the bucket's `runs/<slug>/` prefixes and **not** from every checkout the machine knows; those are the reads `project show`/`project repos ls` make, and P4 rejects seeding from them (finding 19, B3); a later publish never re-adds a name the operator removed |
| 5. matching includes the provider, canonical names | 1.1 (`Canonical`, `MatchRepo`), 1.5 | the glob rules are `provider/owner/name` |
| 6. old baked images ignore a published policy | 1.6 (steps 3, 4 and 5) and 1.5 (step 4, the `policySince` launch gate) | `doctor`'s `policy-vs-jobs` and `policy-rules-skew` and `upgrade` say so, 12a item 6's message is in the drift note, and the gate refuses the launch rather than the run in the release-skew case |
| 7. `config/admin` is readable by launchers; no secret-adjacent data | 1.2 (rules), 1.1 (shape) | the section states it and the rules carry `.read: false` for a run token |
| 8. profile admission is a phase 3 item | 3.3 | phase 1 enforces `allow_repos` only (P6) |
| 9. the routing switch is an object, effective on publish | 1b.1 | an edit takes effect when an operator publishes |
| 10. D2's reach on per-repository jobs still needs `init --repo` until phase 3 | 2.2, 2.4 step 2, 3.2 | stated as the phase's remaining Terraform step |
| 11. who creates a person's secret | 5.1 | the person, with the operator accepting the reference |
| 12. run-token writes include `outcomes/` and the global spend node | **1.2 step 6** (the read-path and write-path rows live in the emulator's own tables, `TestAllowedPaths`/`TestDenialTable`, under `-tags firebase` — R2-12 corrected the step number and the earlier "the parity table gains a row") | the policy edit is additive and confined to `config`, and each existing write gains a row so a later edit to the `config` block cannot take it away |
| 13. the RTDB policy replaces only the ceiling; `fugaro.yaml` still only tightens | 1.3 (the merge) | `policy.Merge`'s ceiling becomes the published policy, and the file layers keep their place |
| 14. the cross-cutting list names `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh`, `docs/multi-model.md`, `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/git-providers.md` | 3.2, 4.6, 5.5, 6.3 | each phase's sweeping task, named there |

## The design's §13 UNVERIFIED items, by owning task

| §13 item | Owning task | The probe, and what happens if it fails |
|---|---|---|
| Cloud Run secret reference inside an env override | 3.0 step 3 (Check 52) | if it works, it is a narrower option for the account mount; the design's option D stays rejected for the IAM reason the same step checks |
| whether an execution's overrides are visible to the container | 3.0 step 3 (the probe), then 3.5 (the phase-3 text); SECURITY.md's paragraph itself lands in **1.5 step 5**, from Check 49 step 6 — no forward edge, finding 14 | what the *phase-3* text may add is limited to the answer; the phase-1 paragraph does not wait for it |
| Secret Manager IAM conditions on a name prefix | 3.0 step 4 | if honoured, it is a *possibility* for phase 5's per-person grants; if not, phase 5 uses one secret per person per account, as designed |
| whether a repository module can reference a secret it does not create | **settled by reading, no probe**: `docs/design/project-secrets.md:18` (a `secret_id` IAM grant works identically whichever root created the secret), and Task 2.2's test | confirmed live in Check 51 step 2 (the sandbox job mounts the project secret ID) and Check 53 |
| the project-wide Bitbucket token scope | 3.0 step 2 (Check 52) — **settled by the owner’s ruling of 2026-10-15: "cannot be scoped"**, with the check writing it as a FACT and running no probe | Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path (the design’s §4.5 branch for a failed verification), so phase 3’s shared jobs are GitHub’s, **3.1–3.7 proceed as written**, Task 3.7’s live check runs the GitHub path, and the design’s D5 row and §13 entry — which still carry the pre-ruling text — are corrected by the plan’s D5 amendment (Task 0.5) |
| whether the GitHub App can push to the backing repository | 6.2 (recorded as that phase's own probe when its check runs) | `--push` needs a deploy key or App token as a project secret, and 6.2 says which |
| Claude Code's usage-limit text | 5.3 (the detector), 5.5 step 4 (the live answer) | a miss is the safe direction (no switch) and is what the skill already documents |
| runs bucket IAM policy size and the service-account quota | 3.0 step 5 (Check 52), 3.2 step 6 (the golden test asserts the count is a function of profiles × accounts, not of repositories) | a sandbox with no headroom stops the phase and is reported |
| how the Firestore database is created | **settled by reading, no probe**: `fugaro init --firebase`'s `firestoreStep`/`deployDatabase` (`internal/cli/init_firebase.go:549`, `:465`), with `firestore.googleapis.com` enabled by the Firebase root (`deploy/terraform/gcp/modules/firebase/apis.tf:15`) and the history account's `roles/datastore.user` (`.../modules/firebase/iam.tf:94`) | phase 6's export and the history job both assume it exists; the export reads it, never creates it |
| whether `dimipaun/fugarosandbox` is usable as the GitHub sandbox | 4.5 step 4 (Check 54) and 3.7 step 3 (Check 53) | if not, a GitHub sandbox repository is created and the FACT line says which |
| whether the launcher override gap is exploitable in practice | 3.0 step 3's D10 counterpart, with SECURITY.md written in **1.5 step 5** (phase 1, from Check 49 step 6's live override) and extended in 3.5 | the phase-1 paragraph is stated from phase-1 evidence; the phase-3 probe only adds what an execution's overrides expose, and no phase claims to close the gap (D10 accepted) |

## Security-sensitive tasks, for independent opus review

Each of the owner's named areas maps to a task, and each of those tasks stands alone in its own PR group (the PR groups section) so a review sees exactly one of them:

| Area the owner named | Task | Why it is security-sensitive |
|---|---|---|
| the RTDB rules and the policy node | 1.2, 0.3 (D6) | the rules decide who can read the policy and the kill switches; D6 changes the per-run cap's direction (a fail-open direction) |
| the runner's slug binding, admission and fail-closed checks | 1.3 (fail closed), 1.4 (**the RTDB URL and API key**, which decide whether a published policy can be read or bypassed), 1.5 (the binding's first half), 3.3 (the full binding and profile admission), 3b.1 (**the budget-token channel**, docs only), 1.6 (**the publish gate**) | a wrong slug lets one repository's run act as another's; fail-open would let an unpublished policy be bypassed |
| the shared-job IAM and bucket conditions | 3.2 | one job SA per (profile, provider, account) broadens the bucket conditions — D4's accepted regression, stated in SECURITY.md; **and the same grant reaches other runs' budget tokens, which is D13, ruled 2026-10-15: accepted and documented beside D4, with Task 3b.1 designing the channel and separate profiles as the interim boundary (R1)** |
| project-scope keys | 2.1 | D2 reverses a default: a key reaches every enrolled repository unless `--repo` is passed |
| the CLI that publishes the policy and seeds the allow-list | 1.6 | **`policy publish`'s marker refusal is what makes "a published policy implies the rules are deployed" true** — it is the reason Task 1.3 may halt on a denied read at all — and the allow-list seed decides which repositories a shared job admits |
| the OAuth pool | **5.1 and 5.2** | a person's token, an operator's grant, and the widening the design accepts. **5.2 is in this list because it is the task that writes the grant**: one job and job service account per (profile, shared account) is what decides which job may read which person's secret, so it is the IAM half of 5.1's design and is reviewed on its own (the Global Constraints list said (5.1, 5.2) while this table said 5.1 — one of them had to change, and the flag decides whether a task may merge on a self-review, so the wider list is the safe one) |
| the export | 6.1 | a file tree that must contain no secret value and, by default, no person |
| the launcher's override surface | 3.0 (the probe), SECURITY.md in 1.5/3.5 | the boundary is documented, not closed (D10); the probe decides what may be claimed |

## Self-review

Written last, against the owner's instructions, checking the plan rather than its prose:

1. **Does every phase merge alone?** Yes — the audit above is that check, and it found one edge to soften and one item to add. Phase 1b is deferrable by P7 (it waits on two open PRs); 4.2 waits on the **full** config-transitions plan, which ships in 0.7.5 (R2); 3.6 waits on #360's Check 38. Nothing in the plan waits on a later task. Phase 0 to 6 each leave `main` green and the system working, as stated per phase in the audit's last paragraph. **The round-1 review found one place where that was claimed but not true** — phase 3's runner would have halted every unmigrated per-repository job once a policy existed — and the fix is a rule in the plan (Task 3.3 step 1: the job's own name decides which contract applies), not a promise. **The round-2 review found the phase-1 counterpart of the same class of mistake**: the fail-closed rule was stated for a run that cannot see the marker at all (finding 1 above), so it is now stated on the run that has the credential, with the credential-less case **on a budget-off job** owned by the launcher's readiness gate — and on a budget-on job a refusal at the runner, not a fallback (B1, `budget_session.go:174-175`,`:186-187`).
2. **Was every cited `file:line` re-verified?** Every one that the plan asserts, yes, against this branch (`fd86542`) — **except the two that postdate it** (#370's `cloneSince = "0.7.5"`, the drift table's one postdating row, and Check 47's heading at `:1686`, which the plan cites at lines 19 and 49 and which the drift table does not carry — the earlier "the two the drift table marks as postdating it" was wrong about the second, round-6 correction; both re-checked on `main` at `2b6f55b`) — and the drift table at the top records what moved, what the design names as a bare filename, and the three citations that could not be re-verified from the repository (the generated `run-gen.go`, the `requestedBy` symbol, and the "the runner already reads `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`" premise). Three corrections came out of writing the later phases and are in this commit rather than the drift table: `claudeOAuthAccountRefusal` is `internal/cli/secrets.go:202` (the earlier draft wrote `:196`, which is the function above it — Task 2.1 step 2 carries the same correction), the spend and cap paths are `internal/budget/model.go:51-80` (not `:44`), and `internal/cli/project.go` already exists and holds the checkout/project helpers, so the enrollment commands get their own file (`project_enroll.go`, Task 4.1 — the group's files are `project_cmd.go`, `project_enroll.go`, `project_export.go` and that existing `project.go`, C2). The `build.tf` range is `:5-93`; the other module ranges cited (`secrets.tf:4-42` with `prevent_destroy` at `:22`, `registry.tf:10-72` with `prevent_destroy` at `:59`, `check.tf:16-142`, `history.tf:174-253`) were read, not inferred.
3. **Is every security-sensitive area flagged for opus review?** Yes — the areas the owner named, plus D10's override surface and D6, in **thirteen** tasks, each with its own PR group: **0.3** (D6, the rules' cap direction), **1.2** (the read rules), **1.3** (the fail-closed read), **1.4** (the RTDB URL and API key), **1.5** (the slug binding's first half), **1.6** (the publish gate and the allow-list seed), **2.1** (project-scope keys), **3.2** (the shared-job IAM and bucket conditions), **3.3** (the full binding and admission), **3b.1** (the token channel), **5.1** (person-owned secrets), **5.2** (the job and job service account per shared account, which is where the grant is written), **6.1** (the export). The list is the one in Global Constraints; this item said "seven" while that list named thirteen, which is the same drift the review found between the list and the table, and the count now follows the list rather than the other way round.
4. **Is every owner-run step marked, and none done by an agent?** Rules deploys (0.4, 1.7), Terraform applies (3.7, 4.5, 5.5), `init --repo` (4.5), secret entry (4.5, 5.5), and the live checks (48 to 56 and 61) — each marked **OWNER-RUN**, each with a guard, a hard-coded project, a restore, and a "paste back FACT lines only, never a value" line. **No step touches `belong`**: every check 48 to 56 and 61 says so explicitly, its guard's stops 2 and 3 enforce it, and no step in the plan creates a credential in the `edgeappinc` workspace or probes edgeserver or edgeweb (R2-6). **The fix round caught one step that would have: Check 61's scratch repository was written as "on the sandbox's own host", which is the `edgeappinc` Bitbucket workspace** — the check now names the owner's own push target instead (a local `file://` bare repository by default, or one under the owner's personal account), and says in its own step 1 that the push target is a different object from the sandbox checkout its guard pins.
5. **Are the Check numbers free?** 45 and 46 are claimed by open PR #359, 44 by #354 (merged, Task 7 of the price catalog's plan), **47 by #363, which is merged** (check the heading at `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md:1686` on `main` at `2b6f55b`; the earlier draft's `:1685` was off by one against current `main` and named a line that does not exist on the plan's `fd86542` baseline, where Check 47 is absent), 43 by #353 (merged), and **38 by #360** (open); `docs/gcp-live-checklist.md:242` says Check 45 is the next free number on `main`. **#362 adds no number** — the earlier draft attributed 43 to it, which was wrong (finding 25): 43 is #353's. This plan takes **48 to 56**, and says so at the top and in each phase's check task. **The 2026-10-15 revision adds 61**, the folder export's fence (Task 6.5), and it is the first number free past every reservation — 57 is #375's, **58 is #393's and is merged** (`fd06635`), **59 is #401's and is now merged too** (`411fc18`, 2026-10-15 — the fix round corrected this from "open") and 60 is #399's and open — which is why the revision re-read the register and the three PRs rather than the gaps: 58 is not in the register on `main` even though #393 has merged, and a number claimed in an open PR is exactly what "reserved" means here.
6. **Is every §12a item and §13 item carried?** Yes — the two maps above. Writing them found one gap, 12a item 12 (the run token's non-read writes), which was added to Task 1.2 as step 6, and one item that was a probe in the design and is settled by reading here (how the Firestore database is created), now cited to `init_firebase.go:549`.
7. **Is every cross-cutting item carried?** The §11 list — SECURITY.md, the user docs, the plugin skills, `fugaro doctor`, `upgrade`'s drift note, the dashboard, launcher grants, version skew, `validate` offline, `scripts/gen-golden-plans.sh`, `docs/multi-model.md`, `docs/checkout.md`, `docs/git-providers.md` — is carried by each phase's sweeping task (1.6, 1b.3, 2.3, 3.2/3.5/3.6, 4.6, 5.5, 6.3), and the phases that change nothing in one of them say so instead of dropping it silently.
8. **What this plan does not do.** It does not implement anything (docs only), it does not decide D5, D3's deferred API alternative, D7's scheduled export or D9's rebase — each is marked where it would land and by whom. It does not claim the launcher override surface is closed, and it does not claim the export makes a project reconstructable end to end.
9. **The weakest part of the plan**, said plainly so a reviewer can attack it: Task 4.1's dependency on 3.3 (the seen-repositories list is only meaningful once project jobs exist) and Task 3.0's open U2 and U3 probes, which decide how much of the launcher-override and secret-reference surface phase 3 may claim — it is no longer a go/no-go on Bitbucket: **that half is ruled**, Bitbucket stays per-repository and the phase's shared jobs serve GitHub. Both are the honest place for those questions to sit, but both are places where a phase's shape is decided after the plan is written, and the plan says what happens then rather than leaving it open.
10. **The review round, and what it caught — kept here because it is evidence about the plan's own method, not a footnote.** Seven findings, all in this document and all corrected in one commit: `Budget`'s fields had no `json` tags, so the documented snake_case keys would never have decoded and — with unknown keys ignored — a camelCase policy would have become a silent zero ceiling (Task 1.1 now carries the tags, refuses a `budget` whose documented keys all miss, and has two tests for it); P5 said the allow-list matched "the same canonical form `task.Slug` builds", when `task.Slug` is the hashed storage identity (`acme-app-3f9c…`) and the compared value is `projectpolicy.Canonical` (P5 now distinguishes the two and says why they agree by construction, with `TestCanonicalAgreesWithTaskSlug`); Task 3.3 said "the GitHub App ID and key become project policy", which would have put `github-app-key` in a node every run token can read (the key now stays a Secret Manager secret granted to the project job SAs, and only its name reaches the policy — the rule 12a item 7 and Task 1.6's `TestPolicyCarriesNoSecretValue` exist for); Task 0.3 mislabelled `rules.json.tmpl:36` as `released` (it is `spent`; `released` is `:32`); P2's first sentence called `config/admin` the third child of `config/policy` and then corrected itself; the §12a map named the interim enrollment path as `--project-wide` when Task 1.6 ships `project repos add`; and the header said phase 0 was four fixes while listing three. Two of the seven are the same failure mode — a claim that was right in one place and sloppy in another (P2, the phase count) — and one, the missing tags, is the reason Task 1.1 now states its decoding rules as a contract rather than as a comment.


## Accepted as deferred

**A list, not a claim.** This section exists because the paragraph that used to close the Review notes — "nothing else is deferred: every other round-1 finding … and every deferrable of round 2 … has a task edit in this revision" — was a blanket claim, and the review found it false twice: once in round 2 (R2-1) and once in the verification round that produced the list below. Each entry here is something a review asked for that this plan does **not** adopt as written, with the reason, so a reader can tell a decision from an omission.

- **D5, and the Bitbucket credential's scope — no longer deferred: ruled by the owner on 2026-10-15.** The ruling is **"cannot be scoped"**: a Bitbucket credential cannot be scoped to one project's repositories, so **Bitbucket stays on the per-repository path** (the branch the design's §4.5 names for a failed verification), phase 3's shared jobs serve GitHub repositories, and **Tasks 3.1 to 3.7 proceed as written** — the phase's machinery is provider-general, and the one step the ruling lands in is **Task 3.7's live check** (its Bitbucket half has no object, so Check 53 runs the GitHub path). The design's D5 row and its §13 entry, which still carry the pre-ruling text, are corrected by the plan's **D5 amendment** (the ninth in the list, applied by Task 0.5); the design's §4.5 runtime-fetch fallback stays a decision for later, unadopted — it reintroduces §4.1's shared-service-account exposure, and Check 52's U3 says only whether it is even available. Task 3.0 step 2 records the ruling in the checklist as a FACT, with no commands and no credential.
- **D3's API-created-job alternative.** Deferred, not built: if an owner genuinely needs a baked workflow after phase 4, that is a new design (design §4.4's second option), and Task 4.4 says so rather than leaving it implied.
- **D7, the scheduled export.** Deferred by D7's own "manual first, scheduled later": Task 6.2 records where the scheduled shape will live (the history job, whose service account already has RTDB admin) and marks the push credential as a probe for that phase's own check.
- **D9, the #363 rebase.** Deferred to Task 5.4, deliberately after phase 5's first three tasks, because #363 multiplies per-repository variant jobs and phase 3 removes them. This plan does not merge #363's code and does not amend its plan silently: the amendment is that PR's own.
- **The migration of installation `belong`.** Not this plan's, and not any agent's: `belong` holds `edgeappinc/edgeserver` and `edgeappinc/edgeweb`, every live step of this plan runs on installation `fugaro` and the sandbox, and moving `belong` is the owner's own work after the model is proven (Tasks 4.4 and 4.5, Global Constraints).
- **Phase 1b as a whole.** Deferrable indefinitely by P7: it is gated on #359's phase 2 and the price catalog (#361), and nothing in phases 2 to 6 reads the routing switch.
- **The verification review's items W2, W5–W9, M2–M5 and T1–T3.** Deferred because they could not be read: the review's own file was not present in the run that made this revision, so only the item *identifiers* reached it, and no edit can be made against an identifier. **M1 is the exception and is addressed** — its subject, a task that writes this plan's design amendments (D13's decisions-table row included) into `docs/design/project-centric.md`, is **Task 0.5** (phase 0, docs only), and its amendment and test now say explicitly that §12's **decisions table** is edited as a table, not only the prose around it. The remaining identifiers are named here so a later round with the review text in hand closes them rather than rediscovering them.
